# Decisions and open questions

Status legend:

- **RESOLVED**: binding.
- **ASSUMED**: a default that stays in effect until the client answers. Build it behind configuration so it can change without a rewrite.
- **OPEN**: needs an answer before the listed milestone. Until then, use the stated default and keep it configurable.

Changing a RESOLVED row needs the human's approval. When you add a row, put the milestone that created it in the Notes column.

| D-051 | 2FA is an enrolment wall, not a session refusal                                        | RESOLVED | A privileged role with no second factor **gets** a session, but `mustEnrolTwoFactor()` refuses every route outside `/api/auth` until the factor is verified. §6 enforcement moved from session issue to the request path.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | M02a                          | M02a. The obvious reading of §6 — refuse the session — deadlocks the product: Better Auth's `/two-factor/enable` runs behind `sessionMiddleware`, so an org_admin created by another org_admin could never enrol and so could never sign in. The wall reads live, so granting a privileged role takes effect at once, and it fails closed on an unknown user id. Architecture §6 updated.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| D-052 | Login branding reads the caller's hostname                                             | RESOLVED | `GET /v1/auth/login-branding` takes an optional `host` query parameter and prefers it over its own `Host` header.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | M02a                          | M02a. The console is served from `<org>.<domain>` while the API answers on `api.<domain>`, so reading our own Host header resolved every branded login to an org called "api". No wider a door than the `slug` parameter beside it: both return only public brand facts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| D-053 | Console and API share a registrable domain                                             | RESOLVED | Every browser app (console, portal, POS) must be served from the same registrable domain as the API — `.erp.example.com` in production, plain `localhost` in development.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | M02a, M14b                    | M02a. The CSRF double-submit token is a cookie the client reads with script and echoes as a header. Cookies are scoped by host and ignore ports, so a console on a different registrable domain simply cannot read it. Found by the e2e suite, which was pointed at `127.0.0.1` while the API was on `localhost`. A deployment constraint, not a preference. Chrome also rejects `Domain=localhost` outright — **verified in the browser** — so branded login cannot be exercised locally on `*.localhost`; development uses `lvh.me` or a hosts entry such as `erp.local`, noted in apps/server/.env.example.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| D-054 | Trusted origins are enumerated, never patterned                                        | RESOLVED | One list feeds CORS, the CSRF origin check and Better Auth: the configured `TRUSTED_ORIGINS` plus one origin per active brand hostname, resolved at boot from `org.organizations`. `credentials: true`, CSRF header in `allowedHeaders`. **No wildcard.**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | M02a                          | M02a. Cross-origin plus cookies has two hard browser rules: the origin must be echoed exactly (`*` is rejected once credentials are involved) and credentials must be true, or the session cookie is never sent and every request looks anonymous. **A `*.<APP_DOMAIN>` wildcard was tried and was wrong:** the parent-domain CSRF cookie already lets a sibling host _read_ the token, and CORS is the control that stops it _using_ the token — an audit reproduced full authenticated API access from any subdomain. The hosts under this domain are not all ours (D-026 landing sites on Vercel, §16 Chatwoot), so one dangling CNAME would have converted a subdomain takeover into an ERP takeover. Adding a brand now needs a restart; that is the right trade.                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| D-055 | i18n and contracts seeded in M02a                                                      | ASSUMED  | `@elysium/i18n` ships a flat en+bn catalogue with `bn` typed as `Record<MessageKey, string>`; `@elysium/contracts` ships the identity Zod schemas the console forms and the server edge both use.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | M02a, M04                     | M02a. Both were M04 skeletons, but M02a ships a screen and a form, and the rules say every string goes through i18n and every form validates against the shared schema. Typing bn against en makes a missing translation a compile error rather than a silent fallback. M04 adds the extraction tooling on top of this shape.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| D-056 | Rate limits are per identifier and per IP                                              | RESOLVED | Both buckets are counted on the credential endpoints and either can reject. Fixed window, in memory, per process; M04 moves the store to Valkey and the limits become global.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | M02a, M04                     | M02a. Better Auth's limiter keys on `${ip}                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | ${path}`, which is per IP only — credential stuffing from a botnet gets the full allowance per address, and a shared carrier NAT (most of Bangladesh's mobile traffic) means one attacker locks out a whole network. The §25 claim was written down before the control existed; an audit found it. In-memory means the threshold multiplies by the replica count, which is why the store is behind an interface. |
| D-057 | No self-service registration; users are invited                                        | RESOLVED | `emailAndPassword.disableSignUp: true`. An org_admin creates a user through `POST /v1/users`, which returns a one-time invite token the recipient redeems at `/api/auth/reset-password` to set their own password.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | M02a, M16                     | M02a. `/api/auth/sign-up/email` was open to the internet — **verified, an anonymous POST returned 200** and wrote to `iam.users` and `iam.accounts`. Worse, it enabled account pre-hijacking: register the address of someone about to be hired, and the later invite attached their role to the attacker's password. Reproduced end to end. The invite uses Better Auth's own reset-password token rather than a second implementation. Until M16 wires email (D-012), the token is handed back to the admin who requested it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| D-058 | The user admin API is org_admin only                                                   | RESOLVED | `POST /v1/users` requires `org_admin` or super_admin, enforced in the service. An existing identity is reused only when it already holds a membership somewhere.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | M02a, M02b                    | M02a. The route shipped guarded by nothing but membership, with a comment deferring the check to M02b — but the route shipped, so the check had to. **Reproduced:** a `sales` user posted their own address with `role: "org_admin"`, the existing-identity branch reused their row, and they held full org admin in one request, then satisfied the 2FA wall by enrolling on their own account. `org_admin` is the `read:cost` and approvals role that posts journals from M03.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| D-059 | The request path never uses the owner role                                             | RESOLVED | `withGlobal()` reaches the global `iam` tables as `erp_app`. `withSystem()` (`erp_owner`) is for migrations, seeds and the guard only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | M02a                          | M02a. Session resolution, the 2FA check, the active-org write, the user lookup and even the unauthenticated login-branding endpoint all ran as `erp_owner` — the role that owns every table and can execute DDL — on every request, when `erp_app` already held exactly the grants each needed. It also made `DATABASE_OWNER_URL` a runtime requirement of the API process rather than a migration-time one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| D-060 | `erp_analytics` reads nothing in `iam`                                                 | RESOLVED | `globalTableSql` defaults `analyticsReads` to false, `ANALYTICS_READABLE` lists what analytics may read, and the tenancy guard enumerates analytics SELECT grants the way it already enumerates writes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | M02a, M24                     | M02a. The generator granted analytics SELECT on every global table unconditionally, so `erp_analytics` could read `iam.accounts` (password hashes), `iam.two_factors` (TOTP secrets and backup codes) and `iam.sessions` (live tokens). **Verified live before and after:** all five readable, then none. Migration 0006 revokes; 0004 is committed and was not edited (invariant 9). A read-only role is still a role that can read.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| D-061 | Row scope is mandatory, not advisory                                                   | RESOLVED | The guard computes `scopeFilterFor(context, action, subject)` for the route's own declared ability and hangs it on the request. Handlers apply it with `scopeWhere(request, columns)` (lists) or `assertInScope(request, row)` (one row). `ScopeInterceptor` refuses to send a scoped caller's response (500) if the handler applied neither. Only an org-wide caller has nothing to apply; a `create` is **not** exempt (re-audit W6) — the handler asserts the row it is about to insert. When the backstop fires after a mutation committed, the audit net still records it (`ScopeNotAppliedError`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | M02b, M03+                    | M02b security review M2. The guard's `ability.can(action, 'Subject')` answers "on _some_ row", so every `own`/`branch` rule was org-wide on the request path — a branch manager read every branch's approvals, with amounts. One typed `ScopeCondition` feeds both CASL (per row) and the SQL (lists), property-tested to agree on every row. Scopes union across roles (sales + branch_manager = own rows **or** branch rows). The backstop catches reads; for a mutation it fires after the handler, so mutations must call `assertInScope` before writing, and a miss is audited rather than lost. Scope is computed per role, never from branches merged across memberships (D-075). Pattern documented in `apps/server/src/platform/auth/scope.ts` for M03+ to copy. **Final pass:** the backstop is detection, not prevention — for a mutation it fires after the write, so the audit net records the escape even when the service already audited the change. |
| D-062 | Checker authority is per approval type, and branch-tier is under threshold only        | RESOLVED | `CHECKER_TYPES` refines §6's single "Approvals (as checker)" row: accounts = payments, hr = payroll, branch_manager = branch items (stock write-off/gain, closing variance), org_admin = everything but `period_reopen`, which only super_admin decides. A checker whose only qualifying role is branch_manager (`checkerTier = 'branch'`) may decide only a request with a `branch_id` in their scope and an amount strictly under the org's configured threshold for its type; no threshold or no amount means refuse.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | M02b                          | M02b. §6 grants `approve ApprovalRequest` as one capability, which let a showroom branch manager approve a payroll run or a reseller payout batch (reproduced, 201), and then any amount for any branch (security review M1). `approval_requests.branch_id` (migration 0010, composite FK per D-047, frozen by the 0009 trigger once decided) is what makes "branch items" checkable. Matrix change, so this row is required by §6.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| D-063 | The approvals list narrows by relevance as well as scope                               | RESOLVED | `GET /v1/approvals` returns rows inside the caller's `read` scope (D-061) **and** either raised by the caller or of a type the caller may decide.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | M02b, M05                     | M02b. The matrix gives accounts, hr and org_admin `read ApprovalRequest` at org scope, which on its own would show hr every supplier payment with amounts. Narrower than the matrix implies, which is the safe direction; the M05 inbox reads exactly this.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| D-064 | Where audit rows are written, and which are atomic                                     | RESOLVED | **In the mutation's transaction** (`AuditService.record(tx, …)`): approval request, decision and threshold changes. **Straight after, in its own transaction**: the org switch (the session row is global, the log is tenant-scoped) and Better Auth events (D-066). **Best-effort net** (`AuditInterceptor`, after the response): every other mutation — today `POST /v1/users` and the demo resource. The interceptor strips COST_FIELDS and redacts before writing, runs outside the serializer so it records what actually left, and logs an error when a mutation completes with no org and no entry.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | M02b, M03+                    | M02b. An entry written outside the mutation's transaction can commit while the mutation rolls back, or be lost when the mutation commits; only the in-transaction path cannot disagree with reality. So every module from M03 writes its own entry with `record(tx, …)` and the net stays a net. The route-matrix suite asserts every successful mutation route leaves a row for its actor.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| D-065 | Approval thresholds are an org_admin setting                                           | RESOLVED | New subject `ApprovalThreshold`; only org_admin holds `read`/`update`. `PUT /v1/approvals/thresholds` sets one, audited with the old value beside the new.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | M02b                          | M02b. A threshold decides which requests skip the second person, so a checker able to raise it could exempt their own team. §6 "users, roles, settings: org_admin". Matrix change, so this row is required by §6.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| D-066 | Credential events are audited into every org the user belongs to                       | ASSUMED  | The Fastify bridge to Better Auth records sign-in (and first factor passed, when 2FA is owed), second-factor verify, backup-code use, 2FA enable/disable, backup-code regeneration, password set (invite/reset), password change, profile update and sign-out. One row per active membership org; never the request body. A user with no membership gets no tenant row (pino is the record). Best-effort: the auth answer is not changed by an audit failure.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | M02b, M04                     | M02b. `/api/auth/*` never reaches Nest, so neither the interceptor nor a service saw it, and these are the events an investigation asks about first. `audit.audit_log.org_id` is NOT NULL and a sign-in belongs to no org, so the alternative was a global security log; that is a bigger design (retention, who may read it) and belongs with M04's platform services if the client wants it. Failed sign-ins are not recorded here; they are rate-limit and security events (D-056).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| D-067 | Approval events are held in memory until M04                                           | ASSUMED  | `approval.requested` and `approval.decided` are collected per call and appended to `ApprovalsService.emitted` only after the transaction commits, so a rolled-back request announces nothing; the list keeps the last 500. Nothing delivers them yet.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | M02b, M04                     | M02b. The outbox and Socket.IO arrive in M04; emitting to nothing would be a lie in the code, and not emitting would leave §19's contract untested. M04 replaces the body of `emit`, not the call sites. Payloads carry ids, type, amount and status only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| D-068 | Below the threshold is auto-approved                                                   | ASSUMED  | A request whose amount is under the org's threshold for its type is written `auto_approved` (no checker, `decided_at` set, frozen like any decision), audited, and emits both `approval.requested` and `approval.decided`. At or above, with no threshold configured, for a control type (D-071), or for a server-derived amount typed by the requester (D-072), it is `pending`. Magnitude is compared, so a negative amount is judged by its size. Only a threshold a second org admin agreed to can relax this (D-073).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | M02b; client                  | M02b. §6 routes "supplier payments **above** the threshold" through approvals and `requiresApproval` already said "no" below it, yet the row was stored `pending` — so below and above differed only by a flag. Stored as a status rather than skipped so the trail exists. **Client question:** §6 also gives branch_manager "branch items under threshold", which reads as a _lower_ checker tier rather than no checker. **Resolved in the safer direction for now (D-076):** branch items (stock write-off/gain, closing variance) are never auto-approved; under the threshold, with a system-derived amount, a branch manager decides them. The client question is narrowed to: may small branch items skip the branch manager entirely? Until they say so, they may not.                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| D-069 | Auth responses carry no session token; cross-site fetches are refused                  | RESOLVED | The Better Auth bridge strips top-level `token` and `session.token` from JSON responses. CSRF refuses any mutation with `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site`, before the Origin and double-submit checks.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | M02b                          | M02a follow-ups. The session lives in an httpOnly cookie so script cannot read it; `get-session` and sign-in echoed it into JSON for any script that could call them. None of our clients read it. `Sec-Fetch-Site` cannot be set by page script, and D-053 keeps every app on the API's registrable domain, so ours are never cross-site — which covers the browser request that arrives with no Origin at all.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| D-070 | What the route gate enforces                                                           | RESOLVED | `check-route-matrix` fails a route that declares nothing, one whose declarations contradict (both `@Public` and an ability, on the handler or inherited), any non-public route missing from `route-matrix.manifest.ts`, and any manifest entry with no live route. `route-matrix.e2e.test.ts` holds one fixture per manifest entry and drives each route as every role, asserting the guard admits exactly the roles the matrix grants and that every successful mutation leaves an audit row; it also asserts manifest = fixtures = live routes. Guard and gate resolve declarations through one function.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | M02b, every milestone         | M02b. The Build item says the gate fails on a route lacking "a declared ability **or** a matrix test". The generated suite is what makes an entry mean something.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| D-071 | Control types are never auto-approved                                                  | RESOLVED | `period_reopen`, `bonus_rule`, `threshold_change` (which carry no amount) and `back_dated_entry` always need a second person. `thresholdSchema` refuses a threshold for them, `CHECK approval_thresholds_never_for_controls` refuses it at the database, and `CHECK approval_requests_never_auto_approved` refuses an auto-approved row of those types (migration 0011; NOT VALID so a database already holding such a row can migrate, validated by an operator). The no-amount types must send `amountPoisha: null` and every other type must send an amount.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | M02b                          | Re-audit C1, reproduced: an org_admin set a threshold of 1 on `period_reopen`, and the next request with amount 0 was auto-approved and frozen — §7's "reopening needs super_admin approval" switched off by a setting. 0011 also makes every term of a request (type, amount, captured threshold, payload, requester, branch, org) immutable from the moment it is raised, and refuses `pending → auto_approved` by UPDATE (re-audit W3: a pending row could be re-attributed to someone else and then approved by its real maker).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D-072 | The maker side has per-type authority; typed amounts do not decide                     | RESOLVED | `REQUESTER_TYPES` says which types each role may raise, from §6: accounts payments, settlements, wallets, payroll posting, closing variances, back-dated entries and period reopen; hr payroll and bonuses; branch_manager stock adjustments, closing variances and branch wallets; warehouse stock adjustments; org_admin all; sales, marketing, reseller and investor none. `sales` loses `request ApprovalRequest` in the matrix to match. Types whose amount the owning module derives (`SERVER_DERIVED_AMOUNT_TYPES`: stock write-off/gain, settlement posting, closing variance, payroll run, reseller payout batch) are always held when raised through the generic `POST /v1/approvals`; only the owning module, passing `amountDerivedByServer`, lets the threshold apply to them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | M02b, M07, M12, M19, M21, M23 | Re-audit W5. With only `request ApprovalRequest`, a sales agent could raise a payroll run, and any requester chose the amount that decided whether a second person looked. Matrix change, so this row is required by §6. **Final pass (C-1):** a type right and its branch scope must come from the same role. The branches a caller may raise a type for are the union, over only the roles whose REQUESTER_TYPES include it, of each role's own `request` scope; a single such branch is inferred when none is named, and an org-level (no branch) request needs an org-wide requester for that type.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| D-073 | Relaxing a threshold is itself maker-checker                                           | RESOLVED | `PUT /v1/approvals/thresholds` applies a **lower** threshold at once (it only asks for more checking). Every request records the threshold in force when it was raised (`approval_requests.threshold_poisha`), and the branch-tier checker requires the amount to be under both the captured and the current threshold.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | M02b                          | Re-audit W4: an org_admin could raise a threshold, auto-approve their own request, and lower it again, bypassing the second person on their own. Chosen over "applies only to requests created after approval" because the maker-checker module already exists for exactly this and it keeps one threshold per type. **Final pass (W1):** approving a threshold change locks the current threshold and refuses with 409 if it no longer equals the value the change was proposed against, so a later lowering is never silently reverted. Lowering locks the row too. The list and `GET /v1/approvals/:id` show a threshold change's terms (type, from, to).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

Scope belongs to the membership that granted it. **Final pass:** `branchIdsByRole` is required on every context, and a role missing from it reaches no branch at all (fail closed); `branchIds` no longer scopes anything.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

| D-079 | `Poisha` is a branded `bigint`                                                         | RESOLVED | `packages/domain/src/money` types amounts as `bigint & brand`, constructed only through `poisha()`, which refuses fractions, `NaN`, unsafe numbers and anything beyond ±9e15. Arithmetic, `percentOf` (basis points) and `allocate` (largest remainder) round half away from zero through one primitive, `divideRounded`. Approval thresholds stay plain `bigint`: they are compared, never computed with.
