A `number` is exact only to 2^53 and fails silently beyond it; a trial-balance sum across five brands is where that would bite. The 9e15 bound is kept as a sanity limit rather than a precision one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| D-080 | How the database keeps the ledger honest                                               | RESOLVED | Migration 0014: deferred constraint triggers on journals and lines (at least two lines, debits equal credits, checked at commit); `erp_app` holds INSERT + SELECT only on journals and lines, and a trigger refuses UPDATE and DELETE for every role including the owner; `journals.created_txid` is stamped with `pg_current_xact_id()` by trigger and a line may only be inserted by that same transaction; lines must match their account's party type, branch and active flag; accounts can be renamed or deactivated but never change what they are; `erp_app` cannot DELETE accounts or periods. Posting requires READ COMMITTED. TRUNCATE is withheld by grant (the guard asserts it) rather than by trigger, so test resets keep working.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | M03                           | M03. The balance check alone would accept a _balanced pair_ of extra lines added to a journal posted months ago, which is a change to a posted journal; `created_txid` is what closes that. Under REPEATABLE READ the period-lock read would use a snapshot taken before a concurrent close committed, so the trigger refuses anything but READ COMMITTED rather than protecting nothing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| D-081 | Periods: rows appear on close, and reopening is an approval executor                   | RESOLVED | A month with no `ledger.periods` row is open. Closing inserts or updates it to `closed`, only once the month has ended in Asia/Dhaka. The period-lock trigger takes a shared advisory lock per (org, month) on every journal insert; closing and reopening take it exclusively, so a close waits for in-flight posts and blocks new ones. Reopening is a `period_reopen` approval request; `ApprovalsService.registerExecutor` lets the ledger reopen the period **in the transaction that approves it**. The database independently refuses closed to open unless `reopen_approval_id` names an approved `period_reopen` for that period, raised and decided after the close, by a platform super admin who is the acting user (D-094). `period_reopen` can only be raised by the ledger (`POST /v1/ledger/periods/reopen`), which writes its payload; the executor refuses a payload whose month label is not the period's own. An approved type that needs an executor and has none is refused rather than recorded as done. | M03                           | M03. §7: "reopening needs super_admin approval". `CHECKER_TYPES` already gives `period_reopen` to nobody but super_admin and D-071 already refuses to auto-approve it; what was missing was anything that acted on the approval, and anything stopping a plain UPDATE from reopening a month. The "after the close" rule stops last month's approval reopening this month's close.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| D-082 | A replayed key with different content is a conflict                                    | RESOLVED | `journals.content_hash` is sha256 of `canonicalDraft(draft)`. Posting a key that exists returns the existing journal when the hashes match and `409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` when they differ. Cash in checks its key before building the draft and compares wallet, category and amount, so a retry after Dhaka midnight (a new business date) still replays.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | M03                           | M03. §7 says a replay "returns the existing journal and writes nothing". Returning the first journal for a _different_ amount would tell the caller their ৳900 was posted when ৳500 was.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| D-083 | Ledger permissions: `LedgerPeriod`, and cash in is `update Wallet`                     | RESOLVED | New subject `LedgerPeriod`: `read` and `update` for org_admin and accounts, org scope (close, list, ask to reopen). Cash in is `update Wallet` (§6 "Wallets, cash entries, petty cash": org_admin and accounts org-wide, branch_manager own branch wallets). Reports are `read LedgerReport` (org_admin, accounts; branch_manager branch) and wallets and the cash book are `read Wallet`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | M03                           | M03. Matrix change, so this row is required by §6. §7 says "org_admin or accounts closes them", which no existing subject expressed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D-084 | The chart-of-accounts template                                                         | ASSUMED  | `packages/db/src/seed/coa-template.ts`: 40 system accounts with stable codes (1xxx assets to 5xxx expenses) plus `1010-<branch code>` hand cash per branch. §7's operating categories (rent, utilities, packaging, transport, misc) are five accounts of one new subtype, `operating_expense`. `wallet_mfs_other` is a subtype but not seeded; an org adds one when it has Nagad. A server test holds the template's class, normal balance and party in step with the domain's subtype table.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | M03, M14a; client             | M03. D-027 (the client's accountant reviews the chart) stays OPEN; this is the default it reviews.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| D-085 | Cost-bearing account balances are withheld from roles without `read:cost`              | RESOLVED | Ledger reports drop every line on a cost-bearing subtype (inventory, goods in transit, supplier advance, supplier payable, COGS, inventory write-off, purchase price variance, inventory adjustment gain) for a caller without `read:cost`, and say `costWithheld: true`; supplier party balances likewise. The account ledger of such an account is 404 for them; the cash book omits cost accounts from each entry's counter-accounts; the day book omits journals whose visible lines are all cost. The wallet-side amount of a supplier payment stays visible to whoever may see the wallet: it is cash, and the branch manager counts it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | M03                           | M03. `serializeFor` strips fields by _name_, and a trial-balance row for account 5100 carries its cost in `debitPoisha`. Only the service knows the subtype, so the service filters. Today only branch_manager reads ledger reports without `read:cost` (§6 "branch P&L without cost detail").                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| D-086 | Cash in: other-income categories, dated by the server                                  | ASSUMED  | The cash-in rule credits only `other_income` accounts (an org adds more as categories), both lines carry the wallet's branch, and the business date is the Asia/Dhaka date of the server's clock. The API takes no date.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | M03, M14a                     | M03. Crediting `sales_revenue` would book a sale with no order, stock movement or COGS behind it; `inventory_adjustment_gain` needs approval. Taking no date keeps cash in clear of back-dating (§7 "anything earlier than yesterday needs approval") until the `back_dated_entry` flow exists.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| D-087 | Error codes before problem+json                                                        | ASSUMED  | `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts` holds the stable codes; the ledger's HTTP errors carry `{ code, message }`, and database refusals are raised with a `CODE:` message prefix the service maps. M04 wraps the same codes in RFC 9457 problem+json.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | M03, M04                      | M03. CLAUDE.md says errors use codes from `errors.ts`, which did not exist; §23 names `PERIOD_CLOSED`. The domain's `LedgerErrorCode` union is type-checked against the contracts list where it is converted.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| D-088 | No route posts or reverses an arbitrary journal                                        | RESOLVED | Journals come only from posting rules through `LedgerApi.post`; `reverse()` is a service call for owning modules. A user-facing manual journal or reversal needs maker-checker and a screen, and belongs with the accounting UI.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | M03, M14a                     | M03. An endpoint that accepts arbitrary lines is a way round every posting rule, and "reverse any journal" is a way to delete cash without a second person.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| D-089 | The tenancy guard checks every policy                                                  | RESOLVED | Every policy on a tenant table must be the generated org policy, the generated analytics policy on an opted-in table, or declared in `DECLARED_POLICIES` (`packages/db/src/rls.ts`) with its exact command, roles and expressions. `NO_DELETE_TABLES` enumerates tables `erp_app` may update but not delete from.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | M03                           | M02a follow-up. The guard found the org policy by name and counted the rest; `iam.memberships` has two, and a `using (true)` beside a correct org policy would have passed, because permissive policies OR together.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D-090 | `elysium/no-float-money`                                                               | RESOLVED | ESLint rule in `packages/config`: `toFixed()` and any reference to `parseFloat` are errors everywhere except `packages/domain/src/money` and tooling scripts. Non-money uses (contrast ratios) disable it with a stated reason.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | M03                           | M00 follow-up.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| D-091 | Journals are not partitioned                                                           | ASSUMED  | `ledger.journals` and `journal_lines` are plain tables.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | M03, M29                      | M03. §7 does not call for partitioning, five brands' volumes do not need it yet, and composite `(org_id, id)` foreign keys into a partitioned table would need the partition key in every reference. Revisit with the §29 performance work. The guard already examines partitioned parents.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| D-092 | A single cash-in is capped                                                             | ASSUMED  | `LEDGER_CASH_IN_MAX_POISHA` (server env, default 1,000,000,000 poisha = ৳1 crore) bounds one cash-in; above it the API answers `POSTING_INPUT_INVALID`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | M03, client                   | M03 security review M2. Cash in has no maker-checker, and two entries of 9e15 poisha each used to make every report in the org throw. Reports now use unbounded `bigint` totals (D-079) so they can never be broken by stored data; the cap bounds what one person can post alone. Client question: the right ceiling, and whether large cash-ins should need approval.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| D-093 | Closing a month locks every date before it                                             | RESOLVED | A journal is refused when any closed period starts in or after its month. Reopening a month is refused while a later month is closed (`PERIOD_LATER_CLOSED`): reopen latest first. The period lock is one advisory lock per org, taken shared by every post and exclusive by every close and reopen.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | M03                           | M03 ledger audit W3. Balances are cumulative: a journal dated in an open May changes June's closing balances even with June closed, and a month before an org's first close was open forever.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| D-094 | The approval a reopen rests on is the database's, not the caller's                     | RESOLVED | Migration 0015: an approval row is born `pending` (or `auto_approved`); `created_at` and `decided_at` are stamped by the database; the decider must be the user the transaction is acting as (`app.user_id`); a period's `closed_at` and `reopened_at` are stamped by the database; closed → open needs an approval decided by an active platform super admin who is the acting user; and only the owner role can set `iam.users.is_super_admin`, by trigger.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | M03                           | M03 ledger audit W1 and W2, security review M3. 0014's guard only asked whether _some_ approved `period_reopen` row named the period, and `erp_app` could insert one born approved, or re-close a month leaving `closed_at` stale so an older approval matched again. Both reproduced. A trigger rather than column grants on `iam.users`, because Drizzle names every column in an INSERT.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

Note on D-001: most modules are custom F-commerce workflows. Building on ERPNext would add Frappe overhead to every one of them in exchange for savings only on the simplest modules. Its permission model is also enforced at the application level, and raw SQL bypasses it.

## Assumptions log (append during sessions)

Format: `- <date> · <milestone> · D-0xx · <assumption> · <why this default> · <how to change it>`

- 2026-09-20 · kickoff · D-034 · Each org carries a `brand` jsonb; neutral placeholder logo and a single shared accent hue until the client supplies real assets · Five brands were described as separate units but the architecture had no branding anywhere, so every invoice and every console would have looked identical · Replace the placeholder values per org; no code change, the values are data.
- 2026-09-20 · kickoff · D-035 · Branded login resolves the org from the route or hostname and preselects it · The client asked for a separate login per sister org; one identity with memberships is the correct model, so we meet the intent at the surface instead of forking accounts · Drop the org-resolution middleware and always show the switcher.
- 2026-09-20 · kickoff · D-037 · A settlement dated in a closed period posts to the earliest open period, keeping its own date as a reference · Courier settlement files routinely arrive after month-end close; the alternative is reopening a closed period every month · Change the resolver in the settlement posting rule; the reference field already records the true date.
- 2026-09-20 · M00 · D-039 · TypeScript pinned to 6.0.3 rather than the latest 7.0.2 · TypeScript 7 removed the classic compiler API, so type-aware lint rules — the ones that catch an un-awaited ledger post or a string/number mix-up on money — would stop working · Bump the catalog entry in pnpm-workspace.yaml once typescript-eslint supports 7.x.
- 2026-09-20 · M00 · D-040 · Packages compile to dist via project references; tests alias to source · The server needs emitDecoratorMetadata, which esbuild cannot emit, so a real tsc build is unavoidable, and aliasing tests to source keeps `pnpm test` independent of build order · Switch to a `development` export condition if dev startup gets slow.
- 2026-09-21 · M01 · D-042 · erp_owner is created NOSUPERUSER and the container superuser is a separate role · A superuser bypasses FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY, so with erp_owner as superuser every tenant policy was a no-op for the role that owns the tables — verified live before designing around it · Change the compose POSTGRES_USER and re-run db:bootstrap; the assertion in bootstrap.ts fails loudly if a role regains the attribute.
- 2026-09-21 · M01 · D-043 · The isolation policy wraps current_setting in nullif · A transaction-local set_config reverts to the empty string rather than unset, so the bare form throws on every reused pooled connection instead of returning zero rows · Emit a different expression from tenantTableSql() and add a migration; the policy is generated in one place precisely so this is a one-line change.
- 2026-09-21 · M01 · D-045 · Bangladesh geography is vendored rather than fetched, from a dataset compiled off the official gov.bd portals · A seed must not depend on a network, and inventing ~500 place names for a delivery system would produce mis-routed parcels and a zone table nobody trusts · Replace data/bd-geo.json and re-run the seed; ids come from the source so a reload is idempotent.
- 2026-09-21 · M01 · D-047 · Foreign keys between tenant tables carry org_id on both sides · PostgreSQL bypasses row security for referential-integrity checks, so a plain reference lets one org point at another's row — corrupting integrity, blocking that org from deleting its own row, and leaking an existence oracle · Drop the composite constraint and go back to a single-column reference, which the guard test will then fail.
- 2026-09-21 · M01 · D-048 · The tenancy guard is opt-out and compares the policy expression · An allowlist would have left every future module schema unguarded, silently, and counting policies would have let `using (true)` pass · Add a schema to GLOBAL_SCHEMAS or a table to GLOBAL_TABLES, which is then a visible decision rather than an omission.
- 2026-09-21 · M01 · D-049 · erp_analytics holds no read grant on any tenant table yet · A blanket cross-org SELECT would be a standing hole for the years before anything needs it; opting in per table keeps the blast radius equal to what analytics actually reads · Pass analyticsReadsAllOrgs to tenantTableSql in the migration that adds the rollup table.

- 2026-09-24 · M02b · D-066 · Better Auth credential events are audited into each org the user belongs to rather than a global security log · `audit.audit_log` is tenant-scoped with a NOT NULL org, and a sign-in belongs to no org · Add a global `audit.security_events` table in M04 and point `recordAuthEvent` at it.
- 2026-09-24 · M02b · D-067 · Approval events are collected in memory until M04's outbox · There is no outbox or Socket.IO yet, and §19's contract still needs exercising · Replace the body of `ApprovalsService.emit` with `enqueue(tx, …)`; the call sites already sit inside the transaction.
- 2026-09-24 · M02b · D-068 · A request under its org's threshold is auto-approved, not queued for a lower-tier checker · §6 routes supplier payments "above the threshold" through approvals and the domain rule already said no approval is required below it · For the branch item types, write `pending` instead of `auto_approved` below the threshold; the branch-tier checker rule (D-062) already admits those.
- 2026-09-24 · M03 · D-084 · The chart of accounts is §7's subtypes as 40 coded accounts plus hand cash per branch, with the operating categories as five `operating_expense` accounts · The client's accountant has not reviewed a chart yet (D-027) and the ledger needs one to post anything · Edit `packages/db/src/seed/coa-template.ts` and re-seed; adding and renaming are safe, changing a subtype is refused once the account exists.
- 2026-09-24 · M03 · D-086 · Cash in credits only other-income accounts and is dated by the server · Anything else books revenue with nothing behind it, and a client-supplied date would be back-dating without the approval §7 requires · Add subtypes to `CASH_IN_INCOME_SUBTYPES`; accept a date once `back_dated_entry` executes.
- 2026-09-24 · M03 · D-087 · Errors carry `{ code, message }` until M04's problem+json · CLAUDE.md requires codes from `errors.ts` and the ledger is the first module with domain errors worth branching on · M04 reshapes the body; the codes stay.
- 2026-09-24 · M03 · D-091 · Journals are plain tables, not partitioned · Nothing in §7 asks for it and composite tenant FKs would need the partition key · Partition in a new migration if §29's budgets demand it.
- 2026-09-24 · M03 · D-092 · One cash-in is capped at ৳1 crore by default · Cash in has no second person, and an unbounded single entry was the one way to push balances past what a Poisha holds · Set `LEDGER_CASH_IN_MAX_POISHA`; route large cash-ins through approval once the client says where the line is.
- 2026-09-25 · M09 · D-095 · A supplier payment posts two lines, Dr supplier / Cr wallet, with no bank-charge line · §7's table puts `(+ Dr Bank charges)` on the wallet _transfer_ only, `supply.supplier_payments` has no charge column and no caller can pass one, so a charge path would be dead code in payables · Add the column, the §7 row and a new decision row together if the client charges fees on supplier payments.
- 2026-09-25 · M09 · D-096 · Supply line cost may be zero; payment and advance-application amounts may not · Free stock (samples, replacements) arrives on the same challan as paid lines and both `supply_lines_cost_not_negative` and `supply_return_lines_cost_not_negative` allow zero, so refusing zero at the edge would make the zero-cost branch unreachable · Change `costPoishaSchema` if the client says free lines must be booked separately.
- 2026-09-25 · M09/M10 · D-097 · `dispatch_unit_cost_poisha` and `original_unit_cost_poisha` are cost fields and are withheld from roles without `read:cost` · Both are purchase cost by another name and were reachable from the order desk and the returns screen; the schemas already carried comments claiming they were withheld while the names were missing from `COST_FIELDS`, so nothing stripped them · `packages/contracts/src/permissions/cost-fields.test.ts` now compares `COST_FIELDS` against the migrations, so a new cost column fails the build instead of leaking.
- 2026-09-25 · M10 · D-098 · §6's "unassigned queue" widening applies only to a caller holding the `sales` role · `reseller` holds the same `read Order|own` grant, so widening it for every `own`-scoped role would hand a reseller every unassigned staff order in the brand; §6's cell text sits on the sales column · Change `orderScopeSql` if the client wants another role on the queue.
- 2026-09-25 · M10 · D-099 · Recording an advance declares `create Wallet`, and raising an exchange declares `create Order` · Money arriving in a wallet is a wallet entry that posts a journal (§7), and `update Order` would let every sales agent post into a till; an exchange creates an order and reverses a delivered sale, while `update Order` is held by `warehouse`, whose §6 cell is "pack, ship, returns" · Move the grants in the controller if §6 is revised. **Update 2026-10-02:** M17 replaces the advance route; verifying a claim keeps `create Wallet` (D-235), and recording one is `update Order`, because it posts nothing.
- 2026-09-25 · M10 · D-100 · Delivery zones, charges and couriers are org configuration under `read Catalog` / `update Catalog`, seeded by `seedDeliveryZones()` as well as migration 0042 · §6 has no zone cell and inventory already puts locations under the catalog grants; 0042 writes nothing on a fresh database because migrations run before the seed creates the orgs, and an org with no zone and no courier cannot take an order at all · Give zones their own subject if the client wants them restricted separately.
- 2026-09-25 · M10 · D-101 · One advance per order; a second is refused with `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` · `customerAdvanceDraft` keys on `order:<id>:advance_received`, so a repeat returns the first journal with `created:false` while the order's own advance column rises — the books and the COD figure would silently diverge · Add a sequence to the idempotency key when the client needs instalments. **Update 2026-10-02:** superseded by D-230 (M17): each advance is its own row and its own journal, keyed on the advance.
- 2026-09-25 · M10 · D-102 · `erp_app` may DELETE `sales.order_lines`, bounded by 0041's trigger to pending, confirmed and on_hold orders · Removing a mis-keyed line is the commonest order-desk edit, `order_lines_qty_positive` forbids zeroing it, and 0041 already anticipated the delete — without the grant that branch was unreachable · Revoke the grant to disable line removal entirely.
- 2026-09-25 · M06–M10 · D-103 · The schema-drift gate covers every module schema, not just `iam`, `org` and `ref` · `schemaFilter` had never been widened as modules landed, so the gate compared three schemas out of twelve and none of the money-bearing ones · Add each new Postgres schema to `schemaFilter` in `packages/db/drizzle.config.ts` in the migration's own commit.
- 2026-09-25 · M04 · D-104 · `check-schema-drift` fails when drizzle-kit prints an error, not only when it exits non-zero · Snapshots `0017` and `0018` shared an id (a hand-copied snapshot file), which makes drizzle-kit print a collision error, generate nothing and still exit 0 — so the gate saw no new files and reported PASS from migration 0018 to 0060 while comparing nothing at all · The chain is repaired and the check now inspects the generator's output; mutation-tested by reintroducing the collision.
- 2026-09-25 · M13 · D-105 · `fraud.phone_signals` and `fraud.provider_checks` are cross-org with no `org_id` and no RLS, reached as `erp_app` inside `withOrg()` · §5 exception 1; the analytics role is reserved for the analytics module and cannot write, while these counters are written on every order outcome — and what keeps the exception narrow is the column list, not a policy: migration 0045 gives those tables nowhere to store an order, a name, an address, an amount, a brand or a user · A test enumerates `information_schema.columns` and fails on any such name.
- 2026-09-25 · M13 · D-106 · `fraud.phone_signals.manual_flags` holds `{at, severity}` only; the reason and author stay in tenant-scoped `fraud.phone_flags` · The text an agent typed about a buyer belongs to that brand; a reason or an org id in a table four other brands read would make this schema the thing it exists to avoid · Count is `jsonb_array_length`, so there is no second counter to drift.
- 2026-09-25 · M13 · D-107 · No phone number and no phone hash appears in any response, event payload or audit row · A hash is a stable identifier for one person across all five brands, and on a socket (no `serializeFor`) or in an append-only audit table it is a permanent cross-brand correlation key · Asserted structurally: the isolation suite fails on any 64-hex or UUID-shaped string in a badge body.
- 2026-09-25 · M13 · D-108 · `FRAUD_PHONE_HASH_KEY` has no fallback and no rotation; a missing or short key stops the process at `app.init()` · A fallback key produces different hashes, so the system would keep working while every number silently became a new person and five brands' history detached from the buyers it describes — unrecoverable, since a hash cannot be reversed to compare · Rotation would be a migration that rehashes from each org's `sales.customers`.
- 2026-09-25 · M13 · D-109 · A provider outage costs zero points and writes no row; the badge falls back to internal-only and says so · A provider being down is a fact about us, not the buyer — penalising it would refuse every order in the country during a third party's bad afternoon — and a stored zero row would read as a clean record for the whole TTL, blinding the check until it expired · Acceptance check 3.
- 2026-09-25 · M13 · D-110 · A number with no history scores at the allow floor (70) and is allowed, with a `no_history` reason on the badge · Most COD orders in Bangladesh come from a number nobody has seen; a model that warns on all of them trains the desk to ignore the badge, which costs more than the fraud it catches · OPEN for the client: confirm strangers should not be treated as suspect.
- 2026-09-25 · M13 · D-111 · `read FraudSignal` has no row dimension, so `own` and `branch` scope admit the whole aggregate · §6 grants the badge to reseller at `own` and branch_manager at `branch`, but a badge is a per-phone aggregate across all five brands keyed by an HMAC — there is no `created_by` to compare a reseller against and no `branch_id` for a branch manager, and without acknowledging that the scope backstop answered 500 for both roles · Declared through `scopeHasNoRowDimension()`, which requires a written reason; OPEN for the client: should a reseller be able to look up a number that is not on one of their own orders?
- 2026-09-25 · M10 · D-112 · An order event may be dated only today or yesterday in Asia/Dhaka; earlier needs `back_dated_entry`, later is refused outright · §7 gives no upper bound, and forward-dated revenue lands in a period no close can reach · `withinPostingWindow` in `packages/domain/src/dates`; tested at both Dhaka boundaries, including that 18:30Z is already tomorrow there.
- 2026-09-25 · M10 · D-113 · An order ships only from a `warehouse` or a `showroom` location; `damaged` and `in_transit` are refused · Dispatching off the damaged shelf sells written-off stock and bypasses the write-off approval entirely (D-008) · Checked at create, at edit and on every transition.
- 2026-09-25 · M10 · D-114 · §6's "customers of own orders" also admits a buyer with no orders at all · The literal reading hides a just-created buyer from the agent who entered them, which breaks create-buyer-then-take-order and reads as the create endpoint having failed; a buyer nobody has ordered for is nobody's, so admitting them takes nothing from anyone · Narrow the rule if the client wants buyers invisible until an order exists.
- 2026-09-26 · M25 · D-115 · A declared profit divides by each investor's agreed `share_bps`, or by capital held at the period's last day, chosen per declaration · D-019 fixes the amount and says nothing about the split; weighting by lifetime gross contributions would let September's money earn August's profit and let capital already returned keep earning · Weights are snapshotted onto the distribution's lines, so changing a share later cannot restate a distribution already paid; all-zero weights are refused rather than split equally.
- 2026-09-26 · M25 · D-116 · A distribution is dated the Asia/Dhaka day the checker approved it; the period distributed is metadata · A September declaration of August profit is a September equity movement, which makes D-037's closed-period problem structurally impossible here — the month it posts into is the month still running · Declaring for a month that has not ended is refused.
- 2026-09-26 · M25 · D-117 · A withdrawal touches the ledger only at `paid`, not at `approved` · Deviates from §15's "all steps post to the ledger": an approval is a decision, not a payment, and a journal posted at approval shows money gone on a day the bank account still held it · The wallet is named at review, because "approve this payment" is unanswerable without knowing which account it leaves, and frozen thereafter by trigger.
- 2026-09-26 · M25 · D-118 · Investor money needs three distinct people: requester ≠ reviewer ≠ checker, and checker ≠ requester · Without the third comparison, A-requests → B-reviews → A-approves put two of three signatures on the same hand · Both types are in `SERVER_DERIVED_AMOUNT_TYPES`, so no threshold can auto-approve one however small.
- 2026-09-26 · M25 · D-119 · `investor_distribution` and `investor_withdrawal` are added to §6's checker and requester maps: org_admin checks both, accounts checks withdrawals only · Money leaving to an owner is a payment, which is the whole of §6's `accounts` cell; declaring profit is an equity decision rather than a payment, so it stays with org_admin · Without these rows the flow 403s for every role §6 assigns and only a platform super admin could use it.
- 2026-09-26 · M25 · D-120 · Investor screens ship in the staff console, not the portal · §15 puts them in `apps/portal`, which is still a stub until M22; an investor signing into the console sees exactly their own row through `read Investor` at `own` scope, and 2FA is already mandatory for the role · Revisit with the portal PWA. **Update 2026-10-02:** superseded for investors by D-310; the portal pages exist and the console screens stay for staff.
- 2026-09-26 · M25 · D-121 · A refused withdrawal or declaration is closed by an explicit `/reject` route · An approvals executor runs only on `approved`, so a rejected decision left the owning row waiting — which for a withdrawal permanently consumed that investor's headroom by the refused amount, and for a distribution made that month's profit undeclarable for ever · Replace with a rejection hook in `ApprovalsService` and delete both routes.
- 2026-09-26 · M12 · D-122 · Steadfast's four `*_approval_pending` statuses map to `out_for_delivery` and `hold`, never to `delivered` · They mean the rider has reported an outcome the courier's office has not confirmed, and recognising revenue on that (D-003) would mean reversing it when the office disagreed — in a closed month, every month · Change the two entries in `STEADFAST_STATUS_MAP` if Steadfast confirms the states are final.
- 2026-09-26 · M12 · D-123 · A courier's `cancelled` on a shipped order becomes `start_return`; on an unshipped one it becomes nothing at all · §9 forbids cancelling a shipped order — it becomes a return — and before dispatch the units are still ours, so whether to cancel or rebook is a person's call rather than a webhook's · Change `orderTriggerFor` in `packages/domain/src/courier/status.ts`.
- 2026-09-26 · M12 · D-124 · The settlement statement is a column-mapped CSV, and the parser refuses a file rather than skipping a row it cannot read · No Bangladeshi courier documents a settlement-file format, and a skipped row is a parcel whose cash silently never arrives — with the totals still balancing, because the row is not in them either · Add a provider-specific `parseSettlementFile` when a courier publishes a format; the column names are already configuration.
- 2026-09-26 · M12 · D-125 · A settlement whose matched charges exceed its matched collections is refused at import rather than posted as a payable · §7 has no journal for it — "Dr W" with a negative amount is not a journal — and couriers carry the balance into the next statement themselves · Add a courier-payable subtype to §7's chart and a posting rule, then relax `settlementOwesCourier`.
- 2026-09-26 · M12 · D-126 · A `settlement_posting` approval carries the matched **gross**, not the net · It is what the two journals together take off the courier's balance, so it is the size of the whole transaction; approving the net would understate a statement that was mostly charges · Change `SettlementsService.requestApproval` and migration 0051's equality check together — they must agree.
- 2026-09-26 · M19 · _(numbering note: M19's rows are D-186–D-192. The gap after D-126 is deliberate — fourteen code comments across `packages/domain/src/payroll/`, `packages/db/schema/hr/`, the migrations and the console already cite those ids, and renumbering fourteen references to close a cosmetic gap is the more likely way to introduce a wrong cross-reference.)_
- 2026-09-26 · M19 · D-029 · RESOLVED (was OPEN): payroll is basic + allowances + bonus − loan instalment − absence deduction · Allowances carry a `prorated` flag (earnings default true, deductions never); a bonus is a per-run input and is never prorated; the absence deduction prorates basic plus the proratable allowances only · Deductions exceeding a month's salary are refused, never clamped — the cause is almost always an amount typed in taka rather than poisha.
- 2026-09-26 · M19 · D-186 · An unrecorded working day counts as present, configurable per run · Whose mistake it punishes decides this: the biometric CSV is imported by an office administrator, so `absent` would mean a forgotten upload docks a month's salary from everybody who turned up, discovered on payday · The count of unrecorded days is stored on every payslip and shown to the checker before they approve; set `hr.payroll_runs.unrecorded_policy`. OPEN for the client.
- 2026-09-26 · M19 · D-187 · Friday is the default weekend, and an org's calendar is created on demand · Friday alone is the single-day weekend most Dhaka retail and warehouse operations run; migration 0057 backfills existing orgs, and `ensureDefaultCalendar()` covers orgs added later because migrations run _before_ the org seed, so a data-only migration inserts nothing on a fresh database · An employee may point at a different calendar, so a six-day showroom and a five-day head office coexist. OPEN for the client, per brand.
- 2026-09-26 · M19 · D-188 · Staff loans are interest-free and carry no approval · §6's maker-checker list does not name staff loans, and §7's `interest_expense` is for loans where the _company_ borrows · See the follow-up: one hr user can currently disburse a principal they typed, which is maker == checker on real cash.
- 2026-09-26 · M19 · D-189 · A payslip is rendered on demand and never stored · §22's rule is authorize-then-sign because a signed URL is a bearer token no later check can revoke; a payslip with no URL has nothing to leak, and it is wholly derived from append-only `hr.payroll_lines`, so a stored copy would be a second source of truth for a document whose first source cannot change · Costs one Gotenberg round trip per download.
- 2026-09-26 · M19 · D-190 · A payroll run under the org's threshold posts in the same request · `payroll_run` is raised with a server-derived amount, which is exactly what lets a threshold apply (D-068, D-072) — so the approvals module can decide the request before the run points at it and the executor then finds no run · Without this, one threshold setting stranded a month permanently: the request is spent so `decide` refuses it, `cancel` reaches only `rejected`, and the month's unique index blocked a replacement.
- 2026-09-26 · M19 · D-191 · One live payroll run per org per month, and one pending run at a time · The unique index excludes `cancelled` and `rejected`, which is what makes the ordinary loop possible — checker rejects March, HR fixes the attendance, submits March again; an unconditional index let a rejected row consume the month for ever · The single-pending rule is correctness, not workflow: two pending runs could each freeze a payslip taking the same loan instalment.
- 2026-09-26 · M19 · D-192 · HR pays salary and disburses loans out of a ledger wallet account, never through `wallets.*` · Invariant 12, and the wallets module exposes no `*.api.ts`; a wallet _is_ its ledger account (M08's own schema comment), so nothing is lost · The console's picker reads `GET /v1/ledger/wallets`, which returns exactly those ids.
- 2026-09-26 · M12 · D-127 · The courier adapters live in `apps/server/src/modules/courier/adapters/` rather than `packages/integrations/couriers` · This session's file ownership put the module in `apps/server`; the adapter interface has no server dependency · Move both adapters and the `CourierAdapter` interface in M15, when the second and third couriers arrive.
- 2026-09-26 · M12 · D-129 · A collection is recorded on a consignment only once the order's transition has actually posted, and migration 0052 refuses one on a parcel whose order has no `handed_over_at` · `settlementCandidates` reports `collected_poisha` as the courier's debt while §7's delivery journal supplies the matching debit, so writing the figure without the journal made the consignment a matchable candidate whose settlement credited `courier_receivable` for money never debited — the courier's balance goes negative and the only symptom is a variance in the reconciliation report · Relax the trigger if a courier is ever settled for a parcel dispatched outside the order flow; the service-side split must stay either way.
- 2026-09-26 · M12 · D-130 · A `settlement_posting` below the org's threshold is auto-approved and posted in one transaction, taking the batch from `imported` through `pending_approval` to `posted` · D-068 auto-approves below the threshold and the approvals module runs the executor inline, before the raising service can attach the approval; refusing that state threw inside `approvals.request` and rolled the request back with it, leaving COD already in the bank permanently unpostable — for exactly the small statements a threshold exists to wave through · Add `settlement_posting` to `NEVER_AUTO_APPROVE_TYPES` if the client wants every settlement checked.
- 2026-09-28 · M07 · D-142 · An opening balance is a receipt for costing purposes and carries the unit cost it is entered at · §8 lists only supply receipts and customer returns as cost-bearing, and taken literally, stock migrated in at M14a would carry no cost, so every COGS line and every valuation for those units would read zero — worse than an approximate cost · Row added retroactively: cited in `packages/domain/src/inventory/movements.ts` since M07 with no entry here.
- 2026-09-28 · M07 · D-143 · A transfer is received as dispatched; a shortfall is written off from the in-transit location through an approval · Stock that left one shelf and did not arrive at another is exactly the event a second person should sign off on; letting the receiving clerk type a smaller number would turn a loss into a typo · Row added retroactively (`transfers.service.ts`).
- 2026-09-28 · M07 · D-144 · `stock.drift_detected` carries counts only, never the drifting quantities · A socket has no `serializeFor` in front of it, so a payload that grew the detail would become a stock report that bypasses permissions; drift is investigated by opening the reconciliation row · Row added retroactively (`packages/contracts/src/events.ts`).
- 2026-09-28 · M07 · D-145 · Rejected stock write-offs are closed by a nightly sweep, not at the moment of rejection · The approvals module runs executors on approval only, so a refused write-off otherwise sits `pending_approval` for ever and reads as though nobody looked at it; the sweep reads `approvals.approval_requests` directly, a narrow exception to invariant 12 · Replace with the rejection hook `ApprovalsService` still lacks (the same gap D-121 works around for investors). Row added retroactively.

- 2026-09-28 · M10/M12 · D-171 · M10 owns the courier **party** row; M12 adds adapters, parcels and settlements around it rather than replacing it · Every delivery debits `courier_receivable` with a `party_id`, and without a row to name there is one receivable balance across all couriers, which makes "how much is this courier holding of ours" — the question COD reconciliation exists to answer — unanswerable · Row added retroactively (`packages/db/src/schema/orders/index.ts`, `courier/index.ts`).
- 2026-09-28 · M10 · D-172 · An order with no named location fulfils from the org's first active warehouse by code · Four of the five brands have exactly one warehouse, and the fifth names the location on the order, which is why `orders.location_id` exists · Make it an org setting if a brand grows a second warehouse without naming locations. Row added retroactively (`orders.repository.ts`).
- 2026-09-28 · M12 · D-176 · An account cannot be activated on an adapter whose `verified` is false unless the installation sets `COURIER_<NAME>_ENABLED=true` · A wrong guess about a courier API fails silently — parcels booked that nobody collects, or a status mapped to `delivered` and revenue posted for cash nobody collected — so enabling an unverified adapter has to be a decision somebody made rather than a default they inherited · Row added retroactively (cited across the courier module); see the Steadfast entry under Unverified integrations.
- 2026-09-30 · platform · D-131 · The outbox dispatcher claims org by org, inside each org's context, and holds the row lock from claim to mark · `events.outbox` is FORCE RLS, which binds the owner role too; claiming with no org context matched nothing, so the dispatcher had never delivered an event in any environment while every suite drained the outbox by another route. One transaction per row also stops a second worker redelivering a row between the claim and the mark · `OutboxDispatcher.drain` in `apps/server/src/platform/events/dispatcher.ts`; `dispatcher.db.test.ts` fails at `delivered: 0` with the old claim restored.
- 2026-09-30 · platform · D-132 · Failed outbox rows retry after 5 s, 30 s, 2 min and 10 min, and a dead-lettered row raises `job.failed` once · A failed row was the oldest pending row, so the next claim took it again and all five attempts went in one pass; any outage longer than milliseconds dead-lettered whatever was in flight, including the order outcomes the fraud counters are built from. The worker also re-reported every dead row on each restart · `RETRY_DELAYS_SECONDS` in `outbox.ts`; columns `next_attempt_at` and `reported_at` from migration 0072.
- 2026-09-30 · platform · D-133 · Routes authenticated by a signature rather than a cookie are exempt from the CSRF double-submit: `/public/v1/` and `/v1/courier/webhooks/` · CSRF borrows cookie authority, and these take none; a landing page's server or a courier cannot obtain the token at all, so both answered 403 to every real caller while passing in their harnesses. The public tracking route is deliberately not on the list: it is a browser route and keeps the check · `SIGNED_MACHINE_PREFIXES` in `platform/http/csrf.ts`. A handler under one of these prefixes must never read the session.
- 2026-09-30 · platform · D-134 · The API starts a pg-boss client at boot, and both entrypoints take their queues from one list · `enqueue(tx, ...)` needs a started runtime in the calling process; the API had none, so any request that queued work threw, and the nightly stock reconciliation and the fraud refresh had no handler in the worker either. Every suite that enqueues starts its own runtime, so nothing noticed · `apps/server/src/job-queues.ts`; a module that adds a queue adds it there with its handler. The worker must start first on a fresh database because only it installs the schema; the API waits up to a minute.
- 2026-09-30 · M15 · D-135 · An order cannot be pointed at another courier while a pending or booked parcel exists · The delivery journal debits `courier_receivable` against the order's courier and the settlement credits the consignment's; after a change the two land on different parties while the org total still agrees, so the only symptom is two reconciliation variances nobody can pair · `TransitionsService.assertCourierChangeAllowed`; cancel the booking to free the order.
- 2026-09-30 · M11 · D-136 · A site's signing secret is derived as HMAC(`INTAKE_KEY_SECRET`, key id); only a SHA-256 fingerprint is stored, and the key id embeds its org · A copy of the database cannot sign a request, and a changed master key makes old keys fail rather than verify wrongly; a public request has no org, and embedding it avoids a cross-org lookup table · Rotating the master key means re-issuing every site key. Switch to encrypted storage if keys must be recoverable.
- 2026-09-30 · M11 · D-137 · A public order request is signed over `timestamp.idempotencyKey.rawBody` with a 300-second window · Binding the idempotency key stops a captured request being replayed as a new order · `signingBase` and `docs/public-api.md` must change together.

- 2026-09-30 · M11 · D-139 · A Turnstile bypass, a non-Cloudflare verify URL or per-process intake rate limits refuse to boot in production; a missing secret switches the intake off with 503 · An unsafe setting must be loud, while a missing one must not take the API down · `intake.config.ts`.

The till shows the ceiling; the server enforces it by holding a sale above it for a manager whose own ceiling covers it · `POS_DISCOUNT_CEILING_BPS` in `packages/domain/src/pos/discount-policy.ts`.

The journal key derives from the same id (`pos_sale:<id>:sold`), so even a path that reached the ledger twice would get one journal · `SyncService` and `canonical.ts`.

A wrong clock or an edited payload posted straight into a counted, signed-off day, so its closing no longer matched the ledger and the next day raised an equal and opposite variance · `outsideShift` and `postingDateFor` in `holds.ts`; `PosPostingService.dayClosed`.

- 2026-09-30 · M23 · D-177 · A branch day close derives each wallet's M08 closing id from the branch, the date and the wallet, never from the client's closing id; a client id already used for another branch or day is refused, and a count that differs from the one M08 already holds is refused as a recount · Derived from the client's id, a retry that changed the date re-reached the first day's closing and recorded it as the second day's, which then counted as closed with no count behind it · `BranchesService.close`; the console issues a new id whenever the date or a count changes.
- 2026-10-01 · M08 · D-193 · Payables and receivables aging is by open item: each party's days are netted, then a settlement clears the oldest amount still open (first in, first out), and money paid ahead of anything owed is shown as "paid ahead", never aged and never a negative bucket. `ageOpenItems` in `packages/domain/src/ledger/aging.ts` is the definition; the query returns one row per party, account and day · The first cut bucketed every ledger line by its own date, so a ৳1,000 bill forty days old, part-paid ৳400 three days ago, read +৳1,000 at 31–60 and −৳400 at 0–7: the right total and a report nobody could chase debts from. A party whose balance is back to zero is left out. Known limit: a reversal posted on a later day settles the oldest open amount rather than the document it reverses, so the total is right and the split can be older than the truth (ledger audit N1) · Revisit if supplies (M09) or orders need matching to a named invoice rather than oldest-first, or match reversals through `reverses_journal_id`.
- 2026-10-01 · M08 · D-194 · The cash dialog offers a receipt photo on every movement and requires one only for petty cash, uploaded private under `petty-cash-receipt` (the only purpose an entry accepts) · The server already took an optional receipt on cash in and cash out and validated it the same way; offering it everywhere keeps the upload mounted when the movement changes, so a receipt cannot be silently dropped or carried into the wrong entry · Revisit if the client wants receipts compulsory above an amount.
- 2026-10-01 · M23 · D-195 · An exchange's replacement sale pairs only with a return of the same branch that no other sale is paired with; otherwise the sale is kept and held for review as `exchange_mismatch`, with no link written and the requested return id and the problem in the review detail. A composite foreign key and a one-sale-per-return unique index (migration 0075) are the floor under the check · `exchangeReturnId` was stored as the till sent it — any uuid, another brand's return, a return already paired. Refusing the document instead would leave the till retrying a sale whose customer already paid (D-164), so the money is kept and the pairing is what a manager looks at · Revisit if exchanges across branches become a business rule.

- 2026-10-01 · M08 · D-198 · A wallet that still holds money cannot be closed (`WALLET_NOT_EMPTY`); every posted journal counts, whatever its date. - 2026-10-01 · platform · follow-up · Every trusted origin — the staff apps and one per active brand site (`withBrandOrigins`) — may now send credentialed PUT, PATCH and DELETE, not just POST (`platform/http/cors.ts`). The brand sites were already trusted for credentialed POST, and the double-submit CSRF cookie on the shared parent domain is existing behaviour, so script injected into any brand landing page could already act as the signed-in user; the threat model should say so, and a later hardening could take brand sites off the credentialed list (they need only the public intake and tracking routes) (ledger audit N8).
- 2026-10-01 · M12/M15 · D-199 · The courier module reads the courier's own name and code through `OrdersPort.courierParties` (backed by `OrdersApi.courierParties`), never by joining `sales.couriers`; `apps/server/src/modules/module-boundaries.test.ts` fails the build if any schema but its own, `org.*` or `ref.*` appears in its SQL, and holds this module to no debt at all · Eight queries joined M10's table for a display name, which is the coupling invariant 12 forbids: M10 could not change the shape of its own table without breaking a module that never declared it was reading it, and no test would have failed. The file's own header had claimed "nothing here reads another module's tables" throughout — a sentence is not a check. `org.*` and `ref.*` are exempt because §5 makes them global and no module owns them · If a third module needs the same names, the port method is already the place to take them from.
- 2026-10-01 · M15 · D-200 · **OPEN (needs a RedX merchant account).** RedX is excluded from M15 check 1 twice over. Its parcel-creation contract test proves only that the adapter matches a third-party plugin, not RedX's API. Steadfast, Pathao and the fake adapter are unaffected — each is transcribed from a cited first-party document · Checked again for this pass: `redx.com.bd/developer-documentation/` 301s to a 404, the developer portal renders nothing without a merchant session, and the `redx-for-woocommerce` plugin was downloaded and read — it has no status handling, no webhook route and no status constants, and it is a community plugin (an individual author, `shopupwp` among its contributors) rather than RedX's own SDK, which the code comment had called it. The adapter stays `verified: false`, so D-176 already stops it being activated without an explicit opt-in, and an unmapped word becomes `unknown`, changes nothing and surfaces in the stuck-parcel queue with its raw text · Closes when somebody with a RedX sandbox confirms the webhook vocabulary: transcribe it, set `redx` to `document` in `VOCABULARY_PROVENANCE` (`packages/domain/src/courier/status.ts`), and set `verified`.
- 2026-10-01 · M05 · D-201 · The review screenshot set is written by `apps/console/e2e/shell-quality.stack.ts` (check 10, plus the inbox test), not by a separate `scripts/screenshot-review.mjs` · The script M05's scope names would need its own signed-in browser, seeded data and theme/language switching — all of which the real-stack Playwright harness already has.
