- 2026-10-01 · M05 · D-202 · The notifications inbox shows this session's `approval.requested` (pending ones only) and `approval.decided` events; it is not persisted, and M16's in-app channel adds `notification.created` to the same list · §10's in-app notifications are M16's (`notify` module, delivery log); until then the events a person already receives are what needs their attention, and a session-only list cannot disagree with a store that does not exist yet · M16 adds the event, a list endpoint and read state on the server. **Update 2026-10-02:** M16 did not build the in-app channel (persisted notifications, a list endpoint, read state); its plan scoped them out, and this inbox stays session-only until a milestone takes them up.
- 2026-10-01 · M16 · D-203 · Promo-code routes use the `LandingPage` subject: `read` lists codes and runs the live check, `create` and `update` make and pause them · §6 has one row covering both — "Landing pages, promo codes" — so a brand's marketing people hold one grant for a page and the codes that promote it, and §6 gives sales `read` there as "apply codes", which is exactly the live check. Inventing a `PromoCode` subject would mean editing the permission matrix and its spec fixture as a side effect of this milestone rather than as a decision · Revisit if the client wants desk agents able to make codes without editing pages.
- 2026-10-01 · M16 · D-204 · On an order, a promo code is a **word** the desk sends, never an id or an amount. The server prices it against the lines it priced itself, folds the goods discount into the order's discount (revenue is then credited net of it by the existing delivery posting, §7 — no new rule; corrected after the M16 ledger audit, which found this row saying it reached Sales discounts), priced on the lines' nets after the agent's own typed order discount is spread over them (`promoBasis`, W3) and takes the delivery discount off the charge, writes the order, and redeems — all in one transaction, so a code whose last use went elsewhere between pricing and redeeming rolls the whole order back. A use goes back on **every** cancel through a new state-machine effect `release_promo`, and on nothing else: a cancel is only reachable before the order ships, and once it ships the use is kept, returned or not. Releasing is conditional on the redemption still being applied, so a cancel delivered twice returns one use. A promo refusal crossing the orders route answers `409` with an RFC 9457 `reason` extension member (`limit_reached`, `wrong_channel`, …), published only when it is a lower-case token · The `promoCodeId` order create used to accept let a client name any code and pair it with any discount. The release was first written inside the customer-stats effect, which a cancel from `pending` does not declare, so it never ran there; making it its own effect fixed that and exposed that the effect switch was not exhaustive — it is now, so an effect the machine declares without a handler no longer compiles. Promo channels share the order vocabulary in `packages/contracts` (`channels.ts`), plus `pos`; the first cut invented `web` and `desk`, which no order carries · Revisit if an edited order's lines should re-price its code; today the discount stays as redeemed.
- 2026-10-02 · M16 · D-205 · Notification routes (templates, opt-outs, the delivery log) use the `Campaign` subject, held by org_admin and marketing at `org`. There is no route that sends: a message is queued through `NotifyApi` by the code with a reason to send it — an occasion, an order — inside that code's own transaction · §6 has no notifications row, and the obvious subject, `Customer`, is the wrong one: sales holds it at `own` scope, while every notify route is org-wide — the delivery log lists every buyer's phone and the opt-out list is the brand's — so admitting sales on `read Customer` would hand each agent the brand's whole contact history. `Campaign` is exactly the audience for what a brand says to its buyers and when · Revisit if §6 gains a notifications row, or if a support role needs to read the delivery log for one buyer (an `own`-scoped read would then be its own route).
- 2026-10-02 · M16 · D-206 · **ASSUMED.** An occasion greeting (birthday, anniversary) gives a personal, single-use 10%-off code (1,000 bps) valid for seven days from the day, with no minimum order and no cap, the same for every brand; sent in Bangla (D-036) through the `occasion.birthday` / `occasion.anniversary` templates at 09:00 Dhaka, the minute quiet hours end. A brand with no such template greets nobody and the job reports each buyer as failed with the reason rather than crashing. The values are one constant in `crm.service.ts` (`OCCASION_CODE`) · §10 asks for birthday and anniversary codes without saying what they are worth, and nothing yet holds per-brand CRM settings; a constant is honest about being one value rather than pretending to be configuration. 10% for a week is small enough to post without approval and short enough that an un-redeemed code does not sit on the books; personal and single-use means a greeting cannot be forwarded and spent by a group · Revisit when the client says what each brand should give: a per-org settings row, read by `runOccasions`.
- 2026-10-02 · platform · D-207 · A `singletonKey` collapses duplicates only on a queue whose `policy` is `exclusive`; `QueueDefinition.policy` now sets it, and `enqueue`'s documentation says so. pg-boss enforces a key only under its deduplicating policies, and this platform created every queue as `standard`, so **every `singletonKey` in the codebase was enforced by nothing** while `enqueue` promised a collapse · Found while building M16's fan-out, whose second run enqueued every brand twice. The risk that mattered was courier booking: `runBooking` checks a consignment is still pending in one transaction and calls the courier in none (invariant 6), so two duplicate jobs picked up by two workers at once would both pass the check and book one parcel with the courier twice. `delivery.book_parcel` and `delivery.cancel_parcel` are now `exclusive`, safe because each has one enqueue site and it always sets a key, as are M16's per-brand CRM queues. `exclusive` cannot be the default: a job with no key is keyed as the empty string, so only one keyless job could ever be pending, which would stall `notify.send`. The other keyed queues stay `standard` because their handlers are idempotent — a settlement fetch by fingerprint, a webhook and a poll by the parcel's state, a fraud refresh by overwrite — so a duplicate costs work, not correctness. `jobs.test.ts` pins both halves: same key twice gives one job on `exclusive` and two on `standard` · The policy is set when a queue is created, and **cannot be changed afterwards**: `createQueue` leaves an existing queue alone and pg-boss 12.33.2's `updateQueue` throws `queue policy cannot be changed after creation` (corrected after the M16 ledger audit; this row first said `updateQueue` would do it). A database created before a release that changes a policy keeps the old one, so the worker now compares each queue's stored policy with its definition at boot and logs `queue policy drift` with the remedy (`JobsRuntime.policyDrift()`, `jobs.test.ts`); the remedy is a drain-and-recreate, in `docs/runbooks/queue-policy.md`. The shared dev database is such a database: its `delivery.book_parcel` and `delivery.cancel_parcel` are still `standard`.
- 2026-10-02 · M16 · D-208 · The re-engagement list (`GET /v1/crm/reengagement` and its export) never contains a number that opted out of marketing SMS: not on the page, not in the count, not in the file. It is gated, audited and capped like the buyer list (`export BuyerExport`, D-148). A buyer is _lapsed_ when they have at least one delivered order and their latest order that was not cancelled was placed on or before today − N days, by the Dhaka calendar. The buyer profile (`GET /v1/customers/:id/profile`) shows brand-wide totals, segment, favourites and greetings to anyone who can read the buyer, but lists only the orders the caller could open (`orderScopeSql`), as the desk's lookup does · "Opted out" was asked of the brand, and a downloaded file is beyond the notify module's suppression the moment it exists, so the list itself is the last place the wish can be kept. The latest _placed_ order rather than the latest delivered one, because a buyer whose parcel is on a van today has bought. Favourites rank units kept (delivered quantity on delivered orders), with category names and size labels from `CatalogApi.skuFacets` rather than a read of `catalog.*` · Revisit if marketing needs a list that deliberately includes opted-out buyers for a non-SMS channel; that would be its own filter, defaulting off.
- 2026-10-02 · M16 · D-209 · The till keeps `NoPromoCodes`: M16 applies promo codes in the order desk only, and `PROMO_CODES` stays bound to the refusing default · M16's Build list says codes are "applied in the order desk", and binding the till's validator alone would be worse than nothing: the sale document carries no code, so nothing would record a redemption, and a single-use code would be spendable at every till in the brand. Doing it properly is its own piece of work — the sale carries the code and the discount the server priced, sync redeems it in the sale's transaction (a conditional claim, as the desk does), a code used up between the check and the sync holds the sale for review rather than rejecting a sale that already happened, and the redemption references a sale as well as an order · Follow-up: bind the till with sync-time redemption and a hold on exhaustion, in the POS track.
- 2026-10-02 · M16 · D-210 · **ASSUMED.** RFM scores are quintiles 1–5 _within each brand_, computed nightly from the counters the order transitions keep: recency from the buyer's last **delivered** order (Dhaka date), frequency from delivered orders, monetary from lifetime value. Ties share a score, so the same history never lands in two segments by sort order. Eight segments from the scores (`segmentFor`): champions (R≥4, F≥4, M≥4), loyal, new, promising, at_risk, hibernating, needs_attention, and prospect for a buyer with nothing delivered · §10 asks for RFM segments without a method. Quintiles within the brand make each brand's champions its own best buyers rather than the group's, and delivered rather than placed orders keeps a buyer whose orders all came back out of the top · Revisit when marketing names the segments it actually targets.
- 2026-10-02 · M16 · D-211 · The CRM is built inside the `orders` module, in the `sales` schema (`sales.customer_segments`, `sales.occasion_issues`), not as its own `crm` module and schema as §3's module table had it. Its jobs are `crm.schedule_segments` (02:30 Dhaka, fanning out one `crm.score_segments` per brand) and `crm.schedule_greetings` (09:00 Dhaka, one `crm.greet_occasions` per brand), not `crm.rfm`/`crm.occasions`: queue names are `module.action`, and a fan-out plus a per-brand job keeps one brand's failure from stopping another's · Everything the CRM reads is the buyer and their orders, which `orders` owns; a separate module would have read `sales.*` through a port for every query or broken invariant 12. Codes and messages are still other modules', reached through `PROMO_PORT` and `NOTIFY_PORT` · architecture.md updated; revisit if the CRM grows tables of its own (campaign audiences, M18).
- 2026-10-02 · M16 · D-212 · Sign-in OTPs and invite emails do not go through the notify module yet: Better Auth's `SmsProvider` stays bound to `RecordingSmsProvider`, and invite tokens are still handed to the admin. M16 promised both earlier (M02a, D-012) · The only real SMS gateway is unverified (D-012 OPEN), and an OTP sent through an unverified gateway is a sign-in that may silently never arrive; the port also returns `void`, which the delivery log cannot use. No email adapter exists. Follow-up: when a gateway is verified, bind `SmsProvider` to an adapter over `SMS_GATEWAY` that records OTPs in the delivery log (category `otp`, never deferred), and add an email adapter for invites.
- 2026-10-01 · M05 · follow-up · `text-primary` as link text fails AA on the dark popover surface (found by the inbox's axe scan; the inbox now uses the foreground colour, underlined). Eight screens use the same class for links (transfers, branches, courier parcels/returns/settlements/stuck, payroll, run detail) and are not in check 6's scan, so they need checking on dark card and popover surfaces; a `--link` token tuned per theme, with a pair in the token contrast test, is the likely fix.
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-213 · A brand's opening balances post to their own `opening_equity` account (`3350 Opening balance equity`), not to `retained_earnings`, and the journal's equity line is a derived plug: every other line is given, and the difference is credited (or debited) to equity. Its idempotency key is `opening_balance:<org>:posted` — the org alone, with no date — so a brand opens its books exactly once · Retained earnings is what a business earned; an opening balance is what it was holding on the day it started recording here. Conflating them hides the plug inside a real figure, and an accountant signing the opening position has to see the plug to agree it and clear it. Keeping the date out of the key matters as much: with the date in it, re-running the import against a different evening would post a second opening journal and double the brand's stock and equity — balanced, and wrong. §7's account table gains the row; migration 0081 appends the enum value · **Amended at the end of M14a (ledger audit W1):** there is no re-opening. The opening row is permanent and balance files close once it is posted, so the post names the accountant who agreed the staged trial balance (`--approved-by`, recorded in the audit row), and a mistake found afterwards is corrected through the owning module — a stock count or approved adjustment, a wallet closing variance — not by a second opening. A wrong supplier due or courier receivable has no correcting path yet (D-088 allows no manual journal): a follow-up if the client needs one.
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-214 · A brand's data migration is run by the implementation team through a command, `pnpm migration <dry-run|commit|discard|status|post|reconcile|sign-off>`, and the training org through `pnpm demo:org` — not through a console screen or an HTTP API. The command boots `MigrationModule` as an application context (as the worker does), runs every write as `erp_app` inside the brand's `withOrg` transaction, records the actor as `system:migration`, and writes an audit row for every commit, discard, posting and sign-off. With no route there is no `DataMigration` permission subject; holding the deployment's `DATABASE_URL` is the authority, as it is for `pnpm db:seed` · The owner asked for M14a lean: it runs once per brand, five times in all, at an assisted go-live, and a six-tab screen with its i18n, route matrix and e2e would be built for five uses. The service is the whole of the logic, so a screen later is a thin edge over it · Revisit if brands are to migrate themselves; the plan's `/accounts/opening` screen and its permissions are the starting point.
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-215 · The four balance files (opening stock, supplier dues and advances, wallet balances, courier COD in transit) are **staged**: a commit records the parsed rows in `migration.batches` and writes nothing to stock or the ledger, and a staged file can be discarded and replaced. One `post` then writes every staged file's `opening_balance` movements and the one opening journal (D-213) in a single transaction, and balance files are closed from then on. SKUs and customers are not balances and apply on commit · D-213 opens a brand's books with exactly one journal, and an accountant has to see the whole opening trial balance — and fix a wrong file — before anything is posted. Stock committed straight into inventory could only be corrected by approved adjustments after the weighted average had blended it · —
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-216 · A SKU opens at one unit cost across every location and every staged stock file, the cost is above zero, and a SKU with any stock movement or cost basis is refused — at review, and again at posting · §8's average is org-wide, so two costs for one SKU blend and round, and check 4 asks the opening journal's Σ(qty × cost) and the inventory valuation's Σ(WAC × on hand) to agree to the poisha. One cost per SKU makes them agree exactly, and it is the true statement: a business has one carrying cost per item. A SKU that already holds stock is a receipt, not an opening · —
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-217 · Re-running an importer is idempotent at three levels: `(org_id, kind, content_sha256)` is unique among batches that are not discarded, so the same file twice is one import (the commit inserts the batch row first, `on conflict do nothing`, and answers with the first batch); an edited SKU or customers file skips every code or phone already present; and the opening journal's key is the org's (D-213), with movements deduplicated per opening, SKU and location. An edited balance file that repeats a staged row is refused, not merged · Check 3. Summing a repeated row would be a decision the operator did not know they made; refusing it names the file to discard · —
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-218 · The customers importer takes `phone, name` and optionally `delivered, returned, cancelled, spent_taka`. A phone that is already a buyer is skipped, never merged; a new buyer's own counters are set from the file (`orders_total` = delivered + returned + cancelled, `ltv_poisha` = spent). No dates are imported, and nothing about fraud (D-291) · The counters are only ever incremented by order transitions, so importing a buyer twice would double their record with no honest way back. Recency in the CRM comes from delivered orders, and a guessed first or last order date would be shown on the profile as a fact · Revisit if the client's exports carry reliable order dates.
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-219 · Imported SKUs keep the brand's own codes (they must be scannable codes, `isSkuCode`), rows group into products by name, products are created `active` with a deterministic slug (`importSlug`), and sizes and colours resolve against the brand's attributes by code or label — the importer never creates one. A code already in the catalog is skipped; a product half in the catalog is refused. Selling price only; a `cost` column is refused (invariant 5) · The codes are printed on labels already on the shelves and typed into courier sheets. An importer that invented `Sky Blue` beside `Sky-Blue` would make two values silently · —
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-220 · **ASSUMED.** Courier COD still in transit at go-live is imported per parcel (`courier, reference, amount_taka`) and opens as `courier_receivable` per courier party; the parcel references are kept in the staged rows. M12's settlement import does not yet clear a legacy reference against that receivable: a courier statement naming a pre-go-live parcel matches no consignment and must be applied by hand · The receivable is real money owed and must open with the books; matching legacy statements is M12's job and is logged as a follow-up · Before the pilot's first post-go-live courier statement.
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-221 · **ASSUMED.** The opening is dated the evening before go-live: today or yesterday in Asia/Dhaka, nothing older and nothing ahead. Opening stock movements are stamped 23:59:59 Dhaka on that date, or now if that is still ahead · §7 sends anything older than yesterday through a back-dated approval, and a future-dated opening would put stock and money into a day that has not happened. A brand that counted its stock on an earlier cut-off needs a decision, not a quiet back-date · Ask the client whether any brand needs a cut-off older than the day before go-live.
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-222 · The reconciliation report puts three figures side by side per line of the opening position — stock, each wallet, each supplier's payable and advance, each courier's receivable and the equity plug: **imported** (the posted files, through `openingPosition`), **journal** (the opening journal's lines read back through `LedgerApi.journalLines`) and **module** (the inventory module's own valuation, Σ WAC × on hand, and each account's and party's ledger balance as of the opening date). A line agrees only when all three are equal; a journal line no file accounted for is a row of its own. The sign-off is recorded once, naming the accountant, and only when every line agrees and the journal balances. `pnpm migration reconcile --out <file>` writes it as CSV for the accountant to sign. Stock is the inventory module's valuation _now_ while the other module figures are as of the opening date, so the report is run and signed after the post and before the brand trades; the signed copy is the record · Check 5 is a human check; the system's part is to make every disagreement visible and to record that the signature happened, so M14b's go-live checklist can ask · —
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-223 · The training org (`training`, `org.organizations.is_training = true`) is seeded by `pnpm demo:org`: its set-up (HQ, a warehouse and a showroom, the chart, three wallets, two suppliers, Steadfast and a size attribute) is a seed run as `erp_owner`, and the repository's synthetic sample files are anonymised and run through the real importers and the real opening post — never a real brand's export, because the money scale is a constant in the code and a brand's costs and dues would be readable by every trainee. The anonymiser replaces phones by position (skipping any number the file itself holds), names from a fixed list and courier references with `DEMO-<n>`, and scales money by 7/10 to whole poisha (zero stays zero). The consolidated wallet and P&L reads leave the training org out, and the notify module suppresses every message it queues · A fake derived from a real phone can be reversed by enumerating Bangladesh's mobile range; one derived from the row's position carries nothing. The demo's money arrives the way a brand's does, so a trainee learns the real screens on real-shaped data, and practice money never reaches a super admin's group totals · Its demo numbers are a real mobile range; the notify suppression is what keeps a trainee's practice template from texting a stranger.
- 2026-10-02 · M14a · D-224 · Purchase cost is kept in a fourth place, `migration.batches`: a staged opening-stock file's rows carry `unitCostPoisha` and its summary `stockValuePoisha` until, and after, the opening posts (invariant 5 names supply, inventory and ledger). - 2026-10-02 · M18 · D-250 · Attribution is resolved when it is read, not stored: the dashboard runs `resolveAttribution` (`packages/domain/src/marketing/attribution.ts`) over each order's own recorded `source`, its promo code's campaign and the brand's current mappings, in §11's order — explicit campaign (our id or a Meta campaign id), then the click-to-message ad id, then `utm_campaign` (trimmed, any case), then none. A step that names nothing the brand knows falls through to the next instead of stopping · A marketer who forgets to tag a campaign can add the mapping afterwards and the orders it describes move to it, with no back-fill job and no second copy of the answer on the order. One pure function is the only place that decides · Revisit when M24's rollups need a frozen answer per day; they can snapshot it then.
- 2026-10-02 · M18 · D-251 · A promo code made for a campaign is that campaign set explicitly on the order (rule 1), checked after the order's own campaign id and before the ad referral. - 2026-10-02 · M18 · D-252 · Ad spend is one figure per (campaign, Dhaka day). A person's entry replaces whatever the day holds, an imported figure included; the Meta import replaces only an earlier import of the same day and never a figure a person entered (the upsert's `where source = 'meta'`, and 0083's trigger refuses `manual → meta` and any change of campaign or day). A figure is never deleted; zero is a figure. A day's Meta figure lands on exactly one campaign: Meta campaigns mapped to the same campaign are added before rounding once (`spendPerCampaign`), an import zeroes any imported figure in its range that Meta's answer no longer supports (so after a Meta campaign id is moved to another campaign, the re-imported days move with it instead of being counted on both; days outside any re-import stay on the old campaign), and it refuses to run while spend typed by hand for a `meta` campaign with no Meta campaign id sits in its range, naming those campaigns (the import cannot tell whose spend that is, and would write it again under a campaign of its own). Imports of one brand take turns on the connection row's lock and write nothing if the ad account changed while Meta answered · M18 check 2 asks for an import that is idempotent per (campaign, date), and a correction typed by the marketing team must survive the next morning's import · Revisit if the client wants Meta to win.
- 2026-10-02 · M18 · D-253 · Ad spend figures post nothing to the ledger. Paying for ads is already a cash out with the `ad_spend` category (M08), which is the money; these rows are the per-campaign, per-day breakdown §11's ratios need · Posting both would count the same taka twice, and the timing differs (Meta bills in arrears, by card) · —
- 2026-10-02 · M18 · D-254 · **ASSUMED.** The Meta import reads ad-level Insights a day at a time (`level=ad`, `time_increment=1`) and sums each campaign's ads per day exactly, rounding once. A scheduled job at 06:00 Dhaka re-imports the last three days for every brand that turned it on, because Meta revises recent spend; a manual import takes any range up to 92 days. The spend day is the ad account's own day, assumed Asia/Dhaka for a Bangladeshi brand. A Meta campaign the brand has not mapped becomes a campaign of its own (origin `meta_import`, named as Meta names it), and every ad id seen is mapped to its campaign so click-to-message referrals attribute without anyone typing ad ids. To move a Meta campaign onto a campaign of the brand's own, move its `meta_campaign` id there and re-import the days it ran (D-252). Only BDT ad accounts are imported: any other currency fails the import and records why · The client's accounts are BDT; converting a USD account would need a rate source nobody has named · Revisit if a brand's ad account is not in BDT or not on Dhaka time.
- 2026-10-02 · M18 · D-255 · §11's metrics count each event in the period it happened in, by Asia/Dhaka date: an order is confirmed in the period of its confirmation, delivered in the period of its delivery (a partial delivery is a delivery, with its delivered part as revenue) and returned in the period it came back. Placed-order revenue is the net item value of the orders confirmed in the period. CPO and CPDO are poisha; ROAS, placed ROAS and return rate are basis points; all rounded half away from zero by the money module's one rule. A ratio is null only when its denominator is zero, so a period with orders and no spend has a CPO of 0 and no ROAS. The dashboard takes up to 92 days (`metrics.ts`) · "Orders that reached confirmed" and "delivered revenue" in a period are only well defined by the date the event happened; a cohort view (orders placed in the period, wherever they ended) is a different report · Revisit if the client wants cohorts.
- 2026-10-02 · M18 · D-256 · **ASSUMED.** The Conversions API event is one `Purchase` per delivered order: `event_id` = the order id, `event_time` = the delivery (a COD purchase completes at the door), `value` = delivered net item value in BDT, `contents` = SKU codes and delivered quantities. `action_source` follows the channel with values Meta documents: Facebook, Instagram and WhatsApp orders are `chat`, phone orders `phone_call`, walk-ins `physical_store`, everything else `other` — `website` needs an `event_source_url` no order records. `user_data` is the SHA-256 of the normalised phone, the country (`bd`) and the buyer id; first name, last name and city are added, hashed, only when the brand turns on "buyers consented", which is off by default. An order delivered more than seven days ago (less an hour in hand) is skipped, not sent, as Meta refuses the whole request otherwise. A test event code is per brand; an event sent while it is set is final like any other, so a brand clears the code before turning conversions on for real. If Meta accepts an event and recording that fails, the job retries and sends the same `event_id` again, which Meta de-duplicates; it is never logged as a failed send · §11's rules (event id, hashing, consent, BDT) plus Meta's own (event time, action source) · Revisit when the client says whether checkout records consent for name and city.
- 2026-10-02 · M18 · D-257 · Both Meta adapters (Insights and the Conversions API) are UNVERIFIED and off: the fake provider is the default everywhere, and `MARKETING_META_PROVIDER=graph` without `MARKETING_META_ENABLED=true` stops the process at boot, as D-012 and D-176 do for SMS and couriers. Each brand's system-user token is sealed with AES-256-GCM under `MARKETING_CREDENTIALS_KEY`, bound to the org, write-only, never logged, shown only as a 12-hex fingerprint. The token travels in the query string because Meta's pages show it there, so no URL is ever logged or put in an error, and Insights paging is followed only on the configured host · Built from Meta's documentation without credentials (see Unverified integrations) · Revisit at the pilot with a real token, pixel and test event code.

Segment reports group by the SKU's catalog product, by size (trimmed, upper-cased), by a closed list of age bands (`under_18`, `18_24`, `25_34`, `35_44`, `45_54`, `55_plus`) and by profession (case- and space-insensitive), each with leads, converted and conversion rate, "not stated" last; optionally over leads created in a Dhaka-date period. - 2026-10-02 · M17 · D-230 · An advance is a **claim** an agent records and accounts **verifies**. The agent (`update Order`, row scope) types the method (`bkash`, `nagad`, `bank`), the transaction id and the amount; nothing posts and the COD does not move. Accounts (`create Wallet`) verifies it into the wallet the money arrived in — never the person who recorded it, which a CHECK also refuses — and that posts Dr Wallet / Cr Customer advance keyed `customer_advance:<advance id>:received` and lowers the COD in the same transaction; or rejects it, which posts nothing. An order may have several advances; one transaction id backs one live claim per brand, whatever method it was typed under. `POST /v1/orders/:id/advance` (which posted on one person's word) and `advancePaidPoisha` on the order create body (which lowered the COD with no journal at all) are removed. This is D-013 as specified (manual; a gateway stays optional) and supersedes D-101 · M17 check 2: an unverified advance must never reach the books, and both removed paths let one reach them · A gateway, or per-claim screenshots, would be new rows and a decision.
- 2026-10-02 · M17 · D-231 · When a verification may lower the COD: only before the parcel moves (pending, confirmed, on hold, packed) and with no live consignment. With a booking live or the parcel on the road it is refused (`ADVANCE_PARCEL_IN_FLIGHT`) until the booking is cancelled or the order ends, because the courier holds the COD it was given at booking. On an order that already ended it is received and ended in one step: a cancel or a return by that ending's policy (D-232), a delivered or exchanged order as a refund (the rider collected the full COD, so the buyer paid twice). The courier booking reads the order `FOR UPDATE` (`bookingSnapshot(..., { forUpdate: true })` in `reserveConsignment`), so a booking and a verification serialise rather than race · The ledger audit's W1: a booking that read the COD before a verification committed would have sent the rider out with the old figure · Revisit if couriers accept a COD update after booking.
- 2026-10-02 · M17 · D-232 · **ASSUMED.** The advance's endings. `org.settings` keys `orders.advance_on_cancel` (default `refund`) and `orders.advance_on_return` (default `forfeit`), read with those defaults when unset; there is no settings screen yet. "Keep" (`forfeit`) keeps **at most the order's delivery charge** across all its advances, oldest first, and nothing once any unit was delivered (the delivery journal already earned the charge): Dr Customer advance / Cr Other income, dated with the ending. Everything else becomes `refund_due` — still a liability — and is posted Dr Customer advance / Cr Wallet only when accounts records that the money went back (`POST /v1/order-advances/:id/refund`, out of the wallet it came into unless another is named). A refund is one person's act (`create Wallet`); it is not on §6's maker-checker list · A cancel is usually the brand's doing and a refused parcel cost a courier trip; keeping more than the trip would keep a buyer's goods money on a full prepayment. Booking the refund at the cancel would have the wallet's books pay out money it still holds · Change the defaults per brand in `org.settings`; a screen and a maker-checker refund are follow-ups if the client wants them.
- 2026-10-02 · M17 · D-233 · Applied on delivery: the delivery journal already debits Customer advance by `applicableAdvance` over the order's advance column; `apply_advances` spreads that over the verified advances oldest first, **after** any exchange credit on the order (the credit has no other way out; a cash advance left over has a refund path). A partial delivery's remainder ends at the return that follows. Exchange credits, and M10-era advances recorded before M17 (an advance column with no advance rows), keep their old behaviour: no refund path of their own · The rows must agree with the journal, or a later ending would settle money the books already relieved · A store-credit wallet is its own feature.
- 2026-10-02 · M17 · D-234 · Return reasons are a fixed, translated list of eleven codes (`customer_refused`, `customer_unreachable`, `wrong_address`, `changed_mind`, `size_issue`, `quality_issue`, `wrong_item`, `damaged_in_transit`, `late_delivery`, `price_dispute`, `other`), required on every `receive_return` and every exchange: the domain machine refuses without one (`ORDER_RETURN_REASON_REQUIRED`), the desk's receive route, the courier returns screen and the exchange route all take `returnReason`, and the transition writes one row per order to `sales.order_returns` (kind, code, the free-text note, Dhaka business date, who), which nothing may change. A deferred constraint trigger refuses a status **update** into `returned` or `exchange` that has no row by commit; an **insert** at those statuses (a data import) is not covered. `GET /v1/orders/return-reasons?from&to` counts by reason and kind in the caller's order scope, with shares that sum to exactly 100%. Till returns (D-169) are out of scope · A brand-editable list would make the report incomparable across months and brands · A new code is a decision row and a migration.
- 2026-10-02 · M17 · D-235 · The grants: recording a claim is `update Order` at row scope; an order's claims are `read Order`; the accounts queue, verify, reject, refund and void are `create Wallet` (accounts and super_admin; org_admin holds no `create Wallet`, as D-099's note already says); the report is `read Order` in the caller's order scope · Verification is money arriving in a wallet and a journal against it, which is the wallets row, not an order grant · Move the grants with a §6 change.
- 2026-10-02 · M17 · D-236 · A mistaken verification is undone, not edited: `POST /v1/order-advances/:id/void` (`create Wallet`, with a reason, and never by the person who verified it: the service and a CHECK both refuse) reverses the received journal through `ledger.reverse`, restores the COD and marks the advance `voided`, freeing its transaction id. Only for a verified advance nothing has used, before the parcel moves, with no live booking; after that a mistake is settled through the order's ending · The ledger audit's W2: invariant 2 fixes a posted mistake with a reversal, and without this path a fake TrxID verified by mistake could only leave the books through a fake refund · Revisit if accounts needs to correct one after shipping.
- 2026-10-02 · M20 · D-270 · Targets, bonus rules and desk productivity live in the HR module and the `hr` schema (`targets.service.ts`, `bonus.service.ts`, `performance.controller.ts`), not in a module of their own · §3 gives `hr` "targets, bonus rules", and a bonus is an ordinary payroll input: a separate module would reach `hr.payroll_inputs` through a port on every apply and gain nothing. Progress needs what the agents' orders came to, which is M10's, so `OrdersApi` gains `agentResults` and `HrModule` imports `OrdersModule` for it alone (invariant 12) · Revisit if performance grows screens and rules beyond HR's.
- 2026-10-02 · M20 · D-271 · **ASSUMED.** A target's weeks start on Saturday, the day after the Friday weekend, and are clipped to the month, so the first and last week are usually short. The weekly split weights each week by its working days on the employee's own calendar (a team target uses the brand's default calendar), with largest remainder through the money module's `allocate`, ties to the earlier week; a month with no working day at all is split by calendar days rather than refused or lost · §12 says "weeks" and "working days" and nothing about where a week starts; a Sunday or Monday start would cut the Saturday-to-Thursday working week in two, and a week borrowed from the next month would count orders the month's target does not cover · Change `WEEK_STARTS_ON` in `packages/domain/src/targets/weeks.ts` if the client counts weeks differently.
- 2026-10-02 · M20 · D-272 · **ASSUMED.** An order counts for the agent who **confirmed** it (`sales.orders.confirmed_by`), never whoever holds it now. `confirmed_orders` counts by the Asia/Dhaka date of confirmation and leaves out an order cancelled after it was confirmed; `delivered_orders` and `delivered_revenue` count by the Dhaka date of delivery, full or partial; revenue is the net goods value of the delivered units through `computePartialDelivery`, the function the delivery journal uses — no delivery charge, never a cost · Confirming is the desk's work and the one act with a recorded author; counting a confirm-then-cancel would pay for gaming the metric, and a revenue figure that disagreed with the books would be argued about every month. **For the client:** `confirmed_orders` still counts an order confirmed and later returned, and both halves of an exchange, so a fake COD order that ships and comes back still counts (ledger audit N5); `delivered_orders` is the metric that cannot be gamed that way · Revisit when the client says whose target a reassigned or exchanged order belongs to, and whether returned orders should un-count.

A rule applies to every active employee target on its metric in the payroll run's month, and two approved rules on one metric both pay · §12 allows team targets and says nothing about how a team bonus is shared (equally, by contribution, to a lead), and any guess would be a payroll figure the client did not choose · Add a sharing rule to the DSL when the client says.
- 2026-10-02 · M20 · D-274 · The bonus DSL's meaning (`packages/domain/src/targets/bonus.ts`): tiers are **cumulative** — every tier whose threshold is met pays; a threshold is compared exactly in integers (`achieved × 100 ≥ gte_pct × target`); three tier kinds — a flat `amount_poisha`, `per_unit_above_target_poisha` for the two count metrics (per order above the target, not above the tier) and `bps_of_excess` for revenue; an optional `cap_poisha`; the parser is strict and refuses an unknown key · Read as "highest tier only", §12's own example pays less at 121% than at 119%; a rounded display percentage would let 99.995% count as 100%; and a misspelt key silently dropped would be a tier that pays nothing under a rule a checker approved · —
- 2026-10-02 · M20 · D-275 · A bonus rule is versioned: every edit is a new version whose JSON never changes (0083), and submitting raises a `bonus_rule` request (never auto-approved, D-071). Only the version's **author** may submit it, so the approvals module's requester-is-not-checker rule keeps the author from approving their own terms; the executor refuses an author-approver as well, and 0083 refuses a version going into force unless its own request is approved by somebody other than its author (M20 ledger audit W1, W6). The executor activates **only the version whose `approval_request_id` is that request**, superseding the one before — and removing the superseded version's bonuses from draft runs, as cancelling a target removes that target's (W4) — so a `bonus_rule` raised by hand through `POST /v1/approvals` activates nothing. A version whose request was rejected or cancelled is closed as `rejected` at the rule's next submit, because the approvals module runs nothing on a rejection (the same gap M19's payroll run has). Approved versions reach pay only through `POST /v1/hr/payroll/runs/:id/bonuses` on a **draft** run, which replaces the rule-made inputs, leaves hand-typed ones alone, and awards nobody the run will not pay (no salary structure that month), listing them as skipped instead (W3); the run's own maker-checker then approves the amounts, and 0055 and 0083 refuse any change once it is submitted · Two approvals — the rule's and the run's — because they answer different questions: whether these terms are the brand's policy, and whether this month's payroll is right · Replace the close-at-next-submit with a rejection hook when the approvals module has one.
- 2026-10-02 · M20 · D-276 · Productivity is computed from order events (`hr.desk_events`, written from the outbox) by `packages/domain/src/targets/productivity.ts`. Pickup: an assignee's clock starts at creation or at their assignment; a claim from the queue counts from creation; reassignment restarts the clock for the new assignee and gives the previous one no sample; an action by somebody else stops the assignee's clock with no sample; an order an agent created for themselves is not a pickup. Median and p90 are nearest-rank. An **active hour is an Asia/Dhaka clock hour with at least one desk action** (created, claimed, confirmed or cancelled) — for good, now the activity extension is out of scope (D-292). The report lists employees with a login · A self-entered order confirmed thirty seconds later is good desk work but not a pickup, and counting it would flatter the one figure that measures how long the queue waits · Revisit if the client wants idle time inside an hour measured, which would need a heartbeat this system does not have.
- 2026-10-02 · M20 · D-277 · `events.outbox` records who acted (`actor_user_id`, migration 0081), read from the emitting transaction's `app.user_id` rather than passed by each emit site: a person's login, a worker's system id (`usr_dispatcher`, `usr_targets_refresh`, …), or null for a public caller with no login (corrected after the M20 ledger audit, which found this row saying null for a worker) · §3 says every event carries its actor, and pickup time is the first reader that needs it: a supervisor confirming an agent's order is not the agent's pickup. Read from the transaction, no emitter can forget it or name somebody else · —

- 2026-10-02 · M20 · D-279 · Target progress is refreshed by the worker: the outbox subscriber enqueues `hr.refresh_target_progress` for any status change that can move a target (confirmed, delivered, partially delivered, returned, cancelled), keyed by org and ten-second bucket on an `exclusive` queue and started a second after the bucket closes, so a burst is one recompute that starts only after the burst's last event committed. The job recomputes this and last month's active targets and emits `target.progress` only for a target whose achieved figure changed. A new target computes at once, and `POST /v1/hr/targets/refresh` forces it · §19 asks for at most one event per second per key, and recomputing on every confirmation would be one rollup per order · —
- 2026-10-02 · M20 · D-280 · A target is set for the current Asia/Dhaka month or a later one, never for a month that has ended; a payroll run's submit recomputes its payslips under the run's lock and refuses if any bonus or deduction changed since it first computed them, and freezes what it recomputed; the draft-only guards on payroll inputs and bonus awards read the run `for share`, so a write that races a submit waits for it and is refused; a target that a run awaiting approval pays cannot be cancelled until the checker rejects the run, and a cancel locks the month's runs before it checks; and a submit refuses a run carrying a bonus whose target is cancelled or whose rule version is no longer in force. Every writer takes the run before its inputs · From the M20 ledger audit (W2, W5), its re-check (W1, W2) and the confirmation pass (W-1, N-3, N-6). A target set after its month's results are known is a bonus amount chosen by whoever types the target value, and the target, unlike the rule, has no checker (W5). And M20's apply rewrites every rule-made bonus at once, which made M19's window between computing and freezing a run's payslips worth closing: a bonus written in between would be on the run and not on the frozen payslip (W2). The re-check found two ways around that: an insert or delete that never locked the run could land between the recompute and the commit, and a cancelled target's bonus would still be approved on a waiting run's frozen payslip · A target can still be set a few days into its own month; if the client wants that closed too, targets would need their own approval.
- 2026-10-02 · M25 · D-310 · The investor's own pages are in the portal at `/investor` (every portal path but `/track/*` lands there), Bangla first with an English toggle, light and dark. The page shows the investor's position, contributions with their lock-in, the profit declared to them, their withdrawals with each status, their statement and their documents; distributions are read from their own statement's declared entries, because `/v1/investors/distributions` is the org's record and refuses an `own` caller. Supersedes D-120 for investors · §15 puts the investor portal in `apps/portal`, and D-120's reason (the portal was a stub) no longer holds; an investor is a partner, so D-036 makes the page Bangla first · The console's investor screens stay for staff, and an investor signing into the console still sees only their own row.
- 2026-10-02 · M25 · D-311 · The portal shows one brand at a time. The session (one cookie, one active org) is shared with the console, so the portal moves it only when nothing has chosen a brand yet (it then opens the person's first investor brand) or on a click: if the session is open in a brand where the person is staff, the portal asks them to choose rather than moving the console's brand under an open tab. A switcher (shown with two or more brands, or whenever a choice is needed) changes it through `/v1/me/active-org`. The API's answers do not name the brand, so the order is fixed: the dashboard is unmounted, every query of the brand being left is cancelled and dropped, and only then does the session move; and the figures are not refetched on window focus (the session is), so a brand changed in another tab remounts the dashboard under the new brand rather than refreshing the old brand's figures with the new brand's. Every figure is cached under the signed-in user as well as the brand and dropped once the session is not theirs, so on a shared browser the next person never sees the last one's figures. Capital per brand is that brand's position; there is no cross-brand total · Each brand is an isolated org and a session carries one active org (§5, §6); one request reading several brands' investor rows is a cross-org read, which only the analytics module may make (invariant 4) · A consolidated figure, if investors ask for one, belongs in the analytics module.

- 2026-10-02 · M24 · D-320 · The rollups (`analytics.daily_org`, `daily_branch`, `daily_channel`, `daily_campaign`, `daily_agent`) are **rebuilt, never incremented**: `RollupService.recompute` reads an org's days from the owning modules' interfaces (`OrdersApi.analyticsFacts` and `agentResults`, `MarketingApi.campaignDays`, `HrApi.pickups`), then deletes and inserts those days in one `withOrg()` transaction under a per-org advisory lock. It runs from three places: the worker's outbox subscriber on `order.created` and `order.status_changed` enqueues `analytics.rollup` for the event's Dhaka day through today (two days when an event from just before midnight is dispatched after it), keyed by org and ten-second bucket and started after the bucket closes (D-279's shape); `analytics.schedule_rollup` at 03:00 Dhaka enqueues the last seven days per active brand; and `POST /v1/analytics/rollups/refresh` (`update Dashboard`) recomputes up to 92 days inline, audited. A spend correction, a late courier status and a changed campaign mapping (D-250) reach the rollups through the nightly run or a refresh · An incremented counter is only as right as every event that ever reached it, and attribution is read at read time, so an order can move between campaigns after the fact; a delete-and-insert of the whole day makes a rerun, a duplicate job and a retry all leave the same rows, which is what check 1 tests · Widen `NIGHTLY_DAYS` in `analytics.queues.ts`, or emit an event from spend entry if a same-hour spend figure is wanted.
- 2026-10-02 · M24 · D-321 · What a rollup row counts, each on its own Asia/Dhaka day (as D-255): placed = created; confirmed = confirmed (an order cancelled later still counts on the day it was confirmed); delivered = delivered in full or in part, with delivered net item value as revenue (`deliveredNetItems`, the delivery journal's figure); returned = returned; **cancelled = cancelled after it had been confirmed** (an unconfirmed cancel is a lead that did not convert, not a failed delivery). Gross profit = delivered revenue − the dispatch cost of the delivered units (`dispatch_unit_cost_poisha × delivered_qty`), stored only as the difference; purchase cost leaves the orders module only to be subtracted (invariant 5). **Outside Dhaka** is a ship-to district other than Dhaka district (district 47 in the vendored geography), not a brand's delivery zones. Spend is Σ ad spend for the day. Pickups are M20's desk pickups (D-276), counted on the Dhaka day of the pickup. Branch rows cover orders a branch took; channel rows every order; campaign rows M18's inputs with attribution as the marketing dashboard resolves it (`readAttributedPeriod`, shared, so the two cannot differ), a null campaign being the unattributed orders. Agent rows follow D-272 (the confirming agent; an order cancelled after confirming counts for nobody), so an org's confirmed figure can exceed the sum of its agents' · §18 names the tables and the dashboard's figures but not their period membership, and §11's rule (D-255) is the one the marketing dashboard already uses · Change `contributionOn` in `packages/domain/src/analytics/rollup.ts` and the orders query beside it, then recompute.
- 2026-10-02 · M24 · D-322 · The leaderboard compares, per brand per Asia/Dhaka month: revenue growth on the month before (bps), delivery success = delivered ÷ (delivered + returned + cancelled after confirming), return rate = returned ÷ (delivered + returned), delivered ROAS, CPDO (poisha), average-order growth (from the exact fractions, not two rounded AOVs), and pickup time as the **mean** desk pickup in seconds. **Response time is dropped**: it was the Message Hub's figure, removed with Chatwoot (D-290). Each metric is ranked by competition ranking ("1224"; a brand with nothing to divide by is unranked); a rank scores `10,000 × (n − rank) ÷ (n − 1)` bps over the `n` brands ranked on it (a lone brand 10,000, an unranked one 0); the composite is the weighted mean of the scores over the metrics in play; brands are ranked by composite. Training brands are left out, as the consolidated wallet total leaves them out (M08) · Brands differ tenfold in size, so only rates, growth and per-order figures compare them; rank-based scores keep one outlier month from deciding the board; a month's median pickup cannot be summed from daily rows, a mean can · Change `rankBoard`/`normalizedMetrics` in `packages/domain/src/analytics/leaderboard.ts`; a median pickup would need the samples stored per month.

- 2026-10-02 · M26 · D-293 · A lender is a per-brand row in `loans.lenders` (a name and a kind: `bank`, `person` or `other`), and its id is the party on every `loan_payable` line (§7's party type `lender`). A lender is renamed, never deleted (0095 refuses the DELETE for every role) · The journal names the party, so "what do we owe Sonali Bank" is a party balance; the same bank lending to two brands is two rows, because each brand is its own org (§5) · —
- 2026-10-02 · M26 · D-294 · **ASSUMED.** Schedules (`packages/domain/src/loans/schedule.ts`): monthly instalments only, 1 to 360; a nominal annual rate in whole basis points, 0 to 10,000, so the monthly rate is `rate / 120,000` with no day count. **Flat**: interest = principal × rate × months / 12, rounded once; principal and interest each spread by `allocate` (largest remainder). **Reducing** (D-020's default): the equal instalment `P·r·(1+r)^n / ((1+r)^n − 1)` as an exact bigint ratio rounded once; each month's interest = the balance × r, rounded half away from zero; principal = instalment − interest; the last instalment takes whatever principal remains, so Σ principal = the loan exactly. A zero rate splits the principal evenly. Due dates are the first due date plus k months from its own day, clamped to month ends (31 Jan → 28/29 Feb → 31 Mar). Terms that would leave an instalment of nothing to pay (a tiny principal over a long, dear term) are refused with `LOAN_TERMS_INVALID` · This is how a bank's own amortisation table is built, so a brand's table and its bank's letter agree to the poisha (M26 check 2) · Change `loanSchedule`; existing loans keep the schedule they were written with.
- 2026-10-02 · M26 · D-295 · Two posting rules (`packages/domain/src/posting/company-loan.ts`): received = Dr W / Cr Loan payable (lender), keyed `company_loan:<loan id>:received`; instalment paid = Dr Loan payable (lender, principal) + Dr Interest expense / Cr W, keyed `loan_instalment:<instalment id>:paid` — the instalment's key, so no second payment row can ever post a second journal for it. The debt and its interest carry no branch; the wallet line carries its own. **ASSUMED:** interest is expensed when the instalment is paid, not accrued month by month · §7's rows as written; accrual would need a monthly job and an `interest_payable` subtype §7 does not have · Add an accrual rule and subtype with a §7 change.
- 2026-10-02 · M26 · D-296 · Recording a loan posts its receipt at once, with no approval — money in, like investor capital — dated by the server's clock in Asia/Dhaka, and writes the schedule in the same transaction. The first due date must fall after that day (default: one month later). There is no back-dating, so a loan taken before go-live cannot be entered without double-counting its cash; M14a has no loan importer · Refusing to admit the bank paid the principal recovers nothing, and invariant 8 dates the receipt · Owner's decision: an opening-balance importer for existing loans (follow-up).
- 2026-10-02 · M26 · D-297 · **ASSUMED.** A repayment pays one whole instalment, as scheduled, and only the lowest instalment without a posted payment, and only while no repayment of that loan waits for a checker (`payableInstalment`). One live payment per instalment is a partial unique index; a trigger (0095) refuses a payment whose principal or interest differs from its instalment's. The journal is dated the day the payment was recorded, as a supplier payment's is, so a repayment still waiting when its month closes cannot be approved (`PERIOD_CLOSED`): the checker rejects it and the maker pays again. No partial payment, prepayment, early settlement, penalty or rescheduling · Banks collect the scheduled instalment, and one in flight at a time means a rejected repayment can never leave a later instalment paid ahead of it · Each of the missing cases is a follow-up with its own rule.
- 2026-10-02 · M26 · D-298 · A new approval type, `loan_repayment`, for §6's "payments": raised and checked by org_admin and accounts (`REQUESTER_TYPES`, `CHECKER_TYPES`), threshold-governed like a supplier payment — under the brand's `loan_repayment` threshold the repayment posts at once with no request; at or above it, or with no threshold set, it is written `pending_approval` with no journal and the executor posts it inside the deciding transaction. The amount is the instalment's, so the type is in `SERVER_DERIVED_AMOUNT_TYPES` (the loans module raises it with `amountDerivedByServer`) and in `MODULE_RAISED_TYPES` (the generic route refuses it). The executor refuses unless the payment names this approval and the approval's amount is the payment's (D-078) — and refuses, rather than returning quietly, when the request names no repayment waiting on it, so such a request (left by a lost race) can only be rejected; the type is in `EXECUTOR_REQUIRED_TYPES`, so a process without the loans module cannot decide it (ledger audit W1, after the build). Migration 0094 adds the enum value · `.claude/rules`: money-moving actions go through approvals whenever the org's threshold requires it; §6's maker-checker list now names company loan repayments · Set a threshold per brand in Approvals.
- 2026-10-02 · M26 · D-299 · `ApprovalsService.registerRejectionHandler(type, handler)`: what a rejected request releases, run inside the deciding transaction, like an executor on approval. The loans module marks the repayment `rejected`, and the instalment can be paid again with a new payment id. M25's withdrawals keep their own reject route · Without it a rejected repayment would wait forever and block every later instalment (the M25 follow-up "a rejection hook in the approvals module") · Other modules may adopt it.
- 2026-10-02 · M26 · D-300 · **ASSUMED.** Reminders: each loan has `reminder_days` (default 3, 0 to 30) and an optional SMS number. `loans.schedule_reminders` runs daily at 10:00 Dhaka (§20) and enqueues one `loans.remind_instalments` per brand (exclusive, one per brand per day). An instalment is reminded once, from its due date less N days onwards — so a run that missed days still reminds once, even after the due date — and never while a payment for it is waiting or posted. "Once" is a unique row per instalment in `loans.reminders`, written in the same transaction as the SMS row and the event, so a racing run rolls all three back. In-app: a `loan.instalment_due` event to `org:role:accounts` and `org:role:org_admin`, shown in the console's notification inbox. SMS: the brand's `loan.instalment_due` template (transactional, Bangla; values `lender`, `seq`, `due`, `amount`) to the loan's number — the brand's own staff number, stored E.164, masked in every response and log. With no number or no template the in-app reminder still goes and the row records `no_phone` or `failed`. No external call happens in the job's transaction: `notify.send` sends after commit · §15 asks for in-app and SMS, exactly once per instalment; the lender is not texted, because it is the brand that has to pay · Change `reminder_days` per loan; the number and template are data.

- 2026-10-02 · M26 · D-304 · A loan recorded by mistake is **voided**, not edited: `POST /v1/loans/:id/void` (`update CompanyLoan`, with a reason) reverses its receipt journal through `ledger.reverse` and sets `voided_at`, `voided_by`, `void_reason` and `void_journal_id` once, in one transaction — only while no repayment is posted or waiting, and only by someone other than the person who recorded the loan (`LOAN_SELF_VOID`, 403: the reversal takes the whole principal out of a wallet, so it needs a second person, as M17's advances do under D-236). 0096's trigger, replaced by 0097's, allows exactly that change (the four columns, once, by another person, naming the journal that reverses the receipt, with no live payment) and nothing else; erp_app gains UPDATE on `loans.loans` for it. A void and a repayment cannot both commit: a payment's insert reads its loan `for share` (a foreign key's `for key share` does not conflict with the void's row lock), and moving a waiting payment to posted checks the loan the same way and in the executor. 0097 is a new migration rather than an edit of 0096, although 0094 and 0096 were never on main: they were committed and applied by every test database since, and invariant 9 is cheaper to keep than to argue. A void loan takes no repayment, is never reminded, and drops out of the lender's outstanding. The statement (D-301) carries `loan.voidable` for the viewer: not void, nothing repaid or waiting, and not the person who recorded it; the console shows Void only then. 0096 (0097 for the case of the id) also ties a repayment to its approval and journal in the database: born waiting, it names a pending `loan_repayment` request for itself and its amount (its `paymentId` compared lowercased, as Postgres writes a uuid); posted, its journal is its own instalment's; it moves to posted only on an approved request and to rejected only on a rejected one; and receipt and repayment journals are each named by one row (unique indexes, 0094) · The ledger audit's W2, W3 and N4, and the re-audit's W-A, W-B and N2: the receipt has no checker, so it needs a correction path (as M17's D-236 gave advances), and maker-checker for money leaving the company should not rest on application code alone · Reversing a posted repayment remains a follow-up.
- 2026-10-02 · gaps · D-390 · **A courier status that proves the parcel left ships the order.** `courierTriggersFor` (packages/domain) returns the handover (`ship`, from `confirmed` or `packed`) for `picked_up`, `in_transit`, `out_for_delivery`, `delivered`, `partial_delivered`, `return_in_transit` and `returned_to_merchant`, then whatever the same status justifies from `shipped` (so `delivered` on a packed order is `ship` then `deliver`), at most two steps, each applied through `OrdersApi.applyTrigger` and the machine in the webhook's or poll's transaction; the handover journal posts before the delivery journal relieves it. `booked`, `pickup_pending`, `hold` (Steadfast's `in_review`), `cancelled` and `unknown` still move nothing before shipment; an `on_hold` order is never moved by a courier. The consignment event records the last trigger and where the sequence landed (so a two-step event reads `packed --deliver--> delivered`, a pair not in §9's table; the order's own history holds both rows). The parcel's collection and delivery are written only when a delivery step itself applied, and its return only when the return step did (ledger audit C1: a figureless partial on a packed order ships it and records nothing). A person's hand-set status that would move the order is refused outside today or yesterday (`APPROVAL_REQUIRED`) and while the parcel's booking is still queued (`CONSIGNMENT_INVALID`: the booking job would otherwise book a rider for a box already delivered), both decided on the same triggers that are then applied; its `differenceReason` reaches the delivery journal's Sales discounts memo, as on the order route; a courier's webhook or poll keeps the courier's clock, and a ship step it dates in a closed month raises `PERIOD_CLOSED` and the job retries, as a delivery for a shipped order always did · Before this nothing produced `ship` but a button the console did not have, so an order stayed `packed` while its parcel was delivered, the delivery was recorded and ignored, and no revenue posted · Edit `LEFT_THE_MERCHANT` in `packages/domain/src/courier/status.ts`.

- 2026-10-02 · preview kit · D-381 · Guards and secrets. Passwords are random per run (four groups of five, no 0/O/1/l/I), written only to `preview-credentials.local.md` (gitignored) or, with `--out -`, to stdout with the progress on stderr; output names the file and never a password. The seed writes only to a database whose comment is `elysium-preview` (`preview/guard.ts`): `preview:local` and the smoke run set it when they create their database, the preview server sets it once by hand, and nothing in the environment stands in for it. It reads the database's name, oid and the server's start time through both of its connections and refuses if `DATABASE_URL` and `DATABASE_OWNER_URL` reach different databases, because it writes through both. It refuses a database where any brand's audit log holds a change by someone outside `@preview.elysium.test`, with no override, because the marker is permanent and a preview later used for real work would still carry it (ledger re-audit W1; the runbook says to unmark a database before real use). It refuses a database where anyone outside the preview can sign in (`--allow-existing-users` overrides; the marker and the audit-log check come first, so it skips neither). Refusals name the runbook, not the command that would silence them. In its own process it pins `NOTIFY_SMS_PROVIDER` and `MARKETING_META_PROVIDER` to `fake`, so a gateway enabled on the server never texts a demo number. Seed robots lose their memberships with their accounts. Under `--out -` the application's logs go to stderr (`LOG_DESTINATION`), so no log line lands in the credentials file, which is `chmod 600` even when it already existed. Messages are phone-masked · Invariant 10, and the one mistake that matters: pointing it at a brand's real data. The ledger audit (2026-10-02) found the first guard, a localhost check plus `PREVIEW_SEED_CONFIRM=preview`, too weak: the variable could be pasted with the command, and only the app connection was checked · Loosen a guard in `preview/guard.ts` with a decision row.
