- 2026-10-02 · preview kit · D-389 · Found by the seed and fixed. (1) `PUT /v1/hr/attendance` with a check-in or check-out time answered 500. Instants are now bound as ISO text cast to `timestamptz` and read back as text, because Drizzle's postgres-js driver leaves `timestamptz` unparsed in both directions. The import path bound the same `Date` and failed the same way, now covered too. (2) The local storage driver's bytes `PUT` logged "a mutation escaped the audit log" on every upload. It and the tracking `POST` (a read sent as a POST) now carry `@AuditExempt(reason)`, metadata on the handler that the audit interceptor reads, replacing a run-time flag either could set on any request; `audit-exempt.test.ts` lists every exempt route, so a new one needs review. (3) That bytes `PUT` now takes bytes only for a `pending` file. A ticket outlives its finalize by minutes, and a `PUT` in that window replaced a stored document's bytes, unaudited, after its hash and sniffed type were recorded; it now answers 409. This covers the local driver only (the preview server's). An R2 presigned `PUT` goes straight to R2 and can still replace a stored object within its 900 s life; that needs a copy to a final key on finalize or a conditional `PUT`, a follow-up. An exemption only silences the interceptor's no-org error: a request with an org is audited whatever the route declares. Till barcodes are `200000000PPSS` (product, size), set only on a SKU that has none, so a code means the same item on every run · Both were shipped bugs a person would meet on the preview · Tests: `hr.e2e.test.ts` ("keeps the check-in and check-out times", "keeps the times an attendance file brings in"), `files.e2e.test.ts` ("takes bytes for a pending upload only"), `audit-exempt.test.ts`, `preview/setup.test.ts`, `preview/guard.test.ts`.

- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-400 · **Staff screen and roles.** `GET /v1/users` (`read User`) lists everyone who holds or held a role in the brand, with status (active, invited, deactivated, disabled), two-step state, last sign-in and open resets; `PUT /v1/users/:id/roles` (`update User`) replaces a person's roles and branch scope in the brand. A role granted or reshaped names the acting admin as its granter (D-077); a role kept exactly as it was keeps its granter; a role no longer wanted is switched off (`is_active = false`), never deleted. Nobody changes their own roles (`USER_SELF_MANAGEMENT`), only a super admin changes a super admin (`USER_PROTECTED`), a branch manager needs a branch, and a branch of another brand is a 400. Changes apply on the next request, because the context is rebuilt from memberships every time · The owner needs a human to run the ERP before it is hosted, and `POST /v1/users` alone could only add people · —
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-401 · **Deactivation, and who may hold a session.** `POST /v1/users/:id/deactivate` switches off every role the person holds in the brand, cancels open resets they are the subject or requester of (withdrawing the approvals behind them, D-409), and ends their sessions: every session, unused link and remembered device when this was the last brand they work in, otherwise only the sessions acting in this brand. Nothing they wrote is touched. Better Auth's `session.create.before` hook now refuses a session (`403 ACCOUNT_INACTIVE`) to an account that is disabled (`iam.users.is_active = false`) or holds no active role in any active brand; super admins, who hold no membership, are exempt. It runs after the password check, so it tells nobody without the password anything. Unlike the enrolment wall (D-051) this refuses the session itself, because nothing a deactivated account could do with one is legitimate · Without it a deactivated leaver could sign in again and hold a live credential that enrols a second factor and calls every `/api/auth` endpoint · Reactivate by giving a role through the roles route
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-402 · ASSUMED · **Password links without email.** `POST /v1/users/:id/password-link` (`update User`, with a reason) hands the admin a one-time link to pass on (D-212 still has no email adapter): for someone who never set a password, an invite sent again, valid **72 hours**; otherwise a reset valid **1 hour**, which voids the old password and ends every session at once. `POST /v1/users`'s invite also lasts 72 hours now (it lasted Better Auth's default hour). A new link voids every earlier one; redeeming a link ends every session again (`revokeSessionsOnPasswordReset`). The token rides in the URL fragment of `/accept-invite#token=…` or `/reset-password#token=…`, which no browser sends to a server, and the console removes it from the address bar once read. Better Auth's public `/request-password-reset` path is disabled (`disabledPaths`), and so is the phone-number plugin's reset pair (`/phone-number/request-password-reset`, `/phone-number/reset-password`: an OTP reset with no audit, limit or second admin, latent until SMS is wired). Every link is minted inside the admin action's own transaction: 32 random bytes written as the `iam.verifications` row (`reset-password:<token>`, value the user id) that Better Auth's `/reset-password` consumes, after voiding every earlier one, so the link, the reset row and the audit entry commit together or not at all and two concurrent mints cannot cross (the ledger audit's W6). A response carrying a link is `Cache-Control: no-store` and never stored for idempotent replay (`@NoIdempotencyReplay`). Limits, per brand, counted from `iam.credential_resets` so every replica agrees: an admin may ask for **20 resets an hour**, one person may be reset **5 times a day** (`429 RATE_LIMITED`). The audit log never holds a token (`redact` covers `token`) · An admin-held link is how invites already worked (D-057); a short reset window limits what a link left in a chat history is worth; the public path let anyone mint reset rows for any address · Change the lifetimes in `packages/db/src/auth` (`INVITE_LINK_SECONDS`, `PASSWORD_LINK_SECONDS`) and the limits in `packages/domain/src/iam` (`RESET_LIMITS`); emailing links waits for M16's email adapter
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-403 · **Second-factor reset after a lost phone.** `POST /v1/users/:id/two-factor/reset` (`update User`, with a reason) deletes the person's `iam.two_factors` row, sets `two_factor_enabled = false`, ends every session and deletes remembered devices and pending second-factor challenges. The next sign-in has no factor to ask for; a role that requires one meets the enrolment wall (§6) and enrols again with the password. Nobody resets their own (an admin could otherwise remove the factor §6 requires of them unseen); resetting a factor that does not exist is `409 CREDENTIAL_NOTHING_TO_RESET` · Recovery codes cover a lost phone only for someone who kept them · —
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-404 · **A sign-in reset of anyone whose account can move money needs a second admin.** Whoever holds a person's sign-in can act as them, so resetting the password or second factor of someone who holds, **or ever held**, a role in the brand that can raise an approval (`REQUESTER_TYPES`) or decide one (`CHECKER_TYPES`), or the investor role — `SECOND_ADMIN_RESET_ROLES`: org_admin, accounts, hr, branch_manager, warehouse and investor today; sales, marketing and reseller may be reset by one admin — is itself maker-checker. "Ever held", because otherwise an admin demotes an accountant, resets them alone and promotes them back (the ledger audit's C1); "raise", because otherwise an admin acts as a warehouse hand to raise a write-off and approves it as themselves (W1). It runs through a new approval type `credential_reset` (migration 0098), with no amount, never auto-approved (in `NEVER_AUTO_APPROVE_TYPES` and both database CHECKs), raised only by the iam module (`MODULE_RAISED_TYPES`), useless without its executor (`EXECUTOR_REQUIRED_TYPES`) and decided by org_admin only. The requester cannot decide it (maker ≠ checker), nor can the person being reset (the executor and 0099's trigger), nor anyone in the requester's grant lineage (D-077). An approved second-factor reset takes effect in the approving transaction; an approved password reset waits for the admin who asked to collect its link through the same route within **24 hours** (ASSUMED), after which it expires. A super admin acts alone, and a super admin's sign-in is a super admin's to reset. The executor asks the rules again with the requester as the actor, so an approval cannot take effect on someone who has since left or joined another brand (W4). Every action takes an advisory lock on the person (one per person, across brands, because what it protects is global) and reads its facts inside the transaction that acts on them; no path takes an approval row after that lock — a deactivation withdraws the approvals first, in a fixed order, and answers a reset raised in the moment between with a 409 to retry — so a decision (approval row, then person) cannot deadlock with it (W4, W5; the re-audit's W1, W2). The executor also refuses when the admin who asked no longer holds org_admin in the brand. Consequence: in a brand whose second admin was granted by the first, a reset asked by the first needs a third admin or a super admin · Otherwise one admin could reset an accountant's password, sign in with the link, and approve their own payment · Change who counts by changing `REQUESTER_TYPES` or `CHECKER_TYPES`; the 24 hours is `COLLECT_WINDOW_SECONDS`
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-405 · **Cross-brand people are a super admin's to reset.** A person with an active role in another brand is reset (password or second factor) only by a super admin (`USER_PROTECTED` otherwise), because their password opens every brand they work in and one brand's admin must not be able to sign in as a colleague of another. Roles and deactivation in a brand stay with that brand's admin. Whether someone works elsewhere is read through their own lens (`withUser` with their id, D-050), the acting admin's org context being unable to see another brand's rows; the staff list shows it as a flag only, never which brand · — · —
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-406 · **Whoever holds a link joins the lineage.** Issuing or collecting a password link — an invite or a reset — adds the acting admin to the person's grant lineage in the brand (`iam.membership_granters`, D-410), extending D-077: the admin could have set the password themselves, so the person must never approve that admin's requests. An invite sent again for someone who never set a password needs no approval: it gives the admin nothing they could not get by inviting a new person, which D-077's W4 risk already covers until links are emailed. "Has a password" means a credential account exists, even one whose password a reset voided, so a reset left to expire cannot be re-issued as an invite to dodge the second admin · — · —
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-407 · **`iam.credential_resets`** is the record of every sign-in reset asked for: a tenant table (RLS, ENABLE and FORCE), SELECT, INSERT and UPDATE for `erp_app` and never DELETE (`NO_DELETE_TABLES`), one open reset per person and kind (partial unique index). 0099's trigger holds `canMoveReset`: a reset is born waiting (with its approval) or completed; waiting goes to approved, completed, rejected or cancelled; approved to completed, cancelled or expired; the rest are final; its terms never change; and a decider is neither its requester nor its subject, and is the acting user · The limits count these rows, so removing one would also hand back allowance · —
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-408 · **`iam.users.last_sign_in_at`**, stamped by the auth bridge after a sign-in finishes — a password sign-in with no factor owed, a verified code or a recovery code — and never after the first factor alone · The staff screen should say whether an account is still used without reading the audit log · —
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-410 · **The grant lineage keeps every granter.** `iam.membership_granters` (migrations 0100, 0101) records everyone who granted a person a role in a brand or held a link that set their password, append-only (INSERT and SELECT, `APPEND_ONLY_TABLES`), backfilled from `iam.memberships.granted_by`. The approvals lineage (D-077) reads both, so a re-grant or a reset by a second admin no longer drops the first granter from it (the ledger audit's C2 and N6). Nobody is their own granter: `memberships_not_self_granted` and `membership_granters_not_self` in the database, and `POST /v1/users` now refuses the caller's own address (`USER_SELF_MANAGEMENT`) and a super admin's unless the caller is one (`USER_PROTECTED`), and re-granting a role someone already holds leaves its granter alone. `iam.credential_resets.user_id` restricts deletion of the person (0100), so a deleted user cannot take their reset history with them (W7) · A puppet who could re-grant themselves, or be re-granted by a second admin, would otherwise fall out of the lineage that stops them approving for whoever held their link · —
- 2026-10-02 · gaps-users-auth · D-409 · **A module may withdraw its own pending approval.** `registerExecutor` returns a handle whose `cancel` sets a pending request of that type, and only that type, to `cancelled` (with a note, audited) inside the caller's transaction; `approval.decided` gains the decision `cancelled`, so the requester hears of it. Used when a deactivation cancels a waiting reset. `ApprovalsService.request` also takes the owning module's transaction (`tx`), so a reset and the approval it waits on commit together; a rejection of an approval that names no waiting reset succeeds quietly, so nothing can stay stuck in the queue (the ledger audit's W3, N5). The console inbox does not show a cancelled decision · Before this a withdrawn reset would have left an approval a checker could only reject · —

- 2026-10-02 · M30 · D-360 · **M30 in two parts.** Part 1 (security review and fixes, load tests, the route report, accessibility, training guides, the go-live checklist and the first super admin command) is built now. Part 2 (alert rules, retention policies, the restore drill and the checklist's sign-off) follows on staging, after M14b · Part 2 needs a staging server and M14b's monitoring, which did not exist when part 1 started · Merge the parts if staging arrives first.
- 2026-10-02 · M30 · D-361 · **The load test runs on a throwaway local stack, never on a shared server.** `pnpm load:test` seeds a database of its own at §29's sizes (20,000 SKUs, 5,000 buyers), starts the built API and worker on their own ports with a local Turnstile stand-in, signs agents in through the real endpoints, and runs k6 in the `grafana/k6` image. The intake's per-source limits are raised for the run, because one k6 container is every visitor. Laptop numbers are an upper bound on production's server-side latency, and the report says what they leave out (Cloudflare's round trip, the network) · The preview server hosts other live apps, and a database shared with a developer would measure whatever else it holds · Point the runner at staging once one exists, with the owner's agreement.
- 2026-10-02 · M30 · D-362 · **A branch-scoped cash out follows petty cash's rules, and the branch books no transfer charge.** A cash out by a caller whose `Wallet` reach is narrower than the brand (in practice a branch manager) needs a receipt and, at or above the brand's `wallet_adjustment` threshold, waits for a second person, as petty cash does (security review C1). The approval is raised in the entry's own transaction. A branch-scoped caller may not record a transfer charge (Dr bank charges / Cr the wallet), and no charge may exceed the amount it rides on (the ledger audit's C-1). Nor may they switch a wallet's daily closing off (N8). A cash out by an org-wide holder (accounts) stays ordinary bookkeeping · A branch manager could book till cash out to an operating expense, through a cash out or a transfer's "charge", with no receipt and no checker, and the daily closing balanced because its expected balance moved by the same amount · Narrow or widen the rule in `CashService.record` (`pettyCashRules`) and `TransfersService.transfer`.
- 2026-10-02 · M30 · D-363 · **Payroll money needs the payroll grant and a second person.** Nobody records a staff loan to themselves, disburses a loan they recorded, or disburses one to themselves (`PAYROLL_SELF_DEALING`, 403), which closes D-188's follow-up. An employee's payout method and account, the login linked to them, and bringing back someone who left need `update Payroll` across the brand (hr, org_admin). Bringing someone back covers a status change from resigned or terminated and any change to a leaving date already set, earlier, later or cleared (the ledger audit's C-3). A branch manager adds a hire with cash payout and edits the showroom's record. Nobody changes the payout account of their own employee record, unlinks their own record, or links a record to themselves (security review C2, C3; the ledger audit's W-1) · One hr user could type a principal and hand it to themselves, and a branch manager could point a staffer's salary at their own bKash number or put a leaver back on pay · Revisit with a `staff_loan` approval type if the owner wants a threshold rather than a second person on every loan.
- 2026-10-02 · M30 · D-364 · ASSUMED · **The order desk has a discount ceiling, per line and per order.** A caller may price each line, and the order as a whole, at most their role's ceiling below the catalogue: unit prices under the list, line discounts and the order discount. A markup on one line never offsets a discount on another (the ledger audit's C-2: priced together, a line at double the list carried a free one, and the buyer refused the dear line at the door or exchanged it). Promo codes do not count; the server prices them from marketing's rules (D-204). The ceilings are sales 20%, branch_manager 20% and org_admin 100%; a super admin may go to 100%, and every other role must price at the list. Above the ceiling the save is refused with `ORDER_DISCOUNT_ABOVE_CEILING` (403). An edit above it is allowed only when it shrinks the order: the same SKUs at the same prices, no more units, no larger order discount, and no line and not the order at a deeper rate than before (W-3 and the re-audit: keeping a discount's amount while the units under it shrink made an order free). An exchange's replacement is priced under the same rule. Only the public intake prices through `OrdersApi` with `systemPricing`, and its body carries no price. The delivery charge is not counted: waiving it is an ordinary goodwill gesture and is bounded by the zone's charge (the audit's N10) (security review C4) · A sales agent could book a line at nothing to an accomplice, and the delivery would post full cost against no revenue with nobody else having seen the price; the till has had a ceiling since D-164 · `ORDER_DISCOUNT_CEILING_BPS` in `packages/domain/src/orders/discount-ceiling.ts`. Ask the owner whether 20% fits the desk's real negotiating, and whether a refusal should become a hold for review as at the till.
- 2026-10-02 · M30 · D-365 · ASSUMED · **A short collection a person types is held to a ceiling.** Wherever a person records the amount collected, at the desk (`POST /v1/orders/:id/deliver` and `/partial-delivery`) or on a parcel's manual status (`POST /v1/courier/parcels/:id/status`), a collection below the cash on delivery needs a difference reason. The shortfall may be at most the person's share of the expected collection (`SHORT_COLLECTION_CEILING_BPS`): sales, branch_manager and warehouse 20%, org_admin 100%, every other role none. Above that, an org admin records it. The courier's own figure, by webhook or poll, is not capped: the settlement match checks it. The two ceilings stack: an order priced 20% below the catalogue and then collected 20% short comes to about 36% off the list (security review C5; the ledger audit's W-4 and its re-check) · Otherwise an agent or the warehouse could price within the ceiling and then deliver at nothing, moving the revenue to Sales discounts, and an own-rider parcel has no courier statement to catch it · Ask the owner whether the door needs a smaller allowance of its own (measured against what is left after the desk discount), and whether going over it should hold for review instead of being refused.

Phone and password sign-in (`/sign-in/phone-number`) stays, behind the two-factor hook, which already covers it (M30 security review A3) · The phone-number plugin created a session for whoever owned a number once the code matched, and the two-factor plugin only guards the credential sign-ins, so the day SMS was switched on an org admin or an accountant with a phone on file could have been signed in past their second factor by an SMS alone · Revisit only if an SMS sign-in is ever wanted: it would have to end in the two-factor challenge.

Checked inside the granting transaction after the person lock, through `iam.users_held_elsewhere` (migration 0102, the person's own lens as D-405's `users_active_elsewhere`). A second role in the same brand is unchanged (M30 security review A5) · One brand's admin could create the identity of someone another brand was about to hire, set its password through the invite they were handed, and wait: the second brand's role then opened with a password the first brand's admin knew. A revoked role counts, because the admin could revoke it after setting the password · Ask the owner whether a brand admin should be able to add a colleague from a sister brand themselves; if so, it needs the person to confirm from their own session first. Someone who already has a role in the brand may still be given a second one there by its admin (the audit's N5). Only an active role here exempts a second role; a revoked one does not (the re-audit). `PUT /v1/users/:id/roles` still re-grants a revoked role here to someone now active elsewhere without this check (follow-up: apply the same rule there).
- 2026-10-03 · m30-followups · D-413 · **The generic file download needs the purpose across the brand.** `GET /v1/files/:id/download` serves a private file someone else uploaded only to a caller whose grant for the purpose's subject is org-wide (`effectiveScope(...) === 'org'`, or a super admin). A narrower holder reads only what they uploaded there. An owning module that has checked the row itself passes `rowCheckedByOwner` to `FilesService.signDownload`, which keeps the purpose gate and drops the reach rule; the investors module's document download does (M30 security review B2) · CASL asked without a row answers yes to a branch-scoped or own-scoped grant, so a branch manager holding another branch's receipt id, any staff member holding a colleague's KYC scan id (everyone holds their own `read Employee`), or an investor holding another's document id was handed a signed URL. The file row cannot say which branch, employee or investor a document belongs to; the module that attached it can · Give receipts and KYC scans a module-owned download route, as investors has, when a screen needs a branch manager to open their branch's receipts.
- 2026-10-03 · m30-followups · D-414 · ASSUMED · **Supply dates stay inside §7's posting window.** Receiving a supply (and entering and receiving in one call), a supplier payment and a supplier return take an `occurredAt` only for today or yesterday in Asia/Dhaka; anything else is 409 `APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, before any draft or row is written, and after an idempotent replay (a retry of yesterday's call still answers). A late paper bill is entered on the day it is entered, with its own invoice number on the entry (`supply/posting-date.ts`, M30 security review C6) · Orders, staff loans and payroll already held to the window; supply did not, so a receipt or a payment could be dated into a month already reported, or into next year where no close reaches it. - 2026-10-03 · m30-followups · D-415 · ASSUMED · **A threshold counts the person's day.** A supplier payment is compared with the threshold together with what the same person already paid out to suppliers, to any supplier, on the Asia/Dhaka day they enter it, whatever business date each carries (posted, no approval); petty cash, and a branch-scoped cash out, together with what the same person spent under petty cash's rules that day (petty cash for an org-wide caller; petty cash and cash outs for a branch-scoped one), from any wallet. Entries a checker approved do not count. The person's day is locked first (an advisory lock), so two entries sent at once count each other. The rule is `requiresApprovalForDay` in `packages/domain`; a request held by the day's total names the total, in taka, in its reason (M30 security review C7; the ledger audit's W1 and W2) · One payment at a time, a person could pay a supplier or a bill in pieces under the threshold and no second person ever looked. Per supplier was not enough: the same role adds suppliers without a checker; and per business date was not either, because a payment may be dated yesterday · A busy accounts day now sends its later supplier payments to a checker once the day's total reaches the threshold; ask the owner whether that is the right trade, or whether the threshold should be set higher with the day counted.
- 2026-10-03 · m30-followups · D-416 · ASSUMED · **The tracking page has a miss budget per brand.** Wrong answers (`TRACKING_NOT_FOUND`) are counted per brand: 300 an hour (`TRACKING_LIMITS.perBrandMisses`). Before each lookup the budget is read without spending it (`RateLimitStore.peek`); once it is spent the brand's tracking answers 429 to everyone, found or not, until the window passes, so it is no oracle. A found answer spends nothing. Turnstile on the page is not built: it needs each brand's site key and the widget on the portal (M30 security review D3) · The per-order bucket stops five guesses at one order but nothing stops one guess at each of thousands of orders from many addresses, and order numbers are sequential · Add Turnstile to the tracking page once the brands' site keys exist (go-live), so real buyers get through an attack that pauses the brand.
- 2026-10-03 · m30-followups · D-417 · **An upload is read at most to its ceiling, and the local PUT and the finalize share the row lock.** `StorageDriver.get` takes `maxBytes` and stops the read the moment it passes (a chunked file read locally; a counted, cancelled stream from R2, whatever the length header says); the finalize reads with its type's ceiling and deletes an object that passes it. With the local driver, the bytes PUT writes under `select … for update` on the file row and the finalize examines and marks the file stored under the same lock, so a PUT waits for a finalize and is then refused (M30 B1's race) · The finalize checked the size from the store's metadata and then read the whole object, and the object could be replaced in between. R2's PUT never passes through the API, so no lock can stop it, and holding a transaction across a network read would break invariant 6; there the capped read is what bounds the cost · The R2 half is unverified against a live bucket. A PUT to R2 after the finalize can still replace the bytes until the ticket expires: copy the object to a final key at finalize (follow-up).

Petty cash and branch cash outs (`CashService.hold`) and supplier payments raise with it, in their own transaction. A supplier payment's request is now raised inside the payment's transaction (`tx`) instead of before it (M30's W-2) · A threshold relaxed between the module's check and the request auto-approved the request and ran its executor before the entry existed, so the entry was written pending against an approval already decided and waited for ever; and a day's running total (D-415) is a rule the request cannot see · —

Without `read Cost` the form saves a quantity-only draft for accounts to price (D-005). One row per product: `name, price_taka` required; `category` (code or name), `sizes` and `colours` (lists split on `|`, `;` or quoted commas; never on `/`, so a two-tone `Black/White` stays one value), `status` (`active` default, or `draft`), `description` optional; `colors` accepted for `colours`. Sizes and colours are matched by code or label, ignoring case, to the brand's first variant attribute of kind `size` and of kind `colour`; an unknown value is refused, never created (a typo would become a colour), and so is text matching two values (one's code, another's label). A name already in the catalog or twice in the file is refused. 500 rows and 5,000 SKUs a file (the import is one transaction holding the brand's SKU counter); any other column (including any cost) is refused by name. Slugs are derived, with `-2`, `-3` on a clash. A brand keeps one in-transit location: if its in-transit location (whatever its code) is switched off, the transfer is refused and points at it, and Catalog setup lists it so it can be switched back on; if the code `IN-TRANSIT` is taken by another kind of location, the transfer says so. - 2026-10-03 · catalog-stock-ux · D-432 · **A receipt keeps its key across retries.** The product page's Receive stock, the new-product form's opening stock and Supply entries send the same `supplyId` (or `draftId`) on every attempt until one succeeds (`requestKey` in `apps/console/src/routes/catalog/variant-grid.ts`); only a success, or the server's 409 saying an earlier save went through with other goods, starts a new one: the key survives edits and closing the dialog, because an unanswered save may have gone through and keeping the key is always safe against the server's check below. The supply routes replay a known id only for the same receipt: `POST /v1/supply/entries` and `POST /v1/supply/drafts` compare the stored supplier, location and lines with the request (`sameReceipt` in `packages/domain/src/inventory/receipt-replay.ts`, order-insensitive; invoice number, memo and date are not compared) and answer 409 `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` on a difference. So a retry after a lost response (a proxy timeout) returns the first receipt instead of posting stock and the supplier payable twice, and a retry asking for other goods is refused loudly rather than told it saved. The receive dialogs and the new-product form also refuse a zero cost per piece (one cost covers every variant; free stock goes through Supply entries, D-096) · Ledger audit H1 and L6, and the re-audit's NEW-1 (a kept key with edited content replayed the old receipt): the key was a new uuid per click · Transfers carry no key yet (the route takes none); a retried dispatch moves stock twice, so a follow-up adds one.

- 2026-10-05 · owner · D-469 · **RESOLVED by the owner.** One ERP per brand and a mother ERP above them; each brand's ERP runs its website. Every business gets its own ERP deployment (one shared codebase and UI, its own server processes, its own database, its own domain); a brand's ERP never presents itself as a child, has no connection to any other brand and gives no clue that a mother ERP exists. A separate mother ERP (its own server, domain and database) lets the owner see and handle every brand's data and shows combined figures for everything. Two servers: one for the mother, one for the brands. Each brand's ERP is also its website's back office (orders, customers, leads, offers, discounts, the website's content, staff); business models may differ per brand. First step: Elysium Lifestyle BD's website (`D:\Projects\elysium`) is fully converted so its own admin panel is retired · Owner's instruction of 2026-10-05 · Plans M31 to M37 and `docs/plans/wave3.md`; supersedes the single-database, many-brand deployment of §3 and §5 for production (the code keeps `org_id` and RLS). Decision ids: M31 D-470–479, M32 D-480–499, M33 D-500–519, M34 D-520–539, M35 D-540–559, M36 D-560–579, M37 D-580–599.

There is only an **option** to import the old customers, so they can sign in to the new website with their existing passwords (website accounts with their bcrypt hashes, linked to buyers by phone). Staff re-upload products and stock in the ERP (M38a) · Owner's message of 2026-10-05 · M36's importer shrinks to customers only; no legacy order history table.

The live site on `elysium-lifestyle.bd` is not touched until everything is ready; then the domains change. Elysium's ERP goes live early, with a fresh database, so staff can start uploading products and stock and printing stickers · Owner's answers of 2026-10-05 · Two DNS records (A, to 103.129.209.29) are the owner's to add; the domain is on Cloudflare.

- 2026-10-05 · M33 · D-500 · ASSUMED · **A product's website fields live on the product, and a new product starts off the website.** `catalog.products` gains `web_visible` (separate from `status`: an active product can sell at the till and the desk without being on the website; the storefront shows a product only when it is active, shown and has an active SKU), `featured`, `new_arrival`, `gender` (men, women, kids, unisex or none), `material`, `care_instructions`, `tags` (lower-case, at most 30, normalised by the domain's `normaliseTags`), `size_chart_id`, `seo_title`, `seo_description` and `previous_slugs`; `description` rises to 20,000 characters. `web_visible` defaults to off · The old site published by default, but its products were created on the website's own admin; here a product is created for the till and the desk first, and something an owner never meant to sell online must not appear there by accident · Flip the column default and the console's card if the owner wants new products on the website at once; M36's importer sets the flag from the old site's `isPublished`.
- 2026-10-05 · M33 · D-501 · ASSUMED · **A category's website fields, and a hidden category hides its subtree.** `catalog.categories` gains `web_visible` (default on), `featured` (the home page's category grid), `gender` (the old site's `all` becomes none), `description` and a picture (purpose `category-image`, public). A category switched off or hidden disappears from the website's tree, menu and category filter together with everything under it; a product's own `web_visible` still decides whether the product is listed (unfiltered, or by search) · A shopper who cannot reach Menswear must not find Menswear › Shirts orphaned at the top level; hiding products by their category as well would make one switch do two things · Add a category condition to `webWhere` if the owner wants a hidden category's products off the website too.
- 2026-10-05 · M33 · D-502 · **Size charts are the website module's; a chart in use is not deleted.** `storefront.size_charts (name, columns text[], rows jsonb)`, the old site's shape, checked by `sizeChartProblems` (1 to 12 columns, at most 60 rows, every row one cell per column, cells of at most 40 characters). `catalog.products.size_chart_id` points at it through a composite foreign key declared in migration 0104's SQL rather than the catalog's Drizzle file (the two schema files would import each other); deleting a chart that products use answers `409 STOREFRONT_IN_USE` with the count · A size chart is website content, and a silently emptied chart on a product page is worse than a refusal · —
- 2026-10-05 · M33 · D-503 · ASSUMED · **One settings row per brand, by section; the website sells from named locations.** `storefront.settings` keeps site information, social links, announcement bar, promo banner, testimonials, the shipping rule and the payment methods as JSON sections, each validated by its contract schema on write and parsed defensively on read; Website → Settings saves one section at a time and the server merges it. Defaults name nothing but the brand (its own name; every text empty). The shipping rule keeps the old site's three zones (inside Dhaka, suburbs, outside Dhaka), each with its own fee and the ERP delivery zones it covers (an ERP zone in at most one; seeded by zone code; a threshold of zero or none means always free when free shipping is on). Payment methods: COD and SSLCommerz switches, at least one on (credentials are M34's). `storefront.web_locations` names where the website sells from (default: the brand's first active warehouse, the order desk's own default); only active warehouses and showrooms. The merchant id is kept for labels and never sent to the website. `siteUrl` gives the console its "View on website" link, `<siteUrl>/shop/<slug>` (the old site's product path) · One row is what the old site had; sections let two people edit the banner and the shipping rule at once; a location table gives each entry a composite foreign key · Confirm the product path with M36.
- 2026-10-05 · M33 · D-504 · **The menu is two levels, and only safe links reach the website.** `storefront.nav_items`: a link, a category (expanded on the website into two levels of its visible subcategories, the old site's menu) or a dropdown holding its own items; only a top-level dropdown holds items (`navProblems`, checked inside the write under a per-brand lock). An item pointing at a hidden category, and an empty dropdown, are left out of the website's menu. Every link staff type (menu, hero, announcement, promo buttons) is a path on the site or an `https://` address (`isSiteLink` in contracts and `isSafeSiteLink` in the domain, held equal by test): never `javascript:`, `data:`, `http://` or `//elsewhere` · The website renders these into its own pages, so the ERP is the last place a typo or an injection can be caught · —
- 2026-10-05 · M33 · D-505 · **The old site's ids are kept in one map.** `storefront.legacy_ids (kind, legacy_id, erp_id)`, primary key `(org_id, kind, legacy_id)`, kinds product, variant, category, size chart, customer, order, discount, flash sale, landing page and custom link; insert and delete only, no foreign key on `erp_id` (it names a row in whichever table the kind says). Written by M36's importer through `STOREFRONT_API.recordLegacyIds`, read by redirects and by M35's connector through `legacyIds` · The connector addresses variants by the old site's ids, and a missing map would break every live marketplace listing on the day of the switch · —
- 2026-10-05 · M33 · D-506 · ASSUMED · **The storefront read API.** `/storefront/v1`: `catalog/products` (category subtree, gender, tags, size, price range in poisha, featured, new arrival, on sale, search; sorts newest, oldest, price-asc, price-desc, featured, name; keyset cursor, plus an optional `page` and a `total` because the old shop numbers its pages), `catalog/products/:slug` (variants with live availability; an old slug answers `200 { redirect: { slug } }`, not a 3xx, because `fetch` would follow it and the path is signed), `catalog/categories`, `catalog/search`, `site`, `size-charts/:id`, `geo/districts`, `geo/thanas`. Signed with `X-Api-Key`, `X-Api-Timestamp`, `X-Api-Signature = sha256=HMAC(secret, timestamp.METHOD.path?query)` (a write adds the idempotency key and the raw body, for M34), ±5 minutes, the same 401 for every failure. Answers leave through `serializeForStorefront` (an allow-list at every depth, then the cost deny-list), as `{ poisha, taka }` money, with a strong ETag, `304` on a match and `Cache-Control: private, max-age=30, stale-while-revalidate=300` and `Vary: X-Api-Key` — **private**, where the plan said only `max-age`, so a shared cache in front of the ERP never hands a signed answer to an unsigned request. Rate limits 6,000 a minute per source address and 3,000 per key (configurable). `site` is kept 30 seconds per brand in the process, forgotten on any write through it · The website is outside the ERP and whatever reaches it can reach a browser; the old shop's numbered pages are a feature M36 must keep · —
- 2026-10-05 · M33 · D-507 · ASSUMED · **The website's images are served from a public path, sizes only.** `GET /media/<org id>/<file id>/<thumb|card|full>` (`@Public`) serves the re-encoded WebP sizes (160, 640, 1600 px) of a stored, public file of purpose `product-image`, `category-image` or `storefront-image` (the new purpose for hero slides and the logo, read through `Storefront`), inside the brand the path names; never the original, never another purpose, and the same 404 for everything else. `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, the variant's SHA-256 as ETag. The org id is in the path because the request has no other way to name its tenant under FORCE RLS. The storefront API's image URLs are built on `STOREFRONT_MEDIA_BASE_URL` (default `API_PUBLIC_URL`), so a CDN can front `/media/` without a code change. The existing three sizes are used: the 1,600-pixel size serves the old site's 1,920 × 750 hero, which the website's image optimiser scales · A CDN or nginx cache in front (M37) keeps the API from serving the same bytes twice; behind it, a found variant's metadata is kept ten minutes in the process and an address may ask 1,200 times a minute (ledger audit L5). - 2026-10-05 · M31 · D-479 · **`TRUSTED_PROXY_COUNT` trusts that many hops, for real.** The API hands Fastify a hop function, `(address, hop) => hop < n` (proxy-addr's trust signature), instead of the number: Fastify 5.12 reads a numeric `trustProxy` as "trust nothing" (`getTrustProxyFn` fails closed on hop counts), so `request.ip` was the proxy's own address for every visitor and every per-address limit (sign-in, the owner link, the tracking page, the order intake) was one shared bucket. Trusting by hop is safe because every deployment reaches the API only through its proxy: the brand and preview stacks publish the port on 127.0.0.1 alone and nginx sets `X-Forwarded-For` to the visitor; Kamal's VMs take 80 and 443 from Cloudflare's ranges only (`deploy.md`). The nearest hop must also be loopback or a private address (nginx, Docker's bridge, kamal-proxy), so a port published to the internet by mistake trusts nothing. 0 still means no proxy, the header ignored (tested: one hop with `X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.7` from a loopback peer gives 203.0.113.7; none gives the peer) · Coordinator's finding, 2026-10-05 · An address list (`TRUSTED_PROXIES`) was not needed: no deployment exposes the API port beyond its proxy.
- 2026-10-05 · M37 · D-580 · **A brand behind Cloudflare gets the visitor's address from Cloudflare.** `BRAND_BEHIND_CLOUDFLARE=true` in a brand's `.env.brand` makes `server-deploy.sh` install `infra/brand/nginx-cloudflare-realip.conf` as `/etc/nginx/snippets/erp-cloudflare-realip.conf` (every deploy) and include it in the brand's site (first install): `set_real_ip_from` for each of Cloudflare's published ranges (read from cloudflare.com/ips-v4 and ips-v6 on 2026-10-05) and `real_ip_header CF-Connecting-IP`, so `$remote_addr`, and with it the API's `X-Forwarded-For` and `request.ip`, is the visitor. The directives sit in the brand's own server block and touch no other site · Elysium's temporary hosts (`erp.` and `demo.elysium-lifestyle.bd`, D-581) are orange-cloud records, so without this every visitor would share a handful of Cloudflare edge addresses in the per-address rate limits (D-056) and the audit log · Refresh the range list when Cloudflare changes it; an origin reached directly cannot claim an address with the header.
- 2026-10-05 · M37 · D-582 · **A brand's website is connected from the server.** `node dist/main.admin.js website-key --label "<label>"` (`pnpm admin:website-key`) issues a storefront site key through `StorefrontService.issueKey`, the method `POST /v1/storefront/site-keys` calls (same derivation `deriveStorefrontSecret(INTAKE_KEY_SECRET, keyId)`, same fingerprint row, same in-transaction audit row `storefront.site_key_issued`), and prints on stdout exactly `ERP_SITE_KEY_ID=pk_…` and `ERP_SITE_SECRET=sk_…`; the key id and every other word go to stderr, log lines too (`LOG_DESTINATION=stderr`, and stdout is redirected to stderr while the command runs). `website-site-url --url https://…` saves Website → Settings' Site URL through `StorefrontService.setSiteUrl`: the same lock, merge and `storefront.settings_updated` audit row as a console save, the rest of the site details read inside the lock and kept. The actor is the system actor `system:admin-cli` (the `system:<who>` convention of `system:courier`), stored as `created_by`/`updated_by` and `actor_user_id`, with `via: 'cli'` on both audit rows; the settings audit summary now carries `siteUrl` for console saves too. The brand is `ERP_BRAND_SLUG`, checked as the API checks it at boot, else the database's one non-training org (`commandBrand`); two brands, none or a switched-off one are refused. It refuses without `INTAKE_KEY_SECRET`. The Site URL must be `https://` (the form's own `siteInfoSchema` rule) with no query or fragment; trailing slashes are dropped. **http://localhost is refused too**, unlike the request's "http only for localhost": the stored site details are parsed with that schema on every read, so an `http://` value would make the whole section fall back to its defaults (and the next console save write them over the brand's phone, email and address), and every link built from it checks for `https://` anyway · The website (a separate app on the same server) needs the key in its root-only env file; the console shows the secret once on screen, so issuing it there means displaying and copying it · Run in the brand's release container (`docs/runbooks/brand-deploy.md` §5); a key whose output did not reach its file is revoked on Website → Connection and issued again.
- 2026-10-05 · M35a · D-540 · **One discount engine, the old website's, in integer poisha.** `packages/domain/src/promo/discounts.ts` ports the old site's `computeDiscounts`: code and automatic discounts; percent (bps), flat, free delivery, buy X get Y (`buyQuantity`, `getQuantity`, `getDiscountBps`; the cheapest units in each group), tiered by the whole basket's subtotal; scope by SKU, product or category (a line carries its category's ancestry); minimum order, minimum quantity, first order only, a date window and an instant window, total and per-buyer limits, a ceiling; the best single discount, or every stackable one together when together they give at least as much, ties to the stack, then priority, then id. M16's `validatePromo` is now a view of the same `evaluateDiscount`, so the desk and the website cannot disagree. **Rounding:** every percentage is `percentOf`, half away from zero to the poisha, once per discount (a buy-X-get-Y rounds the sum of its discounted units); the old engine rounded float taka to two decimals with an epsilon nudge, so the two agree to the poisha except on an exact half poisha, where float taka could go either way. **Deliberate departures from the old engine,** each held to by the differential test: a free-delivery discount on an order with no delivery charge is refused (`nothing_to_discount`, M16's desk rule) where the old engine applied it at no value (same money); a stack is valued as what it gives and each stacked discount comes off its own lines only (`allocate` over what each line has left), where the old engine summed members, counted a waived delivery once per free-shipping member and over-gave on scoped stacks (ledger audit W1); a buy-X-get-Y's units are grouped by price rather than listed (same answer). Old-site documents read through `discountFromOldSite` (`legacy.ts`): `maxDiscount` only for percentage (the old admin's own rule), zero means none, `getDiscountPercent` 0 means 100, an empty products/categories scope matches nothing **Evidence for M35 check 2:** the plan asked for recorded carts exported from the old site; the old site's database is not reachable from the build, so the check runs the old engine itself on thousands of generated carts and eleven named cases written from the old admin's offers, and M36's Mongo export can add recorded carts to the same test · The owner asked for the old site's discounts in the ERP (D-469); one function is the only way the desk, the website and M36's import agree · The oracle is `promo/oracle/old-site-discounts.js`, copied verbatim (commit 333393e), never imported by production code; change the engine and the differential test together.
- 2026-10-05 · M35a · D-541 · **Discounts live in `promo.codes`, extended.** M35a adds `method` (`code`/`automatic`; an automatic discount has no code and needs a title), `title`, `description`, the kinds `buy_x_get_y` and `tiered` (steps in `promo.discount_tiers`, money as `bigint`, insert-only), `min_quantity`, `first_order_only`, `product_ids`, `starts_at`/`ends_at`, `allow_stacking` and `priority` to M16's table rather than moving codes elsewhere: orders, redemptions, the CRM's occasion greetings and M18's attribution all name rows here. Existing codes become method `code`, titled with their own word (0105, brand by brand under RLS). The API keeps M16's path, `/v1/promo/codes`. Terms stay fixed once a discount exists (status only). Two changes reach M16's codes: a category scope now covers the category's sub-categories (before, only SKUs filed directly in it), and a free-delivery code's scope is now honoured (before, ignored); no code in production relied on either, the ERP not being live. A per-buyer limit counts the ERP buyer (by phone); a website account resolves to its buyer before quoting (M34). `redeem` takes a use of each applied discount under a conditional UPDATE in id order, re-counts the per-buyer limit and re-checks a first-order rule under that row lock, and records the amounts the quote computed **in the same transaction** (the caller's contract, as M16's `PricedPromo`) · The old admin's discount screen moves into the ERP (wave 3) · Moving discounts to their own table would be a migration and an FK change in four modules.
- 2026-10-05 · M35a · D-542 · **Flash sales, claimed per order line and given back exactly once.** `promo.flash_sales` (title, subtitle, description, slug unique per brand, banner, switched on, home page, priority, window) and `promo.flash_sale_items` (per SKU: sale price, unit limit or all stock, units sold; `sold <= limit`, price > 0). The ranking is the old site's: the cheapest live offer below the shelf price, then the one ending soonest (`bestFlashOffer`, `planFlashLines`: whole lines only). A claim (`claimFlashUnits`) runs in the order's transaction after its lines exist: one conditional UPDATE per line (`units_sold + qty <= unit_limit`, switched on, live by the database's clock, the sale price still the one charged), all inside a savepoint so a refusal undoes every unit even if the caller catches it; one `promo.flash_claims` row per order line, tied by composite foreign keys to a whole line of its own order, of the same SKU, charged the claimed sale price (`unit_price_poisha`), and to its offer's SKU (0106); a claim that names anything else is refused (`FLASH_SALE_INVALID`). An order whose lines hold claims, or that carries any applied discount, refuses an edit of its lines (and, for discounts, of its discount and delivery charge): cancel and take it again. The order state machine declares `release_flash_units` on every cancel (all units back) and every received return (each line's undelivered units back); delivered units stay sold; each claim is released by a conditional UPDATE and a trigger refuses any other change. A sale price must be below the SKU's price when saved; a sale is switched on only with something on it; SKUs taken off are switched off, never deleted; nothing is deleted. The old site's product-wide cap (`stockLimit` on legacy items) is not carried: M36 maps such items to per-size limits · The old admin's flash sales move into the ERP · Change the effect list in `packages/domain/src/orders/state.ts`, the claim in `flash.repository.ts`.
- 2026-10-05 · M35a · D-543 · ASSUMED · **A first order is a buyer with no order that stands.** A first-order-only discount applies when the ERP buyer has no earlier order other than cancelled ones; a shopper not yet known by phone counts as first (the old site's rule), and checkout re-checks at redeem once the buyer is known, under the discount's row lock (so two orders using the same first-order discount are serialised; two different first-order discounts on two simultaneous orders from one new buyer are not, a bounded gap accepted here). The promo module asks the orders module through `BuyerHistoryRegistry`, which the orders module fills at startup (invariant 12); with nothing registered the answer is "not first" · A cancelled order never was a purchase; the old site counted every order, which refused a buyer whose first attempt was cancelled · Count all orders by dropping the status filter in `orders.repository.ts` `standingOrderCount`.

- 2026-10-05 · M35a · D-548 · **What M34 calls.** `PromoApi.quoteDiscounts(tx, orgId, { lines: {skuId, qty, netPoisha}[], customerId, channel, codes, deliveryChargePoisha, now, automatic? })` → applied discounts with amounts, refused codes with reasons (`not_found` for an unknown word), totals; `redeem(tx, orgId, { orderId, customerId, applied })` after the order row exists; `liveFlashOffers(tx, orgId, skuIds, now)` with `bestFlashOffer`/`planFlashLines` from `@elysium/domain/promo` to price lines; `claimFlashUnits(tx, orgId, { orderId, lines: {orderLineId, skuId, qty, flashSaleItemId, salePricePoisha}[] })` after the lines exist (409 `FLASH_SALE_UNAVAILABLE` with `skuIds` on refusal); releases happen through the order's own cancel and return; `storefrontFlashSales(tx, orgId, { now, homeOnly })` and `storefrontFlashSale(tx, orgId, slug, now)` give the storefront module what its `GET /storefront/v1/flash-sales` and `/:slug` serve (those routes are M33/M34's: the storefront module owns the signed API); `holdsOnOrder` is what an order edit asks. **Order of calls in one checkout transaction:** price, insert the order and its lines, `redeem`, then `claimFlashUnits` (the order a cancel releases them in, so the two cannot deadlock). Each of `redeem` and `claimFlashUnits` runs in its own savepoint: a refusal undoes everything that call took, so a checkout may catch it and reprice in the same transaction. Roles mirror supply (D-005): `create Supply` writes quantity-only drafts (strict schema, no cost field), `read Cost` prices and posts (service re-checks `canReadCost`), `update Supply` deletes a draft. **The window** (`inventory.opening_stock_windows`, no row = open): an org admin closes it (`approve StockAdjustment`, audited); while closed, drafts and posts are refused with `OPENING_STOCK_CLOSED` by the service and by the database's triggers (the row is read `for share`, the close takes it `for update`); reopening is an `opening_stock_reopen` approval (D-608), carried out by inventory's executor; the database refuses closed → open without an approved request decided after the close. (2) A posted entry is reversed while the window is open, exactly or not at all: `POST /v1/inventory/stock-in-hand/:id/reverse` (`read Cost`) raises a `stock_in_hand_reversal` (D-608) valued at the entry's cost; on approval inventory's executor moves the same pieces out of the same location at the entry's cost (`adjustment_loss`, `ref_type` `stock_in_hand`) and the ledger reverses the entry's journal (Dr 3350 / Cr Inventory, `reverses_journal_id`), and the entry becomes `reversed`. Refused with `STOCK_IN_HAND_REVERSAL_BLOCKED` once an entry's SKU averages to another cost (taking the pieces out at the entry's cost would leave the stock ledger and 1300 apart; a count corrects it instead), and by the movement when the pieces have left the location or are reserved; the window does not close while a reversal waits (a reversal needs it open). (3) `POST /:id/post` repeats the location and each line's pieces; a draft changed since is refused with `STOCK_IN_HAND_CHANGED`, and a draft's update is audited with its lines before and after. (4) `InventoryApi.receiveStockInHand` and M14a's `receiveOpening` check the window themselves. (5) The trigger checks a posted entry's journal is its own and worth its lines, and a line changes only while the window is open. **ASSUMED, owner question:** a post needs no second person while the window is open (the owner's plan: an org admin closes the window once everything is in, and every post is audited); if the owner wants a checker on each entry, an approval above a threshold is the change.

- 2026-10-06 · M38b · D-610 · **The packing station: every piece scanned against the order, the order packed through the state machine.** Orders → Pack (`/orders/packing`, `?order=` keeps the open order across a reload). `POST /v1/packing/sessions { code | orderId }` (`update Order`, the order in the caller's scope, `confirmed` only; the code is the order number or a courier code, as M35b's lookup reads them) opens or resumes the order's one `packing.sessions` row (`unique (org_id, order_id)`: a packed order never returns to `confirmed`). `POST …/:id/scans { scanId, code }` resolves the code by barcode or SKU code (`CatalogApi.lookupCode`) and judges it with `judgePackScan` (`packages/domain/src/packing`, fast-check): taken while the order still needs a piece of that SKU, else `over`, `wrong_variant` (the order has another SKU of the same product), `wrong_item` or `unknown_code`; every scan, refusals included, is a row of append-only `packing.scans` under the browser's `scanId` (a retried scan counts once), judged under the session's row lock and the order's (`bookingSnapshot … forUpdate`, so an edit of the lines waits). A refusal is an answer (200, `accepted: false`), not an error, so it is kept; the console sounds a low buzz and shows it red. "Take one out" is a `removed` row. `POST …/:id/complete` requires every SKU at exactly its quantity and nothing else in the box (`PACKING_INCOMPLETE` otherwise), then calls `OrdersApi.transition(…, 'pack')` (the machine's `confirmed → packed`, its effects, history and audit — the transition the Pack button and bulk pack run) and completes the session in the same transaction; a second finish answers `alreadyCompleted` and moves nothing. Triggers (migration 0126): a session opens only for a confirmed order, completes only with its order packed, never changes after; a finished session takes no scans. Scanning the order's own slip again (its number or the courier code its QR carries) finishes the box · The owner's "scan to select products while packing … so a wrong size or a missing piece is caught at the table" (D-600); one path into `packed` keeps invariant 7 · —
- 2026-10-06 · M38b · D-611 · ASSUMED · **The packing video: the browser records, with the order, the time and the packer drawn on.** While a session is open and the brand records, the station draws the chosen camera onto a canvas with a caption band (order number, Asia/Dhaka date and time to the second, the packer's name, a red dot) and records the canvas (`captureStream(15)` → `MediaRecorder`): about 720p, 800 kbit/s, VP8 WebM first, any WebM, then MP4 (Safari), **no sound**, a part stopping itself at 15 minutes or 86 MiB (`RECORDING_LIMIT_MS`, `RECORDING_MAX_BYTES`, `apps/console/src/routes/orders/pack-logic.ts`). Each part has the browser's id, is registered as it starts (`POST …/:id/videos`), and its 4-second chunks are kept in IndexedDB (`elysium-packing`) as they arrive, so a reload or crash loses a few seconds: the next visit uploads what was kept as that part and starts the next. On finish (or closing the order, changing camera, the limit) the part uploads through the files module's own flow (ticket → PUT → finalize) and attaches (`POST …/videos/:videoId/file`: a stored, private `packing-video` the caller uploaded, of the part's type, once); a failure stays in the browser and is retried at 30 s, 1, 2, 5, then every 10 minutes, on the next visit and when the connection returns, never discarded. A session's video reads `off` (recording off when it opened), `pending`, `uploaded`, `missing` (a part not arrived, or none registered, two hours after the finish: `PACKING_VIDEO_GRACE_MS`) or `deleted` (`packingVideoState`). The order page lists the parts and plays one from a signed URL on demand · The plan's "about 720p, about 1 Mbit/s, at most 15 minutes"; 800 kbit/s keeps fifteen minutes under 90 MiB, inside one request through Cloudflare (100 MB); sound adds size and records conversations nobody asked to keep; a caption in the pixels cannot be separated from the picture it vouches for · Bit rate, frame size and limits are constants in `pack-logic.ts`; sound would need `audio: true` in `pack-recorder.ts` and the decision re-taken.
- 2026-10-06 · M38b · D-612 · **A packing video is deleted seven days after the order ends.** `packingVideoExpiry` (`packages/domain/src/packing/retention.ts`, fast-check): for an order `delivered` or `partially_delivered`, `returned`, `cancelled` or `exchange`, the video is kept through the seventh Asia/Dhaka day after the day it reached that status and may go from the eighth (never less than seven whole days; always by nine); an order on its way (`pending` to `shipped`, `return_in_transit`) keeps it, and so does an ended order with no record of when it ended. When it ended is the latest `sales.order_events` row into its current status (`OrdersApi.retentionFacts`), so a partly delivered order that comes back starts again from the return. `packing.purge_videos` (daily 04:15 Dhaka, global) looks at every brand's attached videos and deletes each expired one in its own transaction, re-reading the expiry under the part's lock: the part keeps its row (`file_id` null, `deleted_at`, `deleted_reason`, `deleted_file_id`), the file's row is deleted (`FilesApi.forget`), the audit entry `packing.video_deleted` records the file, the order, the session, the reason and the dates, and `files.remove_object` is enqueued in that transaction to delete the bytes after it commits (never inside it). A deleted video answers `PACKING_VIDEO_GONE` (410) · The owner's "kept up to 7 days after delivery"; returned and cancelled orders from the plan; exchange because it is a delivered order's end too · `PACKING_VIDEO_KEEP_DAYS` in the domain; the cron in `packing.queues.ts`.

In one transaction: claim the key (`checkout_submissions`, unique per website key; a concurrent copy waits on the index and is answered from the record; a key reused for another body is `409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`; a retry gets a fresh view token, since the first is never stored), re-price the cart, refuse with `409 CART_REPRICED` and the new quote if the total or a line's unit price moved, a line became unavailable or the address is not delivered to (`409 CART_NOT_DELIVERABLE`), create the order through `OrdersApi.createOrder` (`channel = website`, `pending`, unassigned, the buyer upserted by phone as the desk and the intake do, the address as the order's snapshot, each line at its quoted unit price, the goods discount as the order discount, the delivery charge net of a free-delivery discount), hold the stock at **one** web location — the first in the brand's order that holds every line (D-532) — with M07's atomic conditional reserve, redeem the discounts and claim the flash units (D-539), write the website's record (`web_orders`: account, email, locale, payment method, phone), link a signed-in account to its buyer (D-533), store a view token's SHA-256, queue the "order placed" email, and for SSLCommerz the sixty-minute timer (D-527). Two shoppers racing for the last unit: one order and one `409 OUT_OF_STOCK` naming the lines (integration test). A COD that disagreed with the quote would roll the order back (never seen; guarded) · The checkout must be one decision with one total; an order split across locations could not be dispatched as one parcel · Owner question: with several web locations, should an order that no single location can fill be split? Today it is refused as out of stock.
- 2026-10-05 · M34 · D-522 · ASSUMED · **Shopper accounts.** `storefront.customer_accounts`, one per email per brand (lower-cased), linked to `sales.customers` by phone. Register (bcrypt cost 12 through `bcryptjs`; at least 8 characters, at most bcrypt's 72 bytes; the old site's 6 still sign in), a six-digit email code stored as `HMAC-SHA256(key derived from INTAKE_KEY_SECRET under "storefront.account-code.v1:", purpose:account:code)`, 15 minutes, 5 tries (the tries commit even when the answer is wrong), a new code consuming the older ones; re-registering an **unverified** address replaces its password and name (the inbox decides who owns it); a verified one is `409 ACCOUNT_EXISTS`. Sign-in refuses an unverified address after a right password (`403 ACCOUNT_EMAIL_UNVERIFIED`) and compares against a dummy hash when there is no account (one `401` for both). Forgot password: 32 random bytes, stored as SHA-256, one hour, used once, every other link ended with it; always the same answer. Reset also verifies the email (the link reached the inbox). Google: the website verified the ID token; the ERP signs in by `google_sub`, else creates an account for a **verified** address with no password, else links Google to an existing **verified** account; never to an unverified one (`409 ACCOUNT_EMAIL_UNVERIFIED`: verify or reset first) and never to an account linked to another Google login (`409 ACCOUNT_GOOGLE_CONFLICT`); a CHECK refuses `google_sub` on an unverified row. The ERP sets no cookie; it answers with the account and the website keeps its own next-auth session. Answers never carry a hash or Google's id · The old site's rules, with the codes and links hashed (wave 3's problem 4) and a pre-hijacked address unable to take a Google login · —
- 2026-10-05 · M34 · D-523 · **Claiming past guest orders.** When an account's email becomes verified (the email code, a Google sign-in that creates or links, a password reset), every website order with no account that gave **that email** joins the account (`claimsGuestOrder` and `claimGuestOrders`). **Never by phone**: an account's phone is typed, not verified, so a verified account that typed a stranger's number and asked for a reset would have taken the stranger's email-less orders, with name, address, items and tracking (M34 ledger audit W5). An order placed without an email stays reachable by its view link · The old site also folded guest stubs by phone; that was the privacy hole · Claim by phone only after a phone OTP, if the owner wants the old behaviour back.
- 2026-10-05 · M34 · D-524 · **What a shopper sees of an order.** `GET /storefront/v1/orders/:orderNo?token=` (a guest's link) or `GET /storefront/v1/accounts/:id/orders[/:orderNo]`: the shopper's status words (pending → placed; confirmed, on hold, packed → processing; shipped; delivered; partially delivered → partly delivered; return in transit and returned → returned; cancelled; exchange → exchanged), the dates, the money charged (items, discount, delivery, paid, still due on delivery), how it is paid (`cash_on_delivery`, `awaiting_payment`, `paid`, `refund_due`), the address given, the lines at the prices charged, and once the parcel is booked the courier and its tracking code. No cost, no staff, no internal note, and a desk order is not found whatever asks. View tokens are 32 random bytes kept as SHA-256: one from the checkout, one per order email · A guest needs a link that works from an email on another device; an unguessable token is that link · —
- 2026-10-05 · M34 · D-525 · **SSLCommerz.** Per brand, on Website → Payments: enabled, sandbox or live, the store id, the store password sealed (D-528). `POST /storefront/v1/payments/sslcommerz/init` (by view token or account) records a session (`payment_sessions`: our random 24-character transaction id, the order's COD as the amount) and enqueues `storefront.sslcommerz_open`, then waits on the row up to `STOREFRONT_GATEWAY_WAIT_MS` (15 s) outside any transaction; the worker calls SSLCommerz's session API (multipart, as `sslcommerz-lts` did) with no transaction open (invariant 6), success/fail/cancel URLs on the website (`<siteUrl>/api/payment/sslcommerz/…`, the old site's paths) and the IPN on the ERP (`<API_PUBLIC_URL>/storefront/ipn/sslcommerz/<org id>`, public, rate-limited, one answer for everything). The website relays the shopper's return (`POST …/payments/sslcommerz/return`). Neither the return nor the IPN is believed: a `val_id` goes to `storefront.sslcommerz_validate`, which asks the Order Validation API and accepts only VALID or VALIDATED for this session's transaction, in BDT, for exactly the session's amount, which must still be exactly what the order collects (`judgeValidation`, property-tested). A `val_id` first moves the session to `validating` under its lock, in the transaction that enqueues the check (D-527). A wrong amount or a changed order (or a second page paid for one order) is `refused`, logged and audited for a person — and the money SSLCommerz took is still booked, into the clearing wallet as an advance **owed back** at once, never applied to the COD, so the bank deposit has a journal behind it (ledger audit W3); a wrong currency or an unreadable amount is refused unbooked for a person. A payment that is not valid or names another transaction books nothing and puts the session back to `ready` (or `expired` past its page's deadline). A brand whose store was switched to the other mode since the page opened gets the payment refused for a person (nothing to ask); a key this server cannot open (a worker without `STOREFRONT_CREDENTIALS_KEY`, a rotation half done) fails the check, which is retried and reported, never a payment refused for good (ledger re-audit). **A sandbox store takes no money on an installation that does not allow it** (`STOREFRONT_SSLCOMMERZ_ALLOW_SANDBOX`, default off; on for staging, preview and tests): checkout and init refuse SSLCommerz, and a sandbox payment is refused unbooked — SSLCommerz's test cards are public, and a booked sandbox payment would send goods out with nothing to collect (ledger audit C1). `init` locks the order first, so two calls open one page. Once per session however often told (the session row locked; the advance's reference unique). `risk_level` 1 is accepted and flagged; the desk confirms every website order anyway. Gateway fees are recorded when the settlement arrives (a follow-up) · Wave 3's problem 3: the old site never compared the amount and had no IPN · Owner question: hold risky payments instead of accepting them?
- 2026-10-05 · M34 · D-526 · **A website payment is a verified advance.** An accepted SSLCommerz payment becomes a `sales.order_advances` row with method `sslcommerz` (the CHECK widened; agents still type only bKash, Nagad or bank), recorded by `storefront:checkout` and decided by `gateway:sslcommerz` (the four-eyes CHECK holds: the gateway's validation is the evidence accounts would look for), posted through the existing rule — Dr SSLCommerz clearing / Cr Customer advance (`customerAdvanceDraft`, keyed on the advance) — and, before the parcel moves, the COD lowered by the amount in the same transaction (`OrdersApi.recordGatewayAdvance`). A gateway payment on an order that already ended is received and **owed back in full** whatever the brand's cancel policy — the "keep up to the delivery charge" policy is for an advance paid before the order ended, never for money that arrived after the system cancelled it (ledger audit W2); one whose parcel is on the road stays a pending claim for accounts. The clearing wallet is the org-level wallet with provider `sslcommerz` (`wallet_mfs_other`), found by provider; when there is none, `WalletsApi.ensureSystemWallet` creates `1120 SSLCommerz clearing`, or the next free code up to 1129 if a staff wallet already sits at 1120 (ledger audit W4), one first setup at a time per brand (a transaction lock), when the gateway is saved or on the first payment; accounts moves it to the bank with a wallet transfer when SSLCommerz settles. The property test holds `cod = items − discount + delivery − advance` after every payment · No second posting rule for one kind of money; a clearing wallet keeps gateway money apart from the bank until it lands · —
- 2026-10-05 · M34 · D-527 · ASSUMED · **An unpaid online order.** A failed or cancelled return ends the session and cancels the order through the state machine (releasing its stock, discounts and flash units) only if it is still pending with nothing paid; the transaction id is ours and unguessable, so a forged return cannot name another shopper's order. `storefront.expire_unpaid` runs `STOREFRONT_UNPAID_ORDER_MINUTES` (60) after the checkout and does the same, expiring open sessions. Both lock the order first, so an agent's confirm or a payment and the cancel happen one after the other. **A payment being checked keeps the order**: while a session is `validating` (for up to 15 minutes) a fail leaves the order alone and the timer looks again later — both decide with the order's sessions locked, so a check that starts at that moment is seen or waits; a check the gateway never answers in that time expires with the order (ledger audit W1). A payment page never outlives its order's deadline, and none opens with less than a minute left. A success return with no `val_id` answers "checking" and cancels nothing. A valid payment that arrives after the cancel is still booked and owed back (D-526) · An abandoned online order must not hold stock for ever (the plan's risk) · Change the minutes per installation.
- 2026-10-05 · M34 · D-528 · ASSUMED · **The website's credentials.** `STOREFRONT_CREDENTIALS_KEY` (32 bytes, base64; `STOREFRONT_CREDENTIALS_KEY_ID` names it) seals the SSLCommerz store password, the email server's password and the bodies of emails waiting to be sent, with AES-256-GCM bound to the brand, the row and the field (`platform/crypto/sealed-box.ts`, the courier's arrangement); without it nothing is sealed and the screens say so (`WEBSITE_CREDENTIALS_MISSING`). Passwords are write-only, shown as a 12-hex fingerprint — an HMAC under a key derived from `STOREFRONT_CREDENTIALS_KEY`, so the fingerprint cannot be used to guess a weak password offline (ledger audit W7). Changing them is a new subject, `WebsiteCredentials`, org_admin only; reading the screens is `read Storefront` · Whoever can repoint SSLCommerz receives the shoppers' money, and whoever can repoint the email server receives their reset links: not a marketing permission · Split the key per module if the two ever need rotating apart.

Templates are code (`packages/domain/src/notify/emails.ts`), Bangla and English, the brand's site name as a value: email code, password reset, order placed, confirmed, shipped (courier and tracking code), delivered, cancelled, and the server test. Written in the caller's transaction, sent by `notify.send_email` with no transaction open, three tries; the rendered body is sealed until sent and then cleared; with no server switched on (or in the training org) the email is recorded as `skipped`. Addresses are masked in every log line (`maskEmail`). The test button sends at once, outside any transaction, and answers with what the server said · The old site already mailed through each brand's own server · —

The console shows the account on the order page (with the payment method and the online payments) under the order's own scope (`read Order`, as the order page has it), and lists every account (Website → Customers, and the buyer profile's card), every email sent (Website → Email) and every online payment (Website → Payments) only to a caller with an **org-wide** reach of `read Customer` or `read Order` (org_admin, accounts, marketing); a sales agent's own-scope reach cannot be applied to those lists, so they are refused rather than widened (ledger audit W6) · Shopper emails and phones are buyer data, not website settings · —

The website's server forwards browser events with the pixel's event id (`POST /storefront/v1/events`, at most 20 per call, 120 a minute per shopper address, older than about a week skipped); personal data is normalised and SHA-256-hashed before it is stored and shown masked; the outgoing payload is sealed (`STOREFRONT_CREDENTIALS_KEY`) and cleared once sent; `storefront.tracking_send` sends with no transaction open (invariant 6), one retry after 300 ms. `Purchase` is written in the checkout's and the ncom handover's transaction; an SSLCommerz order's waits until the payment is booked and is never sent unpaid. Without the credentials key nothing personal can be kept, so events are answered `skipped`. ASSUMED: event id `purchase_<orderNo>` (the website gives its pixel the same id on the confirmation page), the old site's default proxy paths `/e1/s.js`, `/e1/t`, `/e2/s.js`, `/e2`, a 30-day log, and `fake` as the default provider with `live` needing `STOREFRONT_TRACKING_ENABLED=true` (D-257's two-key rule).

The org id is in the path because nothing else names a tenant before the signature is checked under FORCE RLS (the IPN's arrangement). Outward ids are the old site's where `storefront.legacy_ids` has them, else uuids; both are read. `active` is a product on the website; drafts are offered while "Include draft products" is on, at zero availability. A hold (`marketplace.ncom_holds`) is the inventory's atomic reserve at one web location (`ref_type ncom_hold`), all or nothing, given back exactly once; it is **not a sale**: it becomes an order's stock when ncom hands over an order whose id or number is its reference, and one ncom never releases is an org admin's to release on Website → Marketplace (`update WebsiteCredentials`). The old site's checkers (`scripts/ncom-check.mjs`, `scripts/ncom-order-check.mjs`) pass against it.

The order collects the brand's goods at ncom's prices and the delivery (`ncomOrderMoney`: the discount capped at the goods, other shops' lines kept aside as foreign items), and the address is matched to a district and thana with warnings for what is not. `order.updated`/`order.cancelled` need `baseRevision` equal to the held one (else 409 with the revision) and go through the desk's own edit and the state machine; what the order can no longer take is 409 with a warning on the order. Status changes are queued in `marketplace.ncom_outbox` in the change's transaction (deduplicated by the source event, ncom's own changes not echoed), signed with the order secret and sent by the worker to `<base>/api/orders/callback` (https only): 2xx delivered, 409 or another 4xx except 408/429 refused, otherwise retried after 15 s, 1 min, 5 min, 15 min, 1 h, 3 h and 6 h, failed after 8 tries. Webhooks (`/ncom/<org id>/webhook`, webhook secret) are taken once per event id and only add warnings.

- 2026-10-05 · M35b · D-556 (amended after the ledger audit) · **ncom's money is checked, not trusted to be there.** `ncomCents` reads a missing or malformed amount as nothing (`null`), never zero, and `ncomOrderMoney` refuses (400) an order whose total or shipping is missing, whose lines lack a quantity from 1 to 10,000 or a price, or whose figures do not add up (lines' totals plus shipping, less any coupon; or units less `discountTotalCents` plus shipping). ASSUMED: a non-zero `taxTotalCents` is refused (no ERP order carries tax), and only `order.created`, `order.placed` or no topic files an order. The discount is split between the brand's lines and other shops' lines by value with the money module's `allocate`, so the COD and the other shops' part add up to ncom's total exactly. The same ncom order under a new key answers the order already filed. An edit is refused (409, a warning on the order, nothing applied, under a savepoint) when a line of the brand no longer resolves, when none of its lines is left, when the orders module refuses it, or when the order would not then collect the brand's part less any advance; a refusal of the orders module on intake is a final 422.
