Every connector answer has its cost fields stripped as it is written, and the cost sweep covers every connector route. The callback does not follow redirects. Migration 0124 (0120 on its branch; its header's "0119" is today's 0123) makes an ended hold final in the table and lets the sweep drop webhook claims after 90 days.
- 2026-10-06 · M35b · D-554 (amended after the acceptance audit) · **The scanner app as it ships needs only its server address changed.** Its APK calls the old site's `POST /api/admin/scanner/login` and `GET /api/admin/scanner/shipment` and shows a refusal's `error`; the ERP serves both paths with the same work as `/v1/scanner/sign-in` and `/v1/scanner/shipment`, answers their refusals as `{ error, code }`, and the brand's nginx forwards `/api/admin/scanner/` to the API (CSRF-exempt, open CORS without credentials, the sign-in's rate limit). The app never asks for an authenticator code, so an account that needs one is told to pair the phone from Orders → Scan and paste the code (the app's paste-code sign-in). Its "sign in on the web" button opens `/scanner-connect`, which the ERP does not serve; the paste-code path replaces it. A rebuilt app should move to the `/v1/scanner/*` paths.

The link is never re-pointed to the target category or trimmed of the product by a delete: it is already shared in ads and posts, and a link that silently opened another range would mislead. - 2026-10-06 · integration · D-423, D-559 and D-639 (noted at the M35b merge) · ASSUMED · **An archived variant can meet a live ncom hold.** The single-variant archive (D-423, `PATCH /v1/catalog/skus/:id`) checks no held stock (M39b's bulk archive does), and a reserve reads `sellable` before it locks the stock row, so an archive can land on a variant ncom holds; holds are never released on a timer (D-559). ncom's handover then refuses an order whose only line it was (the hold stays until a person releases it in Website → Marketplace) or files the line as another shop's with a warning. No stock or money goes astray: the reservation stays counted until it is released. Left as it is at the merge because the fix touches every reserve caller (the website's checkout hold, order confirm, ncom) and M39b's live archive path. ncom also lists the shelf selling price, not a running flash sale's price, and the Marketplace screen's default weight, not M39b's per-variant weight (D-553 as built; no cost reaches ncom) · Found by the ledger re-audit of the M39b merge · Fix: route the single archive through `SkuStatusService`'s held-stock check, lock the SKUs `for share` before `inventory.reserve` in every caller (a `CatalogApi` method), name ncom and checkout holds in `CATALOG_SKU_HELD`, and test `/reserve` then archive → 409; owner question: should ncom see flash prices and per-variant weights?

- 2026-10-06 · M38a · D-650 · ASSUMED · **A 45 × 35 mm roll and a custom sticker size, laid out by one rule.** `POST /v1/catalog/labels` takes `medium: thermal-45x35` beside 38 × 25, 40 × 30 and 50 × 25, and `medium: custom` with `widthMm` 25 to 110 and `heightMm` 15 to 100 (one decimal at most; `STICKER_SIZE_LIMITS` and its Zod schemas in `@elysium/contracts`, which the server parses with and the console checks with). Every size, the fixed rolls and sheets included, is laid out by `stickerLayout(widthMm, heightMm, content)` in `@elysium/domain/barcode`, which the PDF and the dialog's preview both draw from. The EAN-13 module is a whole number of 203 dpi dots, the largest of 0.5, 0.375 and 0.25 mm that fits with 0.5 mm clear at each edge (a sheet keeps its laser module, 0.375 or 0.33 mm), unless a smaller one keeps more lines; 0.25 mm (76 % of nominal, the nearest whole-dot size to the symbology's 80 % floor, what the 38 × 25 roll has printed since D-604) is the floor, so a sticker under 29.25 mm wide is refused (`CATALOG_LABELS_INVALID`, and in the dialog) with the least size that would do. Bars prefer 7 mm at 25 mm tall plus 0.6 mm per mm taller, at most 0.6 of the symbol's width, never under 5 mm; they shorten before any line goes. The brand and price row and the barcode with its digits are never given up; then, least important first: the name's second line (a name that fits one line by the cutting estimate never takes two), the extra line, the SKU code, size and colour, the product name. The fixed rolls print as before (38 × 25 at 0.25 mm and 7 mm bars, 50 × 25 at 0.375 mm); 45 × 35 gets 0.375 mm, two name lines and room for the extra line; 32.5 × 20 drops the SKU code · The owner asked for 45 × 35 and "a custom field for flexibility"; thermal printers take labels up to about 4 inches · A 300 dpi printer, other limits or another floor: change `ROLL_MODULES_MM`, `MIN_STICKER_MODULE_MM` or `STICKER_SIZE_LIMITS`.

It is the first line given up when a sticker is short of room (D-650). - 2026-10-07 · trust-votes · D-654 · **Trust votes: the admins vouch for one of their own.** `iam.trust_votes (org_id, voter_user_id, candidate_user_id, created_at)`, a tenant table beside the memberships (RLS and FORCE, `tenantTableSql()`'s policy; SELECT, INSERT and DELETE for `erp_app`, never UPDATE: a vote is cast or withdrawn, never edited), unique per (org, voter, candidate), voter ≠ candidate, and a trigger refusing a vote in anyone's name but the acting user's (migrations 0125 and 0126). `POST /v1/users/:id/trust` votes and `DELETE /v1/users/:id/trust` withdraws (`update User`: org admins and super admins); each audited in its own transaction (`iam.trust_voted`, `iam.trust_withdrawn`). A candidate is an active org admin here who is not a super admin or owner (`TRUST_NOT_CANDIDATE` otherwise). `GET /v1/users` rows carry `trust: { votes, needed, trusted, votedByMe, canVote, reason, reasonCode }` for such admins, null for everyone else. Trust is counted live by `trustGraph` in `@elysium/domain/iam`, never stored · The owner: "make the staff page an option where admin can give trust votes. If someone gets trust from 2/3 majority he can be trusted for all." In Elysium the owner invited Riya and Riya invited the rest, so nobody but the owner could ever approve Riya's requests (D-077, D-410) · Widening candidates to accountants and other checkers is one line in `isTrustCandidate`; not asked for.
- 2026-10-07 · trust-votes · D-655 · **A trusted checker may decide a request from their own grant lineage; nothing else changes.** `ApprovalsService.decide` still walks the lineage (`iam.memberships.granted_by` and every `iam.membership_granters` row); only on a hit does it ask `IamApi.checkerTrust`, inside the decision's transaction, under the candidate's trust advisory lock (shared; the vote routes take it exclusively, because the votes table has no UPDATE grant to row-lock with) and with the active admin memberships held `for share`, so a withdrawn vote or a deactivated voter cannot cross the decision. Trusted: the refusal is set aside. Never for a `credential_reset` approval: whoever holds a person's password link stays out of deciding resets, trusted or not (D-404 to D-406 unchanged, as the coordinator asked). Maker ≠ checker is untouched (`canDecide` and the table's CHECK): a trusted person still never approves their own request. Super admins were already exempt from the lineage and stay so · Owner's request · Storing a "trusted" flag would go stale the moment a voter left; counting live costs four small reads, only when the lineage refuses.
- 2026-10-07 · trust-votes · D-656 · **Only independent votes count: no votes inside an invitation chain, no sibling votes, a quorum of two.** A voter counts for a candidate only when (1) neither invited the other, directly or through others, over every granter ever recorded, active or revoked; (2) they share no inviter, directly or through others, super admins and owners left out of that one test (the owner invites everyone, so otherwise nobody would be independent; an owner is still held to (1) and never vouches for anyone they invited, however far down); (3) the voter is an active org admin here or an active super admin, with two-step sign-in enrolled (an owner counts as enrolled: their link is minted behind the mother's second factor, D-473). Trusted iff votes from such voters ≥ ceil(2/3 × how many such voters there are) and ≥ 2, so one person alone never makes anyone trusted. Computed live: a voter who is deactivated, loses the role, turns two-step sign-in off or becomes related stops counting at once. A vote that would not count is refused (403 `TRUST_VOTE_REFUSED`, "You invited this person", "This person invited you", "You were both invited by the same person", "Turn on two-step sign-in to vote"; a vote for yourself is `USER_SELF_MANAGEMENT`) rather than stored · The owner's added rule, with the coordinator's fraud-prevention design: two accounts Riya invited cannot vouch for each other or for Faisal · With the owner above everyone, a brand where one admin invited all the others has no independent voters until a second super admin, or an admin invited by a super admin, joins.
- 2026-10-07 · superadmin · D-657 · **A super admin's vote counts for anyone, and one is enough.** The owner's decision: a super admin holds supreme authority. `trustGraph().standing` lets a super admin vouch for any admin, whatever the lineage, and `tally` marks a candidate trusted on one super admin's vote. The independence rules (D-656) still bind admins vouching for one another; nobody vouches for themselves; two-step sign-in is still required to vote.

- 2026-10-07 · superadmin · D-659 · **Super admins make super admins: the second at once, from the third on by every existing super admin's vote.** `POST /v1/users/:id/super-admin` nominates or votes yes, `DELETE` withdraws; `iam.super_admin_votes` (migrations 0127, 0128) holds the votes; a nomination succeeds when every active super admin has voted yes, so with one super admin the second is immediate. Super admins also get "Invite a super admin" on the Staff screen.

- 2026-10-07 · parent-sku · D-661 · **A product's parent SKU names it, and its web address is the parent SKU in lower case.** The parent SKU is a product's SKU code without the variant segments: org code, category code (left out for a product with no category, as its codes do) and the zero-padded sequence, `ELY-HAWA-0020`, whose variants are `ELY-HAWA-0020-M`, `…-L` (`parentSkuCode` in `@elysium/domain`). Every create path through `CatalogService.createProductIn` (the form, the new-products spreadsheet) and every duplicate gives the new product the slug `parentSkuSlug(parentSku)` = `ely-hawa-0020`, **whatever the request sends**: `slug` on `POST /v1/catalog/products` and on the duplicate routes is now optional and ignored (kept so older callers are not refused). Unique by construction, since the sequence is unique per brand; `freeProductSlug` stays as the guard (an address another product was given by hand takes the product's number after it, `ely-hawa-0020-0020`). Names may repeat (D-653 holds). Product responses and list rows carry `parentSku` (`productParentSku`: read back from the product's own SKU codes, so a product that moved category keeps the prefix its labels carry, D-623; for a product with no SKU yet, the org code and its category now; null for a product whose codes were imported as they were by M14a's opening-data importer, which keeps its own codes and name-based slugs). The product page's header and a list column show it; the new-product form no longer derives an address from the name and says "The web address will be the parent SKU". The slug can still be changed on the product (the Website card; old addresses redirect, M33). Existing products keep their slugs: no backfill (Elysium's catalog was just emptied; other brands have none that matter yet), no migration · The owner: "ELY-HAWA-0020 … make this the parent SKU … it will be the slug of the product in lower case, so products can have the same name" · A backfill is one UPDATE per brand if ever wanted; the previous-slug redirect would keep old links working.
- 2026-10-07 · parent-sku · D-662 · **Duplicate makes 1 to 50 copies, numbered one after another.** `POST /v1/catalog/products/:id/duplicate` takes `{ name?, copies? }` (`copies` 1 to 50, default 1; `name` defaults to the source's). Every copy is a full new product (D-641's copy: category, description, weight, website fields off the website, size chart, photos with colours, every active variant with its price, regular price and weight, new internal barcodes; no stock, cost, orders or history; a draft), all in **one transaction**: the first copy takes the brand's SKU counter, which stays locked until commit, so the copies take consecutive sequences (0021, 0022, 0023) and no other product of the brand takes one in between; each copy's variant codes are the source's with the copy's parent SKU in front (`ELY-HAWA-0021-M`). The archived-value and category checks run once, after the first sequence, under the same shared locks D-641 took (ledger re-audit N1). The answer is the first copy as the product page reads it plus `copies: [{ id, sourceId, name, parentSku, slug }]`. The dialogs (product page, list row menu) gain a Copies field checked with the server's own limits (`DUPLICATE_LIMITS`, `duplicateCopiesSchema` in `@elysium/contracts`; Bangla digits read) and open the first copy · The owner: "when we copy or duplicate any product … 0020 will be 0021, 22, 23, serial maintained automatically, and the size variants after that automatically" · Change `DUPLICATE_LIMITS.copies`.

At most 200 new products per request (products × copies, `DUPLICATE_LIMITS.total`, refused by the schema and by the dialog): the transaction holds the brand's SKU counter, so every other product creation in the brand waits for it. List every adapter built from fixtures because official docs or credentials were missing.

- 2026-10-05 · M35b · **Meta Conversions API and GA4 Measurement Protocol for the website** (`apps/server/src/modules/storefront/tracking.gateway.ts`) — UNVERIFIED, has never spoken to Meta or Google, off unless `STOREFRONT_TRACKING_PROVIDER=live` and `STOREFRONT_TRACKING_ENABLED=true`. Built from Meta's Conversions API documentation (`POST /{version}/{dataset id}/events?access_token=…`, `data[]` with `event_name`, `event_time`, `event_id`, `action_source`, `user_data` hashed per Meta's normalisation rules, `custom_data`, `test_event_code`) and GA4's Measurement Protocol (`POST /mp/collect?measurement_id=…&api_secret=…`, `/debug/mp/collect` in test mode, `client_id`, `events[]`). Verify with a brand's dataset, a test event code and a GA4 property's DebugView before switching it on.

The callback path `<base>/api/orders/callback` is the old site's. Verify with ncom's connector test from its dashboard on a staging brand first.

- 2026-10-05 · M39a · D-621 · **What uses a row is asked of the modules that hold it, inside the delete.** `platform/references/references.ts`: an owner exports a registry built on `ReferenceRegistry` — `CatalogReferences` (categories, lists, values, products, SKUs; from `CatalogModule`), `LocationReferences` (`InventoryModule`), `DiscountReferences` (discounts and flash sales; `PromoModule`) — and every module that points at those rows registers a checker in `onModuleInit` (`*references.binding.ts`) that counts **its own** rows only, its SQL in its own `references.repository.ts` (invariant 12; the module-boundaries test scans them). The shape is `BuyerHistoryRegistry`'s: the owner is the module the others already import, so no import cycle. A checker returns `{ reason, count, examples, movable }`; reasons are a closed list (`USAGE_REASONS` in `@elysium/contracts`, `UsageReport` for `GET …/:id/usage`), examples are names the caller may read (products, menu labels, discount titles), never a buyer or a cost, and orders and till lines are counted only. `release` lets a checker remove what exists only because of the row (the old site's ids); the catalog's `moveCategory` lets the website move menu entries. **Order inside every delete:** lock (`for update`, id order), count the owner's own uses, ask every checker, refuse with `*_IN_USE` (409) and the counts in words, release, delete, audit. A reference that committed first is counted; one that arrives after the lock waits on it and then fails on its foreign key — tested with a held transaction and with a twelve-round race. SQLSTATE 23503 on the **deleting** side ("update or delete on table …", or one of 0119's `*_IN_USE:` guards) maps to the module's `*_IN_USE` (`isStillReferenced`), never a 500; the inserting side is left as it was. New codes `CATALOG_IN_USE`, `INVENTORY_IN_USE`, `SUPPLY_IN_USE`, `PROMO_IN_USE`, `NOTIFY_IN_USE`, `MARKETING_IN_USE`, `BRANCH_IN_USE`. A test reads every foreign key into the catalog from `pg_constraint` and fails on one no checker counts (the courier return line's key is in 0048's SQL only) · The catalog is a leaf module and may not read the tables that reference it; a delete that trusted the foreign keys alone would answer the owner with a constraint name, and soft references (discount scopes, campaign product lists, old ids) have no key at all · Register a checker for a new module that points at these rows; the `pg_constraint` test names the ones missing.
- 2026-10-05 · M39a · D-622 · **Every delete keeps what it deleted.** Each hard delete writes its audit row inside its transaction with `operation 'delete'` and `before` = the deleted row's content, its dependants included (a list's values, a product's SKUs and their values and image file ids, a supplier's contacts and documents, a campaign's refs, a discount's tiers, a flash sale's items); a bulk action writes one row per entity (archiving a value with its variants writes the value's row and one per SKU; emptying a category or a size chart writes, besides the deleted row, one `catalog.product.category_moved` or `catalog.product.size_chart_moved` row per product moved, so each product's own history shows it). A supplier's row keeps who it was, not how to reach it: its code, name and terms, its contacts' ids and documents' labels, never a phone, an email or an address (invariant 10). The website's existing deletes are retrofitted: a size chart keeps its grid, a menu item keeps every child its cascade removes, a hero slide keeps its row · After a hard delete nothing else holds the row, and the interceptor's net records only the request and the response · —
- 2026-10-05 · M39a · D-623 · ASSUMED · **Categories: delete, empty and delete, archive.** Delete when no product (any status), subcategory, menu entry or live discount names the category. **Empty and delete** (`POST …/:id/empty-and-delete { targetCategoryId | null }`, `delete Catalog`): the products move to the target (or to no category), the subcategories move up to the category's own parent with a slug that is free there (`-2`, `-3`), the menu entries are pointed at the target (or removed, each audited) through `CatalogReferences.moveCategory`, the old site's ids are forgotten, then the category is deleted — one transaction; the target must be active and outside the subtree. A **live discount** (not archived) naming the category refuses, naming the discount: a discount's scope is its terms and is never rewritten (D-541); an archived discount's scope keeps the dangling id, harmlessly. **Archive and Restore** are explicit (`POST …/archive`, `…/restore`, `update Catalog`), replacing Catalog setup's hidden "In use" checkbox; an archived category, or one under an archived category, refuses a new or moved product and an empty-and-delete's products (`CATALOG_CATEGORY_INVALID`, naming the archived one; the console's target picker leaves both out) and **its products leave the website** (`webWhere` and the cart's `sellable` require no archived category at or above the product) until they are moved or it is restored. A deleted category's code is free for reuse; products keep their printed codes, and a variant added to a product later takes the prefix its codes already have (`skuCodePrefix` in `@elysium/domain`), so one product never reads two ways · The owner asked to delete categories; a category the website still showed products under after it was put away would surprise a shopper · Owner question: should an archived category's products stay on the website? Drop `IN_LIVE_CATEGORY` from `catalog-web.repository.ts`.
- 2026-10-05 · M39a · D-624 · **Sizes, colours and other lists, and their values: `is_active`, delete, archive, reorder.** `catalog.attributes.is_active` and `catalog.attribute_values.is_active` (0118, default true). - 2026-10-05 · M39a · D-627 · **Dependants leave only with their parent, by cascade; triggers refuse every other delete.** 0118 makes four foreign keys cascade: `catalog.sku_price_history` and `inventory.stock_levels` (SKU and location) from their parents, `promo.discount_tiers` from its discount and `promo.flash_sale_items` from its sale. `erp_app` gains no DELETE on any of them (stock levels stay in `NO_DELETE_TABLES`), and 0119's triggers refuse, for every role, a price history row while its SKU exists, a stock level that holds or reserves a piece, or while its SKU and location both exist, a tier while its discount exists, and an item while its sale exists. Every other key stays `restrict`, so a SKU with a movement is never deleted whatever the service does. Chosen over the investigation's `SECURITY DEFINER` purge, which 0026 and 0104 avoid deliberately; the guards run as the caller and name the row's own `org_id` (a cascade runs as the table owner with row security off). DELETE grants added only where a parent is deleted directly: `supply.suppliers`, `promo.codes`, `promo.flash_sales`, `notify.templates` (with a trigger refusing a template whose key was ever sent), `marketing.campaigns` · Price history was designed as append-only evidence; it stays so for every SKU that exists · —

- 2026-10-05 · M39a · D-633 · **Branches are renamed and switched off, never deleted; a `Branch` subject for org_admin.** `PATCH /v1/pos/branches/:id { name?, isActive? }` (`update Branch`) and `GET …/:id/holds` (`read Branch`): switching off is refused (`BRANCH_IN_USE`) while a till is switched on or a shift is open there (the till's module), any wallet-subtype ledger account of the branch holds money, with or without a `wallets.wallets` row (`WalletsApi.walletsHoldingMoney`), anyone is employed there, active or suspended (`HrApi.employeesAtBranch`), or its locations hold or reserve stock (`InventoryApi.stockAtBranch`). The API document is behind a merchant-portal login; the shapes come from Steadfast's own `steadfast-it/SteadFast-Courier-Laravel-Package` README, and the two public sources disagree about the base URL (`portal.packzy.com` vs `portal.steadfast.com.bd`). Base URL, header names, status vocabulary and statement column names are all configuration. `verified = false`, so an account cannot be activated on it unless `COURIER_STEADFAST_ENABLED=true`. No acceptance check depends on it: every test runs against the fake adapter, and the live sandbox smoke test is skipped for want of credentials.
- 2026-09-30 · M15 · **Pathao courier adapter** (`apps/server/src/modules/courier/adapters/pathao.adapter.ts`) — UNVERIFIED, has never spoken to Pathao, off unless `COURIER_PATHAO_ENABLED=true`. Hosts, login, create-order fields, the webhook header and its event names come from Pathao's own `pathao-eng/courier-woocommerce-plugin`; the status-poll endpoint and the collected-amount field come from community clients only. No Pathao city, zone or area ids are sent. Whether Pathao deduplicates a retried create is unknown. Fixtures are transcribed, not recorded.
- 2026-09-30 · M15 · **RedX courier adapter** (`apps/server/src/modules/courier/adapters/redx.adapter.ts`) — UNVERIFIED, has never spoken to RedX, off unless `COURIER_REDX_ENABLED=true`. Nothing is first-party: parcel creation is transcribed from `redx-for-woocommerce`, a third-party WordPress plugin (an individual author, `shopupwp` among its contributors), not RedX's own SDK (D-200); the status endpoint is community-described and the status words are recalled, not read. Poll-only: nothing documents how RedX authenticates a webhook. No sandbox default.
- 2026-10-02 · M16 · **BulkSMSBD SMS gateway** (`apps/server/src/modules/notify/providers/bulksmsbd.gateway.ts`) — UNVERIFIED, has never sent a real message, off unless `NOTIFY_SMS_PROVIDER=bulksmsbd` and `NOTIFY_BULKSMSBD_ENABLED=true` are both set; the fake gateway is the default everywhere, production included (D-012). Built from BulkSMSBD's public developer page (bulksmsbd.com/bulksms-api-bangladesh.php), re-read 2026-10-02: `smsapi` with `api_key`, `type=text`, `number` (`88017XXXXXXXX`), `senderid` and `message`; `202` is success and `1002`–`1021` are named failures. The page does **not** document the response body's shape (so success is a body containing `202` and no documented failure code, and no provider message id is recorded), HTTPS (the page shows `http://`; this adapter defaults to `https://` on the same host, as configuration, because the API key would otherwise cross every hop in clear), or how Bangla is sent (`type=text` is sent as shown, though `1012` says masked SMS must be Bengali). Verify with a real key and a Bangla message before the pilot, and replace it if the client already uses another gateway.
